- [debug-format] Debug records appear to show void* returns as void: TBF_pReadLevel, TBF_pReadLevelTRSBloc and TBF_pReadFix are recorded as void but return the buffer in v0 (NULL on open failure), and the HdwTx_R3 caller stores the result into GLI_g_TextureMemSpace[]; all three matched only when declared void*. Hungarian 'p'/'pv' names with a recovered void return (e.g. fn_pvMallocInBinaryPreservingTheCurrentMode) should be checked against v0 use at return and in callers. (2026-10-11)

- [compiler] mtbpc follow-up (COP0 $24 sweep over ~24 functions): (1) MWCC 3.0.1 moves 'asm { mtbpc zero }' into BRANCH delay slots but never into jal delay slots (emits mtbpc; nop; jal); 3.0.3 also fills jal slots, so functions whose retail mtc0 sits after a jal need their file on compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt if that costs no matches (Engine.c/GameDisp.c/LoadLvl.c/ia_dnm.c moved; WaterFx.cpp cannot, 3.0.3 breaks WaterFx_SetPosition/Perturb). (2) mtbpc can add hazard nops retail lacks; where retail has 'mtc0; (no nop)' then 'mtc0; nop' outside delay slots (SHW_fn_vDrawShadow) keep the old '.set noreorder / mtc0 zero,$24' spelling with an explicit nop. (3) 'asm volatile { mtbpc zero }' still schedules but acts as a CSE barrier, reproducing retail reloads of globals after the write (fn_vEngine). (4) Loops entered by 'b test' with mtbpc in the slot (FireFx_vProcess/vDisplay) only matched as goto-entered loops; natural while/for give a beqz guard. (2026-10-11)

- [compiler] Float constant spelling changes scheduling: in AGO2_vReInit's cosine-table loop, 'fAngle += 6.2831855f / 512.0f;' matches retail exactly while the pre-folded literal 'fAngle += 0.012271846f;' (same bits, 0x3c490fdb) schedules the lui/ori of the constant and the table address differently (controlled pair, nothing else changed). When an FP loop differs only in where a lui/ori constant is placed, try writing the constant as the original's arithmetic expression. (2026-10-11)

- [source-layout] Recovered layouts can contain an enum member that occupies one byte even though the baseline uses -enum int: C_PS2MC_Manager has 'PS2MC_tdeMessageID m_AutoSaveErrorMessage /* +0xa514 */' followed by 'm_pLoadSaveBuffer /* +0xa515 */'. Declared as the enum, every later field shifts by 4 (store to m_bFirstBootCheckHasBeenDone hit +0xa8fc instead of +0xa8f8). Storing it as unsigned char fixed __ct__15C_PS2MC_ManagerFv and PS2MC_fn_vLoadScreenShot; the class then needs explicit tail padding because 'new C_PS2MC_Manager' allocates 0xa900. Check member offsets after enum fields when a store is off by 4. (2026-10-11)

- [compiler] A 'b epilogue+4' with the shared return load (e.g. lw v0,local) in its delay slot is MWCC filling the delay slot from the join block, not an early return. Writing an explicit early 'return x;' in that branch inserts an extra nop before the epilogue (152 vs 148 bytes); falling through to the single final return matches. HIE_fn_p_stGetGeometricObjectFromSuperObject. (2026-10-11)

- [compiler] Unfolded element-offset family, single-access case (retail: index sll first, then addiu base,off, addu, access at 0): build the member address in a char* and index it with a dead scaled-index assignment inside the subscript: 'pc = (char*)list; pc = pc + OFF; ((T*)pc)[iIndex = (int)n * K]' (int iIndex otherwise unused). The dead assignment makes 3.0.1 schedule the sll before the base add; without it the order is base-first, and the char* temp plus separate '+ OFF' statement keeps the offset unfolded. Same idiom as the permuter-found ISI_fn_dGetVertexRLILODLightMapU. Matched GEO cone/sphere setters (4), COL_hGetGameMaterialOfIndexedSphere/AlignedBox, ISI_fn_dGetVertexRLILOD, PS2MC_fn_iGetFunctionResult. A scan finds ~139 single-access unmatched functions with this shape. Not original source; marked for cleanup. No compiler build (21 tried) or pragma reproduces the order. (2026-10-11) (2026-10-11)

- [compiler] Dead-index idiom, extensions: it also fixes base-first cases (Toby_SetTransparency: pc = (char*)&C->field; *(float*)(pc + (iIndex = id * sizeof(T))) = v), byte-granular scaling with iIndex = id * sizeof(T) when the index is not a multiple of the access type (Tobies, VIDSPE_fn_vPlayVideoNow), and multi-access code where retail reloads the array pointer per field needs each access to redo 'pc = base; pc = pc + k;' with its own [iIndex = i * K] (fn_bGetVectorFromPhonem). Still unsolved: retail's explicit 'addiu x,x,0' after an already-offset base (IPT pad buttons, usnd crit sections) and CollSet setters where retail fills the beqz delay slot with the index sll. (2026-10-11) (2026-10-11)

- [compiler] Guarded unfolded stores where retail puts the index sll in the beqz delay slot into a fresh register (CollSet setters: bltz; slti; beqz; sll a1; addiu v1,a0,0x64; addu; sw 0): compute the scaled index inside the second && operand, 'int o; if (i >= 0 && (o = i * 4, i < 2)) *(float*)((char*)&p->arr[0] + o) = v;' with the body on the next line (line map). The index computed in the body or before the if gives a nop slot or hoists the sll. Matched fn_vCollSetSetColliderReal/Actor. Getters with a no-code gap line (COL box/sphere 610/612) get retail's dsll32-before-lw schedule from a dead 'if (L && L) { }' on the list pointer, leaving only an addu operand swap. Retail's 'addiu x,x,0' after an already-offset base (IPT pads, INO FFB, usnd) is still unreproduced. (2026-10-11) (2026-10-11)

- [compiler] Self-recursive tree walkers (IPO_fn_vReInitIPO, IPO_fn_vLevelReInitRLI, fn_vReinitTheStaticHierarchie): retail inlines the recursive call one or two levels deep (extra nested child loops with the same body, the innermost still a jal to itself), and callers that walk from the world root inline the whole walker on one source line. Wrapping the plain recursive definition in '#pragma auto_inline on' / '#pragma auto_inline reset' reproduces it exactly; without the pragma nothing is inlined. The same pragma around IPO_fn_vInternalComputeRLI makes IPO_fn_vComputeRadiosity match. (2026-10-11) (2026-10-11)

- [source-layout] Indirect call through a looked-up function pointer (fn_ucGetDsgVar): retail computes the call's data argument before the lookup call. A single expression getfn(type)(buf+off, ...) sinks the address computation after the lookup (136 vs 124 bytes, 2 extra saved regs), as do register locals or computing pData alone. Assigning pData first and then the function pointer to a local, then calling it, matches (as does a comma expression or an inline wrapper taking the fp as its first parameter). (2026-10-18)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 C++ (.cxx): a 12-byte vector local declared with an initializer at its point of use (MTH3D_tdstVector_ v = p->uParam.stVectorValue;) is kept in stack memory and copied with lwc1x3 then swc1x3 through an addiu sp temp, matching retail fn_p_stDotProduct/fn_p_stVectorOperations; a top-of-function declaration plus assignment is scalarized into callee-saved FPRs instead. Debug info marks such locals with size-0 symbols named after the variable (stV1, stPersoPosition, stActorsPosition) in the extracted scaffold. (2026-10-19)

- [compiler] padloop differs between compiler builds: MWCC 3.0.3 (020716) pads short FP loops (c.cond/bc1 with -pragma padloop on) one nop less than 3.0.1 (020123). fn_xRealModuloAround (AITools.c, a 3.0.3 file) has retail 3/4 nops; identical C gives 3/4 under 3.0.1 (MATCH) but 2/3 under 3.0.3; padloop off gives 1/1. A 3.0.3 file whose only residual is one missing nop before a short loop's back-branch is a compiler-build residual, not a source-shape problem. Same file needs 3.0.3 elsewhere (inline float->unsigned conversion where 3.0.1 calls fptoui), so retail was likely an intermediate build. (2026-10-19)

- [source-layout] Extractor stub local lists can be in reverse declaration order: in mcmngr.c SVG_fn_s32CreateIconFilesForSave and SVG_fn_vCheckStatusChangeInMC0 only matched after declaring the stub's locals in the opposite order. Try reversing the whole local list early when register allocation is off. (2026-10-19)

- [compiler] Casting an unsigned parameter to (int) at a call site changes argument evaluation order: in mcmngr.c sceMcWrite/sceMcRead calls, passing (int)_u32Size loaded a2 before a0 as retail does (SVG_fn_s32WriteToFile, SVG_fn_s32ReadFromFile). The converse also holds: an int local passed as (unsigned)x (SVG_fn_s32ReadNextFileInfo). (2026-10-19)

- [source-layout] Stack vectors the target copies with integer lw/sw (not lwc1/swc1): declare them as 'typedef union { MTH3D_tdstVector_ v; int i[3]; } UV3' and copy per component, stV.i[k] = ((int*)src)[k]. Float-field/struct copies give lwc1/swc1, and ((int*)&x)[k] or *(int*)&x.xX hoist addiu address temps (CAM_Act.c fn_ucCamCine* family). (2026-10-19)

- [source-layout] Integer clamp shape: retail 'slti x,hi+1; bnez; move t,x / li t,hi' then 'slt at,t,zero; beqz' then a second 'slti x,hi+1' comes from M_Max(lo, M_Min(x, hi)) with M_Min(a,b) = ((a) > (b) ? (b) : (a)) and M_Max(a,b) = ((a) > (b) ? (a) : (b)). A '<'-based Min gives slti hi; a '(Min < lo) ? lo : Min' form gives bgez instead of slt-with-zero (GamSave.c fn_vValidateAllOption). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] VU0 lqc2/sqc2 matrix copies: putting the asm block directly in the function body (one statement per line) reproduces the retail line map (8 instructions, 7 line changes: the sqc2 in the branch delay slot inherits the branch line), while the inline-helper form (PS2_M_vCopyMatrix) matches the bytes but collapses all copied instructions onto the call line. A float[4][4] member is not a valid asm address operand; a 'float (*pDst)[4][4]' local holding &m_Matrix gives the retail 'addiu a2,a0,0x30'. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The inline (unsigned int)float conversion in retail uses trunc.w.s (the R5900 truncate), not cvt.w.s: 'lui 0x4f00; mtc1; c.le.s 2^31,x; bc1t big; trunc.w.s; mfc1; b done; big: sub.s x,x,2^31; trunc.w.s; mfc1; or r,r,0x80000000'. MWCC PS2 3.0.3-020716 emits that sequence for (unsigned int)float; 3.0.1/3.0 emit a jal fptoui instead (100/144 bytes for GetTotalSizeWithMipMaps). u_VertexBuffers.c lives under the game source root but is PS2GL library code, and it needs the 3.0.3 build for exactly this reason, so it was added to config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] When a candidate is EQUIVALENT with a single register rename, check the MWCC debug local list before adding a local: retail's GetTotalSizeWithMipMaps records zero locals, so the original reassigned its parameter ('OriginalSize = (unsigned int)(1.34f * (float)OriginalSize);'), which keeps the converted value in the incoming register a0. With a fresh local MWCC reuses v1 for the conversion result and for the +15 (EQUIVALENT, not MATCH); reassigning the parameter reproduces retail's 'mfc1 a0,f1; addiu v1,a0,0xf' exactly. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 o32 ABI passes the 5th and 6th integer/pointer arguments in $t0 and $t1 (not on the stack); a call with 4 args emits no t0/t1, with 5 args emits 'daddu $t0,zero,zero' and with 6 args also 'daddu $t1,zero,zero'. Verified with a controlled probe (5/6-arg prototypes) and used to match CINE_SendRequestsBeginSounds/EndSounds in CineDef.c (10/10 relocations, exact line map). Note the callee's own prologue 'daddu $fp,$t1,$zero' shows $t1 doubles as the o32 static chain, so only a 6-parameter declaration reproduces both t0 and t1. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 keeps a single function-scope union/struct local in memory and gives EVERY assignment site its own stack slot: for four sequential if-blocks that store to one '_SND_tduRefEvt uRefEvt' local the slots are sp+0x30, sp+0x34, sp+0x38, sp+0x3c but the code order maps block1->0x30, block2->0x3c, block3->0x38, block4->0x34 (first block lowest, the rest in reverse order). The store is then reloaded into the argument register ('sw v1,0x30(sp)' + 'lw a3,0x30(sp)'). Reproduced exactly in CINE_SendRequestsBeginSounds/EndSounds with one local declared once at function scope. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 unrolls a 10-trip store loop into an 8-store main loop plus a 1-store remainder loop; the main loop's guard is 'slti v1,i,2' (a group count, not an element count) and the remainder is entered via 'slti at,i,0xA'. Writing it as 'i=0; do { p=&base; p->a[i]=0; p->a[i+1]=0; ... p->a[i+7]=0; i+=8; } while (i<2); if (i<10) { do { p=&base; p->a[i]=0; i++; } while (i<10); }' reproduces the retail exactly (DefineMenu2D.c fn_vReinitGrayedEntries, 256/256). The base pointer must be assigned inside the loop body: with the array subscript written inline ('aMenu2DPages[PageNumber].abGrayed[i]') MWCC rematerializes the whole address expression in the remainder loop (32 extra bytes) and keeps the index parameter live, which also shifts the prologue's register allocation. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 branch sense for a two-arm if/else is controlled by the source polarity, not by the logic: 'if (x != 0) {A} else {B}' compiles to 'beqz x, B_block' with A as the fall-through, while 'if (x == 0) {B} else {A}' compiles to 'bnez x, A_block' with B as the fall-through. DefineMenu2D.c fn_ulR3GetEnvScoreWithRevisit and fn_vR3SetEnvScoreWithRevisit only matched after inverting the test this way; the emitted blocks (and the delay-slot store) swap accordingly. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] A redundant nested test reproduces a retail branch that reuses an earlier comparison: an 'if (x < 10)' nested inside an outer 'if (x < 10)' makes MWCC emit a 'beqz at' in the inner block that reuses the outer comparison's result (DefineMenu2D.c fn_vR3SetEnvScoreWithRevisit, 'beqz at' at +0x54 skips the store). A redundant nested 'if (x == 0)' test of the same kind also reproduces the retail's 'beqz at' plus the stale-register reuse. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 register allocation follows the LOCAL DECLARATION ORDER, not the definition order: in fn_vDecompressTransform declaring 'short* p;' first (before the two pointer locals) makes p get $s0 and the matrix pointer $s1; declaring the matrix pointer before the vector pointer was the difference between an exact 420/420 MATCH and a two-register-swap mismatch. A single declaration line with several locals keeps the order. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 keeps a float local in a callee-saved FPR only when it is declared in a nested block; four float locals in a block scope live in f20/f21/f22 (plus f0 for the last value) and the callee-saved assignment depends on the DECLARATION order (fX,fY,fZ,fW -> the first-defined value lands in f22, then f21, f20). fn_vDecompressTransform's four-column decompression matched 420/420 with one block per column, the four float locals declared 'float fX, fY, fZ, fW;' and the calls issued in reverse component order (w, z, y, x). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0.1-020123 and 3.0.3-020716) folds a vector member's constant offset into the array base (addiu base,base,4 then +index*stride), while retail computes the row address (base+index*stride) and leaves the component offset in the load displacement (lwc1 fX,4(row)). Spelling the access as ((float*)&array[index])[component] reproduces retail exactly; array[index].member and array[index][component] both fold the offset into the base instead. Reproduced on the 12-float-stride vector array in fn_v_InterpolBetweenKeys (a3x_mem.c), where all three interpolated components matched only with the ((float*)&...)[i] form. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Copying a 3-float vector with three separate field assignments makes MWCC PS2 reload the array base and the index after every store (it cannot prove the store does not alias the global), producing a longer sequence than retail. Binding one source pointer first (a statement macro or inline helper: MTH3D_tdstVector_* p = &array[index]; dst.xX = p->xX; dst.xY = p->xY; dst.xZ = p->xZ;) computes the row address once and yields retail's interleaved lwc1/swc1 pattern, with the last store in the branch delay slot. A plain struct assignment instead emits a batched lwc1 x3 / swc1 x3 copy. Matched fn_v_InterpolBetweenKeys (a3x_mem.c) 1480/1480 with the line map. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 folds a constant member offset into the store displacement for a[i].f, so retail's '(a+fieldoff) + i*size' addressing needs the pointer-to-field idiom. SND_R3 SwitchMgr::fs_vAdd is a clean case: retail computes (m_pElt+4) + m_uiDefault*16 and stores the data pointer at offset 0 of that address, with the base in v1 and the scaled index in a1. Matching it needs three things together: a char* temp holding &m_pElt[0].m_pData, a 16-byte element type whose first member is the pointer, and a dead 'iIndex = m_uiDefault' assignment inside the subscript. Without the dead assignment MWCC allocates the base to a1 and the index to v1 (the opposite of retail); without the char* temp it folds the +4 into the store displacement (sw v0,4(base)); the plain 'm_pElt[i].m_pData' spelling yields 324 bytes instead of 328. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 materialises the address of a punned local when that address is used more than once: 'lVertice = *(int*)&fFreq & 0x1FF' over three phases becomes 'addiu a2,sp,0xC' + 'lw v1,0(a2)' instead of the retail's folded 'swc1 f3,0xC(sp)' + 'lw v1,0xC(sp)'. MWCC 3.0.3-020716 folds the punned stack address (268 vs 272 bytes for GLI_vSinusEffetOn3DVertex), so this punn family is a per-file 3.0.3 candidate (the punned GLOBAL address is materialised by every build). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail code sometimes stores raw float bit patterns into a float global with integer stores (sw of 0xBF19999A into a colour field) and reads the same global back as a float, i.e. the source used two views of one object. Reproducing the folded retail addressing needs a union with an int view and a float view of the global (union {struct{int xX,xY,xZ;} l; MTH3D_tdstVector_ v;}); a punned access through a cast materialises the address instead (see the punn insight). Verified on WatrPlan.c stSinEffectAmp/GLI_gsfWaterPlaneFogColor/stWaterPlaneNormale and the g_xSinusSizeMul* scalars. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 evaluates float call arguments right-to-left, so 'powf(2.0f,20.0f)' loads the second constant first (lui 0x41A0 before lui 0x4000). Assigning the arguments to named float locals first ('xPowBase = 2.0f; xPowExp = 20.0f; powf(xPowBase,xPowExp)') restores the retail's left-to-right constant order and matched GLI_vInitWaterplaneModule 456/456. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The literal 0.012271846f and the expression 6.2831855f/512.0f are NOT the same float: the literal is 0x3C490FDA while the division yields the retail bits 0x3C490FDB. Use the division form to reproduce retail's sin-table phase constant (GLI_vInitWaterplaneModule's cosf loop, which also needs 'float powf(float,float); float cosf(float);' prototypes or the compiler promotes the arguments to double and calls fptodp). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail comparison against a loop-independent constant can be materialized as a register copy of the loop counter's initial value: IPT_fn_ucGetFirstValidJoystick compares a_stDevice[i].ucValideAndActiveDevice with the constant 2 (the 'active' state), and MWCC copies the counter (which still holds 2) into a1 in the first branch's delay slot instead of reloading the constant inside the loop. Comparing against the loop counter instead produces the same size but a different register allocation. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0/3.0.1 emit a trampoline block for 'default: return;' inside a switch: the switch's default path becomes 'b <block>' where <block> is a new 'b <epilogue>' pair (impPS2glFogiv was 324 bytes vs the retail 312). Writing the same skip as 'goto <label at the end of the function>' produces the retail's single branch to the epilogue and matches byte-for-byte. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 narrows a bitfield store to the smallest unit that contains the field: a 15-bit field at bit 0 of a 64-bit unit compiles to a halfword read-modify-write (lhu / and 0x8000 / or / sh) and a 1-bit field at bit 15 to a byte RMW (lbu / and 0x7F / or / sb). The field width supplies the mask, so the source needs no explicit '&' (pTag->NLOOP = nLoop; matched gifSetTag 820/820). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 lowers a switch whose cases fall through into a jump table indexed by the RAW switch value (index = value, not value-min): 16 fall-through cases 'case 15: ...; case 14: ...; ... case 0: ...' produce a 16-entry table, 'sltiu $at, value, 16' range check and 16 unrolled blocks that fall through in source order (gifSetTag, 820/820). The fall-through also reproduces the retail's 'unrolled loop' shape when the case bodies share a decrementing pointer. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3) fully deletes code after a 'return' or 'goto', so a retail binary that keeps an unreachable copy of a statement (e.g. impPS2glFogfv keeps a second, never-reached copy of the fog-parameter switch) cannot be reproduced by any available compiler build; only the retail build's partial dead-code elimination keeps it. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.3-020716 inlines the float-to-unsigned conversion (c.le.s 2^31 / trunc.w.s / or 0x80000000) exactly like retail, while 3.0.1 and 3.0 call fptoui. impPS2glFogfv needs the inline form; the file's baseline compiler (MWCC 3.0.1, config/library-3.0.1-sources.txt) cannot produce it, and hand-writing the conversion in C yields the right arithmetic but the opposite branch polarity (c.lt.s instead of c.le.s). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MIPS delay-slot rule used for decompiling: a jal's delay-slot instruction executes BEFORE the call, so a 'mov.s $f22,$f0' in the delay slot of the SECOND call saves the FIRST call's result. Reading fn_vDecompressTransform's four fn_fUncompressNumber calls with that rule gives the retail mapping (1st call -> f22, 2nd -> f21, 3rd -> f20, 4th -> f0) and the store order xX<-f0, xY<-f20, xZ<-f21, xW<-f22, i.e. compressed short p[0..3] map to xX,xY,xZ,xW. Getting this backwards made the four values look like a compiler bug. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Rayman 3 retail: HIE_tdstSuperObject_.hLinkedObject (union at +0x4) holds the linked object, and for characters it is the tdstEngineObject_* (p_stCharacter). CineDef.c reaches the actor through '((tdstEngineObject_*)p_stSuperObjCine->hLinkedObject.p_stCharacter)' and then uses ->h_3dData, ->h_StandardGame, ->h_Dynam and ->h_Brain->p_stMind (AI_tdstMind_: p_stIntelligence +0x4, p_stReflex +0x8, bDoingIntel +0x10). Writing the direct actor pointer instead changes the generated loads (retail uses the super object as the base) and loses the match. (2026-10-20)

- [relocation] Retail CineDef.c sound-type globals resolve through gp: g_lSoundObjectTypeAnim = 0x36A5CC (gp-0x7C24), g_lSoundObjectTypeVoice = 0x36A5D0 (gp-0x7C20), g_lSoundObjectTypeMenu = 0x36A5D4 (gp-0x7C18), g_lSoundObjectTypeMusic = 0x36A5D8 (gp-0x7C14), g_lSoundObjectTypeAmbiance = 0x36A5DC (gp-0x7C10) with _gp = 0x3721F0. The 'sound' request block of CINE_SendRequestsBegin/EndSounds uses g_lSoundObjectTypeAnim (not a 'sound' type), and the block order in the source is Sound, Music, Voice, Ambiance. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] R5900 sqrt.s encoding via MWCC inline asm: 'asm { sqrt.s dst, src }' emits COP1 | rs=01000 | src in bits 20-16 | 0 in bits 15-11 | dst in bits 10-6 | funct=4, i.e. the SOURCE register is in bits 20-16 and the DESTINATION in bits 10-6 (not the usual fs/fd split). The retail's 'c1 0x44' (src f0, dst f1), 'c1 0x84' (src f0, dst f2), 'c1 0xC4' (src f0, dst f3) and 'c1 0x10044' (src f1, dst f1) are all this one instruction, so the printed 0xNN only encodes the register numbers. Verified with a probe (asm { madd.s s, z, z } asm { sqrt.s n, s }) and by matching GEO_fn_vComputeBoundingSphereOfTwoSpheres (src xSqrNorm in f0, dst xNorm in f1) and GEO_fn_vComputeBoundingSphereOfOneSphereAndOnePoint (src f0, dst f3). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail copies an MTH float vector with WORD copies (lw/sw) that keep the local destination offset folded (GEO_vTurnLookAt: 'lw v1,0x20(s0); sw v1,0xa0(sp)'). MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3 cannot reproduce that for a local destination: a plain float copy gives lwc1/swc1, and the int-copy idiom (*(int*)&local.xX = *(int*)&src.xX) materializes the local address with addiu before the store. The same build difference also shows up for a twice-used vector component: the retail folds sp+0x94 while MWCC materializes it into a register. This is a compiler-build residual, not a source-shape problem (blocks GEO_vTurnLookAt in GeoObjEd.c). Also: MWCC symbolic FP inline-asm operands accept only simple scalar lvalues - pointer dereferences (*p), array elements (p[0]) and struct members (v.x) are all rejected ('illegal operand' / 'illegal addressing mode'). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 virtual-base layout (verified with a probe on 'class CFlow { virtual bool f(void); }; class CSrcFlow : public virtual CFlow {}; class Der : public CSrcFlow { unsigned int m_a[6]; };'): the derived object holds the virtual-base-offset pointer at +0, the vtable pointer at +4, its own members from +8, and the virtual base last (aligned); sizeof(CFlow) is the virtual base's size (4 for a vptr-only base, 8 for vptr+int, 12 for vptr+int[2]). Overriding one of the virtual base's virtual functions in the derived class adds 4 bytes to the object (Der becomes 0x2c instead of 0x28), because MWCC keeps an extra override slot for the virtual base. A constructor of such a class takes MWCC's hidden virtual-base argument (a 64-bit value tested with dsll32/dsra32); the most derived class passes 1, an intermediate one 0, and the callee skips its own setup when the argument is 0. (2026-10-20)

- [relocation] SND_R3 retail mangling details recovered from config/symbols.txt: the string class member is usnd::sstring::_Init (mangled '_Init__Q24usnd7sstringFPCcUi' - the leading underscore is part of the method name, not a linker prefix), its nil pointer is the static member usnd::sstring::ms_czNil ('ms_czNil__Q24usnd7sstring', a NULL char*), and the RAM-location enum is the *global* _ERamLocation (mangled '13_ERamLocation', not namespaced). The container constructor is usnd::stree::stree(const CMP& ro_Compare, bool b_Multi, const ALLOC& ro_Alloc) (mangled F + RC<cmp> + b + RC<alloc>): the bool is the _Multi flag stored at +0x4 of the tree, and its default arguments explain the retail's 1-byte zero bss copy for sallocator (a POD) versus the uninitialised temporary for less<T> (which derives from binary_function). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3: an ||-chain of enum equality tests compiles to li/beq chains, but casting the whole condition to unsigned char ('if ((unsigned char)(a == X || a == Y || ...))') makes MWCC emit the equality-as-value form (xori rd,rs,imm; sltiu rd,rd,1) with short-circuit branches, which is what retail MAT_fn_bAreRotationEquivalent uses for its five-value transform-type test. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail delay-slot fill for an if/else pointer select: putting the 'else' assignment inside the else block ('if (cond) { h = &tmp; } else { h = param; }') makes MWCC sink that assignment into the beq delay slot, which is where retail MAT_fn_bAreRotationEquivalent keeps it (hSource1 = param0 in the beqz slot, overwritten by the scaled path). Keeping the assignment before the if instead makes MWCC hoist it to the function prologue. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 folds a constant stack address into the load/store offset, so a retail 'copy a 16-byte vector column through a base pointer' (addiu reg,sp,off; lwc1 fd,0x0(reg)) cannot be reproduced by a macro that copies through a local pointer to a stack local: the candidate folds it to lwc1 0x30(sp) and loses the base-pointer materialization (4-byte size difference). Same for retail code that computes a local address twice (once for a call argument, once for a later access) - MWCC CSEs the two. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MTH cross-product helper: the retail MAT rotation code loads the four cross operands in the order (rightB, leftA, rightD, leftC) before 'asm { mula.s a,b } asm { msub.s result,c,d }', i.e. the macro body must assign _mthRightB, _mthLeftA, _mthRightD, _mthLeftC in that order (as in MEC/DNMCame.c's MTH_CROSS_COMPONENT_TO) to reproduce the retail load order and register allocation. (2026-10-20)

- [abi] MWCC PS2 o32 passes a float parameter in $fa0 (not $f12) when it is not among the first four argument slots: the retail COL_fn_vComputeBoundingBoxOfSphereAndBox takes (minBox, maxBox, sphereCenter, minPoint, maxPoint, radius) with the float last, which puts the radius in $fa0 and the fifth integer argument in $t0. With the float fourth (as the debug record lists it) MWCC uses $f12 and the bytes differ. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 unrolls a counted loop by 8 (main loop + scalar remainder, the remainder entered by jumping to its test) only when the trip count is a plain local/parameter; the same loop bounded by a struct field (e.g. 'for (uc = 0; uc < mp->ucNbTarget; uc++)') stays rolled and re-reads the bound from memory each iteration. Retail MOR_fn_xPrepareMorph uses both shapes: the '_axEndIndexInObjectTable[i]--' loop is unrolled by 8 against a local int count, while the morph-struct copy loop is rolled against mp->ucNbTarget. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 keeps a small parameter in a callee-saved register by default; retail code that stores the incoming parameter to its home slot in the prologue ('sh a1,0xFE(sp)') and reloads it at each use (rematerialization) can be reproduced by declaring the parameter 'volatile' (e.g. 'volatile short xStartIndexInObjectTable'): MWCC then writes the home slot in the prologue and reloads it per use, which also removes the common-subexpression temp and shrinks the frame to the retail's size (0x100 instead of 0x110). Reproduced in MOR_fn_xPrepareMorph (PhysColl.c). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail MOR_fn_xPrepareMorph addresses the engine objects table (tdstObjectsTablesList_.d_stObjectsTable, 20-byte elements) through the element's +4 field: in straight-line code the field offset is folded into the base ('addiu v0,v0,4; addu v0,v0,index*20; lw rd,0(v0)') and inside the comparison loop it is folded into the load offset ('addu v0,v0,index*20; lw rd,4(v0)'). Reproducing the straight-line shape needs the address-of form '(PO*)&table[index].h_Target' (MWCC folds a plain '*(&x)' back to 'x'). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 folds a constant byte offset into an address constant's LO16 relocation addend only when the base is a char-typed lvalue: '(char*)&sym + 1' compiles to lui/addiu/addiu (3 instructions), while '&struct.charMember + 1' compiles to lui/addiu with the +1 carried in the relocation addend (2 instructions). MWCC 3.0.3 folds the cast form as well. Retail AGO2_MEM_Shrink builds '&g_AGO2_MEM_FreeZones + 1' as one address constant, so reproducing it under the 3.0.1 game baseline needs a char-typed alias of the zone start (an anonymous union member in the FreeZone struct); the union keeps the 12-byte layout and the int member name. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a 12-byte struct assignment (three int fields) with lwc1/swc1 float loads, not integer lw/sw: 'zones[j] = zones[j+1]' emits lwc1/swc1 pairs, while copying the three fields individually ('zones[j].pStart = zones[j+1].pStart; ...') reproduces retail's integer lw/sw block copy (AGO2_MEM_FreeFromHandle's free-zone shift loop). (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The DWARF decl_file of an out-of-line copy of a usnd STL template member can be wrong: the retail symbol _Init__Q24usnd199stree<i,spair<Ci,P15CSoundSemaphore>,smap<int,CSoundSemaphore*>...>::_Init> at 0x001caab0 is named '_NilElmt__Q24usnd9streebase' in the debug record and attributed to sndcsoundsemaphore.cpp, but its retail line numbers (442-448) are stree.h's (identical to the CLinkTypeActor instantiation) and the address ordering puts it right after CSoundSemaphoreCont::mfn_roGetInstance (0x001caa20, which calls it) and before f_vSynchro (0x001cabd0) - i.e. in the sndcsoundsemaphorecont.cpp object. Trust the ELF address order and the line numbers, not the extracted file, for these weak template copies. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail MWCC lowers a short-circuit &&/|| used as an if-condition into a branch-based condition register: 'beqz s1,L_false; lw v0,0x10(s1); bgtz v0,L_true; L_false: move v1,zero; L_true: beqz v1,skip' (one 'move' at the false merge, the true value free in the register that already held 1). MWCC 3.0-011126/3.0.1-020123/3.0.3-020716/3.0b38-030307 instead emit either plain branches or slt/sltu value comparisons, so this retail form (313 'move vX,zero + beqz vX' sites in SLKA_250.78) cannot be reproduced from C with the available builds; an explicit-variable emulation (flag=0; if(a) if(b) flag=1; if(!flag) ...) yields the same shape but a different instruction count. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3 folds an if/else-if chain whose case conditions are duplicated (x==1 / x==0 / x==0 / x==1) by value-range analysis: inside the first branch the later tests are proven false and their bodies are dropped, which changes the program's semantics. Retail (IPT_fn_vReadInput) keeps all four tests, so such a chain must be written as four independent 'if' statements, not as else-if. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3, 3.0b38) hoists a global address setup ('lui %hi(sym)') into the delay slot of a conditional branch whose fall-through block starts with that access; retail in that situation keeps the nop and leaves the lui at the branch target (IPT_fn_vReadInput +0x50). Flagged as a delay-slot-fill compiler difference, like the return-block case in the existing insight. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] usnd STL (renamed Dinkumware xtree) API recovered from the retail PS2 objects: stree::_Lmost() is a non-const member returning the head node (_HeadLink), stree::_GetHead() is const and returns sTreeNode*, begin() = iterator(_Lmost()->m_poLeft), end() = iterator over the head, the iterator's operator++(void) returns void (retail mangling __pp__Q...8iteratorFv, the postfix form does not exist), iterator_base::operator== is out-of-line (__eq__Q34usnd9streebase13iterator_baseCFRC...) while operator!= is inlined as xor/sltiu/xori on the raw _Ptr, and the map value is reached through ((sTreeNode*)_Ptr)->m_oValue (the spair sits at +0x10 of the node). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0 all schedule a call whose argument is a plain register copy differently from the retail usnd STL code: retail keeps 'daddu a0,reg' as its own instruction before 'jal' and hoists the next statement's load into the jal delay slot, while MWCC folds the argument move into the delay slot and leaves the load after the call (4 bytes short, e.g. stree::_Erase in sndcsoundsemaphore.cpp). Reproduced with usnd_free(node); node = node->m_poLeft; - no source spelling tried (cast, void* temp, do/while, pragma peephole off) changes it. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 register allocation for the usnd STL container walk in CSoundSemaphore::f_vSynchro: retail assigns this->s0, the handle count->s1, the CSoundSemaphoreInfo* ->s2 and the byte offset of the static handle table->s3 (this is copied in the first jal delay slot), while the same natural C under MWCC 3.0.1 assigns ro_Info->s0, count->s1, offset->s2, this->s3 and copies 'this' eagerly in the prologue. Five local-declaration order permutations did not change the allocation; the residual is 640/640 bytes and 30/30 relocations, so it is a pure register-allocation difference. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail game code for TEMPGRP/Ago/AGO2_Mem.c sits between MWCC 3.0.1 and 3.0.3: AGO2_MEM_Shrink's '&g_AGO2_MEM_FreeZones + 1' address constant only folds into a single lui/addiu under 3.0.3, while its loop-unroller remainder loop matches 3.0.1 (3.0.3 pipelines the address computation into the loop head and changes the unrolled/rolled split). The sibling file TEMPGRP/Ago/AGO2.c is already in config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt, so the whole Ago group may have been built with a later compiler; no single available build matches retail exactly. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3 and every 3.0.1b/3.0b build tested) folds an array index with a constant offset into the final load's displacement: p[i-K] becomes (p + i*scale) - K*scale, emitted as 'lui at,0xffff' plus a signed 16-bit displacement, whereas retail computes (p - K) + i*scale, materialising the whole constant (lui 0xfffe / ori 0xc780) in $at and adding it to the base BEFORE the scaled index. Two source steps reproduce the retail form in 3.0.3: offset the pointer in its own statement with an explicit redundant cast (pp = (char**)(pp - 20000)), which stops the fold, and write the scaled index out through a dead local so it stays a separate register (*(char**)((char*)pp + (iIndex = hString * 4))). Without the dead local the base and index come out swapped (base v1, index v0) and the result is only EQUIVALENT. Matched FON_fn_szGetTextPointerForHandle (Anim.c) 116/116 bytes with the retail line map (body on one source line, closing brace on the next: the retail line table has entries only at +0x0000 and +0x006c). Related: the dead-index idiom of 2026-10-11 and the swapped base/index pair of 2026-09-30. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 attributes an instruction that ends up in a conditional branch's delay slot to the BRANCH's source line, not to the line of the statement that produced it. In HMU_fn_vSndFree the retail's 'daddu s1,zero,zero' (the i = 0 of the direct-sound search loop) sits in the delay slot of the beqz that tests HMU_fn_bIsInitialized(), so the target line map lists it under the branch's line 604 even though the initialisation is written on the loop's line 607. A delay-slot instruction therefore never contributes a line-map entry of its own. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 builds the byte-offset induction variable of an indexed array loop as index*elementSize (sll) when the index initialisation is not adjacent to the loop, but as an independent offset IV initialised with a literal zero ('daddu s2,zero,zero') when the 'i = 0' sits in the loop's own for header. Moving 'i = 0;' from before a guard into the for header changed HMU_fn_vSndFree's +0x44 from 'sll s2,s1,2' to 'daddu s1,zero,zero' and +0x5c from 'sll' to 'daddu s2,zero,zero', turning a 312/312 byte mismatch into a full MATCH with the retail line map. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 attributes the branch that jumps out of a single-statement if/else arm to the closing brace's source line, so braces on their own line add a line-map entry the retail does not have. Removing the braces (or keeping them on the statement's line) makes the join branch inherit the statement's line and reproduces the retail line map; confirmed for the inner if/else of HMU_fn_vFree and for the bIsInitialized[1] test of HMU_fn_vSndFree, both of which then matched with lines=ok. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The extracted debug local list of HMU_Mem.c pairs the stack offsets with the wrong names: for HMU_fn_vFree the extractor lists (p_vBeginBloc, uwBlocId, eMemoryBlockUse) with offsets (garbage, 0x38, 0x3e), but the retail calls Mmg_fn_vWhereIs(ptr, &uwBlocId, &p_vBeginBloc) with a1 = sp+0x3e and a2 = sp+0x38, i.e. uwBlocId is the u16 at +0x3e and p_vBeginBloc the pointer at +0x38 (same in HMU_fn_vSndFree: +0x6e and +0x68). Use the call's argument registers to place the address-taken locals instead of the extracted offsets. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 register allocation for repeated '*(quad*)pBuf++ = *(quad*)src' copies: the direct form makes the allocator give the five source addresses the high temporaries (t1,t0,a3,a2,a1) and the destination pointers the low ones (v1,a0), while the retail gives the sources a3,a2,a1,a0,v1 and the destinations t0/t1. Splitting the copy into 'q = (quad*)pBuf++; *q = *(quad*)src;' (an explicit local pointer) reproduces the retail allocation exactly - confirmed byte-for-byte on impPS2glVertex2fv/2iv/2f/3fv/4fv in PS2GL_DataInput.c. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 stores a constant float to memory with an integer store, not swc1: '(*pVBuf)[2] = 0.0f' becomes 'sw zero,8(reg)' and '(*pVBuf)[3] = 1.0f' becomes 'lui t2,0x3f80' + 'sw t2,0xc(reg)'. A float-typed lvalue with a constant value therefore keeps the integer-store form; the same constant in a non-constant expression uses swc1. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 lowers '(float)someInt' as 'lwc1 fd,off(base)' followed by 'cvt.s.w fd,fd' - the int bits are loaded into an FPR and converted there, not loaded into a GPR first. Seen in impPS2glLightiv (int* params -> float fparams[4]) and impPS2glVertex2iv (int* v -> float x,y). (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] PS2GL tagGLcontext: the field at +0x6c24 (extractor name pnColorMatMasks) is a POINTER to int, not an int - retail does 'lw a0,-0x29FC(at); lw v1,0(a0)' on it, i.e. it is used as '*(int*)g_PS2glContext.pnColorMatMasks'. The same 'p'-prefixed fields after it (pbFogging +0x6c28, peFogMode +0x6c2c, pfFogDensity +0x6c30 ...) are also pointers. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 line attribution: an instruction the scheduler moves into a branch or call delay slot inherits the branch's line (not its own statement's line), and a hoisted 'lui' for a global access gets the line of the statement that uses that global. This makes the retail line map reproducible by grouping the statements of one copy onto a single source line (e.g. 'q = (quad*)pBuf++; *q = *(quad*)src;' on one line). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: an unconditional branch to a shared function-tail return block is delay-slot-filled like a conditional one. In Keyword.c fn_p_stEngineGotoKeyWord the error path that falls through to the function's final 'return p_stTree;' is 'b <move v0,s1 block>' with a nop delay slot in retail, while MWCC 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3 emit 'b <epilogue>' with the return block's 'move v0,s1' hoisted into the delay slot (the move block then stays as dead code). Retail delay-fills its other two return paths (parameter-zero and positive-error) in the same function, so the fill decision is per-path; no scoped pragma (schedule, peephole, conditional_move, tailcall, opt_*, optimization_level) and no compiler build changes it. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: the 16->32-bit promotion of an unsigned-short struct field is coalesced in place with the halfword load (andi v0,v0,0xffff) whenever the load is dead afterwards, while retail sometimes copies it into a fresh register (andi v1,v0,0xffff) and reuses the load's register for another temp. Keyword.c fn_p_stElseKeyWord: retail loads uwNodeToSkip into v0, masks it into v1 and computes the base pointer into v0; MWCC keeps the mask in v0 and puts the base in v1. The same source shape in retail fn_p_stIfNotU64 is masked in place, so this is a register-allocator heuristic difference, not a source-shape one: ~40 source variants (pointer vs byte-pointer arithmetic, unsigned-short/int locals, separate base statements, operand order, type casts) and all four compiler builds keep the mask coalesced. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Source-shape evidence (Keyword.c fn_p_stElseKeyWord): the retail skip computation materialises the node base pointer separately (addiu v0,s0,-8) and adds a separately scaled index (sll v1,v1,3; addu v0,v0,v1), which is reproduced by the byte-arithmetic form '(char*)p_stNode + p_stNode->uwNodeToSkip * sizeof(tdstNodeInterpret_)'. The plain pointer form 'p_stNode + p_stNode->uwNodeToSkip' makes MWCC fold the -8 into the final sum (addu v0,v0,s0; addiu v0,v0,-8) and does not match. The same byte-arithmetic spelling appears in Cond.c fn_p_stBooleanCondition (p_stTree->uwNodeToSkip * sizeof(tdstNodeInterpret_) + (char*)p_stTree). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (game baseline): the field-wise int-lvalue copy (*(int*)&d.xX = *(int*)&s.xX) makes MWCC MATERIALIZE the field address in a callee-saved register (extra saved regs, +0x20 frame) and load through it, while a union view with an int[3] member (union { MTH3D_tdstVector_ v; int i[3]; }) keeps the source loads sp-relative and emits the destination as one base register with offsets 0/4/8. Both spellings give lw/sw word copies; only the union view reproduced the retail bytes exactly (MultiVP.c fn_vInitCameraMatrix, 204/204 MATCH after the union change). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The retail 'unfolded element-offset' store form: in Procedur.c fn_vUpdateCharacterSkinList (0x228a10) and fn_p_stSkinMaterialProcedure (0x228850), retail materialises the struct base for STORES (sll idx; addiu base,off; addu base,idx; sw/sh at 0(base)) while the loop LOADS in the same functions stay folded (lw v,0x1c4(base+idx)) - retail folds a constant member offset into loads but not into stores. MWCC 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 fold it into both, and no natural spelling reproduces the store form: plain array, cast to the same type, (char*)base+off, byte-arithmetic (char*)&arr[0]+i*sizeof(T), a char* temp with a separate '+off' statement, and the dead scaled-index subscript all fold. Only a void*-typed temporary keeps the base materialised (sw at 0(base)), and that form evaluates the base before the index while retail evaluates the index first, so it still mismatches. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0.3-020716 does not pad a loop's back-branch to 8 bytes where 3.0.1-020123 does: in Procedur.c fn_p_stModuleControlProcedure (0x23a190) retail has 'addiu s0,s0,-1; nop; bgez s0,loop; nop' (the branch at 0xa0, 8-aligned) and MWCC 3.0.1 reproduces it exactly (268/268 MATCH), while 3.0.3 leaves the branch at 0x9c. The file is pinned to 3.0.3 in config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt, so the function cannot match there; moving Procedur.c to 3.0.1 would gain fn_p_stModuleControlProcedure but loses 5 recorded matches that only build under 3.0.3 (fn_p_stValidateMemoryCard, fn_p_stLoadingLevel, fn_p_stVideoProcedure, fn_p_stRLIProcedure2, PS2_fn_vSetControllerMessage). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Procedur.c is Latin-1 encoded (the French/Spanish/Italian/German controller-message strings in PS2_fn_vSetControllerMessage). Any tool that rewrites the file as UTF-8 (the editor or a python read_text/write_text round trip) replaces those bytes with U+FFFD and silently corrupts a function that is already recorded as MATCH. After editing this file, restore the untouched bytes with 'git show HEAD:<path> > <path>' (byte copy) and confirm 'git diff' shows only the intended lines. (2026-10-20)

- [abi] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 o32 passes EIGHT integer/pointer arguments in registers (a0-a3 then t0-t3) and puts argument 9 and beyond in 8-byte stack slots, even for 4-byte parameters: the caller stores them with sd (a zero slot as 'sd zero', a 32-bit value as 'sd vX' after a lw). Verified on the 11-parameter fn_vChangeViewPortPercent call in MultiVP.c (args 5-8 in t0-t3, args 9-11 at 0x0(sp)/0x8(sp)/0x10(sp)), which MATCHed 188/188. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC line-table rule for matching retail line maps: the code of a call inside an 'if (...)' condition is attributed to the line of the block's opening brace, not to the 'if' line; and several statements written on one physical line collapse into a single line-table entry. Putting an if-body's statements on the brace line therefore removes the extra entries and reproduced the retail offsets of fn_vCreateGameViewPort (444/444 MATCH). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 'dsll32 rd, rs, 0' is a 64-bit left shift by 32 (it materialises the constant 2^32), NOT a sign extension: dsll32/dsra32 together are the int conversion and dsll32/dsrl32 the unsigned-int conversion. A 32-bit borrow is therefore written 'x + ((1UL << 32) - y)' (MWCC emits 'addiu r,zero,1' + 'dsll32 r,r,0' + 'dsubu r,r,y' + 'daddu'), while 'x + (1 - y)' keeps the 1 as a plain 64-bit value and emits no shift. Seen in Timer.c TMR_fn_wReadTimer. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 narrows a 64-bit load to 'lw' when the value is only used through an int: 'int iSum = a + b;' (64-bit members) loads b with lw and adds with addu, but 'long lSum = a + b;' with an explicit '(int)' cast at the use keeps the 64-bit 'ld' and the 64-bit 'daddu' before the dsll32/dsra32 (int) + dsll32/dsrl32 (unsigned int) conversions. The 32-bit narrowing is semantically valid, so only the wider spelling reproduces the retail bytes (Timer.c TMR_fn_wReadTimer tail). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: the comparison spelling 'x > N-1' (e.g. 'timerType > 2') compiles to 'slti $at, x, N' with the scratch register, while 'x >= N' keeps the result in a normal register ('slti v0, x, N'). Retail uses the $at form for the '> N-1' spelling (Timer.c TMR_fn_wSetTimerValue, wResetTimer, wStartTimer, wCreateTimer all need '> 2' to match). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2: an error-check written as 'if (cond) { Erm_fn_v_UpdateLastError(...); return 0x7FFF; }' (error block as the THEN branch) compiles to a conditional branch that SKIPS the error block ('slti/bnez' to the success path), while the equivalent 'if (cond) {success} else {error}' produces the opposite branch polarity and a different block layout. Timer.c matches with the first form for the library/handle/state/type checks and with 'if (x >= 16) {error}' for the range check. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2: a retail block that stores a phi register at a join ('sh v1,0(s1)' after 'beq state,4,join' with 'addiu v1,zero,2' in the delay slot) is not a merged conditional store but an UNCONDITIONAL store of a constant plus a 'skip the reset' test: 'if (state != 4) { reset; } state = 2;'. The delay slot then carries the constant of the unconditional store, and the store itself is emitted once after the join (Timer.c TMR_fn_wStartTimer, 400/400 MATCH). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 keeps a value live across a branch only when the source names it in a local: a direct 'a_stTimerTab[i].member' read used in both arms of an if/else is REMATERIALISED (the address is recomputed in each arm, 804-byte candidate), while copying the member into a block-local 'unsigned long' makes the compiler load it once, keep it in a register across the branch and fill the branch delay slot with the second load (exactly 756 bytes, the retail size). The local must be declared inside the case block, after the call that can modify the global. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail lowers an array access whose element index is (index-1) as (arrayBase - elementSize) + index*elementSize, materialising the constant on the base: 'lw base; sll idx,idx,2; addiu base,base,-4; addu base,base,idx; lw 0(base)'. MWCC PS2 3.0-011126, 3.0.1-020123, 3.0.3-020716 and 3.0b38-030307 instead fold the constant into the load displacement ('lw base,-4(base)', 1 instruction shorter) or compute (index-1) then scale ('addiu idx,idx,-1; sll idx,idx,2'). The retail idiom occurs only 5 times in the whole ELF: fn_p_stGetPersoInList (AI/AIBase/List.c), fn_p_stSOLEdit x3 (AI/AIGame/ProcRay2.cxx) and WPG_fn_hRecursiveBuildBestOrderedPathFromAtoB (AI/AIBase/WPgraphe.c). On our compiler the only spelling that materialises the -4 on the base is an int-cast base ('int lBase = (int)p_List->p_hTabElt - 4;'), but that changes the register allocation of the whole function (the count ends in a2 instead of v1), so the retail shape is not reproducible with the available builds. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.3 register allocation depends on the type of the base part of an address expression, not only on the source order: with a pointer base ('(char*)p_List->p_hTabElt + (count << 2)', or 'p_List->p_hTabElt[count-1]' through a pointer variable) the byte-loaded count ends up in $v1 and the masked position in $a2, and the 0xff-branch base/scaled-index pair is $v0/$v1; with an int base ('(int)p_List->p_hTabElt - 4 + ...') the count ends up in $a2, the position in $v1, and the pair becomes $v1/$v0. Reproduced with a 5x5 cross product of base/index spellings on fn_p_stGetPersoInList, so an int-cast workaround for one code-shape problem silently swaps the allocation of the whole function. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Evidence that the retail 'addiu base,-4 + index*4' lowering of an (index-1) array access is a codegen-context artefact, not a source property: the retail ELF contains BOTH forms for the same source - the standalone fn_p_stGetPersoInList (AI/AIBase/List.c) keeps 'addiu base,-4; addu base,base,idx<<2; lw 0(base)', while the retail INLINED copy of that very function inside fn_vSortListByModel (AI/AIBase/List.c, MATCH) folds the constant into the load ('lw base,-4(base)'). One C source therefore has to lower two different ways, which no single MWCC 3.0.x build can do; an int-cast base unfolds both but breaks the already-matched fn_vSortListByModel. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 emits 'addi' (not 'addiu') for the switch dispatch index when the case labels are contiguous constants: 'switch (x) { case 0x200: ...; case 0x207: ...; }' compiles to 'addi v1,v1,-0x200; sltiu at,v1,8; lui/addiu table; jr'. The retail PS2GL driver code in u_SPGArray.cpp/u_drawSpriteGen.c has exactly that 'addi v0,v0,-0x200' which spimdisasm marks as 'handwritten instruction', so that note is a false alarm: probes of 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 show they all use addiu for a plain 'x - 0x200' expression and only the switch dispatch produces the addi. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 bitfield container type decides the access width: a bitfield declared in an 'unsigned char' container is read and written as a byte ('lbu'/'sb' plus a mask/or of the shifted value), while the same bitfield in an 'unsigned long' container uses a word access. The retail PS2GL driver updates g_gsContext+0x12a (ZTE at bit 0, ZTST at bits 1-2) and g_gsContext+0x1b4 (ModFlgs bit 37 = TEST_1_Modified) with byte accesses, so the game's tagGScontext must declare ZTE/ZTST and the ModFlgs flags 32-39 in unsigned char containers; the extractor's 'unsigned long' containers cannot produce those bytes. Reproduced with a probe: 'unsigned char g:1' gives 'lbu/sb' with masks 0xfe/0xf9. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2's choice between folded and unfolded addressing of an indexed struct member depends on the WHOLE translation unit, not only on the function: in u_SPGArray.cpp the identical natural source 'p->m_pV[p->m_CurrentFrameIndex]' compiles to the unfolded form (materialised base 'addiu', 'addu base,index*4', 'lw 0(base)') while UnAcquireAndDraw is still an empty stub and to the folded form ('addu base,index*4; lw 4(base)') once that body is filled in. The same function body therefore matched 232/232 before and 220/232 after an unrelated edit in the same file. Re-check both spellings (and re-run the check) before concluding that a source shape is wrong, and be aware that reconstructing one function can change another function's code in the same TU. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 keeps a local whose address is taken in memory and re-reads it after the store ('sw r,off(sp)' followed by 'lw r2,off(sp)'), while retail code tests and dereferences the same pointer through a REGISTER copy and only hands the stack slot's address to the free function. Reproducing retail needs two locals: a register-resident copy used for the field accesses and an address-taken copy used for the call, e.g. 'if ((p_stDynamic = p_stDyn = h_Dynam->p_stDynamics, p_stDyn != NULL)) { ... p_stDyn->flags ... HMU_fn_vFree((void**)&p_stDynamic, 1); }' (GAM/Actions/Dynam.c fn_v_DynamicFree, fn_vDynamCopyClone: the same split reproduces the inlined copy in fn_vDynamFree and fn_vDynamDesinit). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The game's PS2GL driver (u_SPGArray.cpp, u_drawSpriteGen.c, ...) copies the PS2GL library's impPS2glDepthFunc: 'unsigned char zTst = 1; switch (g_PS2glContext.eDepthFunc) { case 0x200: zTst=0; break; case 0x207: zTst=1; break; case 0x203: zTst=2; break; case 0x201: zTst=3; break; case 0x202: case 0x204: case 0x205: case 0x206: break; } g_gsContext.TEST_1.ZTST = zTst; g_gsContext.ModFlgs.TEST_1_Modified = 1;' — the retail case-block order (0x200, 0x207, 0x203, 0x201) and the reuse of the initial 1 for 'case 0x207' match that source exactly. The library source is in the repo at src/Rayman3/dev/R3/Version13/ps2/PS2GL20/Src/PS2GL/PS2GL_Fragment.c. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] u_SPGArray.cpp has one common residual across three functions (VAC_Acquire, C_LoadingScreen::LoadTexture, C_VertexArrayContainer::UnAcquireAndDraw): code, size and relocations match the retail exactly but the callee-saved/temporary registers sit one position higher than retail (retail a2/a1/a0 + a3 vs candidate a3/a2/a1 + a0; retail s2=this/s1=NbSprites vs candidate s3/s2). Tried without effect: all 24 local declaration orders, local types (void*/unsigned int), block-scoped locals, moving the saved-value capture, statement reordering, and the other four available MWCC builds (3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38). It looks like a register-allocator difference for this translation unit rather than a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail flag updates in an if/else store the field THREE times (once per arm, once at the join, and a copy of the join store in the following branch's delay slot). Plain 'field |= X; field &= ~X;' in the two arms only produces the two arm stores; routing the value through a local reproduces all three: 'ulFlags = field | mask; field = ulFlags;' in each arm plus a trailing 'field = ulFlags;' at the join (GAM/Actions/Dynam.c fn_v_DynamicAlloc and fn_vDynamCopyClone, both MATCH with the retail line offsets). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC's line table has one entry per source LINE, not per statement: two statements written on the same line share one entry, so retail code that puts a whole if/else arm on one line reproduces the retail line offsets exactly. Confirmed on GAM/Actions/Dynam.c fn_v_DynamicAlloc (the two flag blocks, and the '&=' / '|=' pairs of the switch cases) and fn_vDynamCopyClone (the eSize selection, the two 'if (bAlloc) { call(); }' blocks and the final flag block): reformatting those blocks onto one line changed lines=different to lines=ok with no byte change. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1-020123 (game baseline, -O3,p) pads a conditional branch target to 8 bytes by turning the branch's delay slot into a nop, while 3.0.3-020716 leaves a 4-mod-8 target alone and fills the delay slot with the fall-through's first instruction. Evidence: HIE_fn_vFastPS2PushMatrix (HieMtStk.c) - retail 'bnez a2, 0x32012c' with 'sll v1,a2,1' in the delay slot; 3.0.1 emits 'nop' and moves the target to 0x320130 (+4 bytes), 3.0.3 reproduces retail byte-for-byte (476/476). A file whose only residual is such a nop needs the 3.0.3 per-file compiler entry. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail game code computes a 2-level array access as (base + outer_index*stride) + 0 + inner_index*scale: the zero is a separate 'addiu x,x,0' add between the outer and inner index terms (IPT_Pad.c 10 sites, ipt_kwan.s 22, DefineMenu2D.s 16, FFB.s 13, AGO3 13, sndeethrd 8; 96 sites in 12 files). It appears only when the array base is a runtime value AND the inner index is a variable; 1-level accesses (PLD g_ax_pA3dGENERAL[i]) and constant inner indices (PLD g_tdstStackInfos[i][0]) have no zero add. MWCC 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 always fold the zero (no source spelling reproduces it: plain access, &arr[0] casts, char* arithmetic, pointer-to-array casts, 2D-array casts, volatile and scoped pragma variants were all tried), so this is a compiler-build residual, not a source-shape problem. No recorded MATCH in the repo reproduces it. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0-011126, 3.0.1-020123, 3.0.3-020716, 3.0b38-030307) always folds a ZERO constant offset out of an indexed address, while the retail compiler keeps it as an explicit 'addiu reg,reg,0' between the base part and the scaled variable index. Retail: 'addu v0,v0,v1; addiu v0,v0,0x0; sll v1,a2,2; addu v0,v0,v1' = (base + row) + 0 + index*size; ours: 'addu v1,v1,a2; addu v0,v0,v1'. Evidence: PS2MC_SetDisplayMessage (0x00331ea0) &PS2MC_g_MessageTable[lang][id] (116 vs 120 bytes) and the three PS2MC_g_Buttons[lang][choice] accesses in PS2MC Display (0x003321c0, 580 vs 572); the same shape also appears in sndeethrd usnd_vLeaveCritSect, the INO FFB functions, the Menu2D family and the IPT family. About 40 source spellings were tried (2D/1D array subscripts, char* casts with an explicit +0, pointer-to-row locals, dead-index assignments, row-pointer variables) and every one of them folds the zero; none of the 68 real 'addiu reg,reg,0' instructions in the retail ELF lies inside a recorded match, so this family is still unresolved and looks like a retail-compiler-build difference rather than a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 'x == 0' is 'sltiu rd,rs,1' (1 iff rs == 0) while 'x != 0' is 'sltu rd,zero,rs'; a comparison result that is stored into an unsigned char and later tested with an explicit '!= 0' keeps the char re-extension 'andi v1,v1,0xff' before the 'beqz', whereas a bare truth test lets MWCC drop the mask. Reproduced on HIE_fn_vFastPS2PushMatrix (HieMtStk.c): 'unsigned char b; b = (f == cur); if (!b) b = (f == -1); if (b != 0) {...}' yields 'xor/sltiu v1,v1,1; bnez; nor/sltiu v1,v1,1; andi v1,v1,0xff; beqz' exactly. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail IPT_Pad.c bIsValidatedPadButton/bIsInvalidatedPadButton are single ternary returns, not if-chains: '((hPad != NULL) && (hPad->lState > 0)) || ((hKey != NULL) && (hKey->lState > 0)) ? (unsigned char)1 : (unsigned char)0' (and the && variant with lState < 0) reproduce the target's branch polarity, block layout and register allocation exactly. The debug local list order is [param0, hPadButton], but declaring hPadButton FIRST is what matches the retail's v1/a0 allocation - the extracted local order is reversed relative to the original source here. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The retail compiler lowers a signed division by 2 to 'srl rd,rs,1; andi at,rs,1; or rd,rd,at' (a logical shift with a sign fix-up) and only evaluates it on the negative path, and it applies the (x/2)*2 = x simplification for x >= 0. MWCC PS2 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 instead use 'addiu rd,rs,1; sra rd,rd,1' and evaluate the division on both paths. Evidence: PS2MC CalculateMessagePosition (0x00331f20) computes '(float)(NbLines/2)*2.0f' as 'bltz s1; srl a0,s1,1; mtc1 s1,fv0; ...; andi v1,s1,1; or a0,a0,v1; cvt.s.w; add.s fv0,fv0,fv0' (the positive path is just (float)NbLines, the doubling is a float add) where our builds emit 'bgez s1; addiu v1,s1,1; sra v1,v1,1; cvt.s.w; mul.s' (232 vs 216 bytes). The same source can also be written '(float)(x/2) + (float)(x/2)' to get the float add.s, but the x >= 0 simplification still does not appear. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 register allocation for a block with several address locals is fixed by TWO independent orders: the local DECLARATION order decides which pointer gets which register, and the ASSIGNMENT order decides which address is computed first. They are not interchangeable - on HIE_fn_vFastPS2PushMatrix (HieMtStk.c) the retail allocation needed declaration order (matrix, previous, stackMatrix, globalMatrix, previousMatrix, localMatrix) with assignment order (matrix, previous, globalMatrix, previousMatrix, stackMatrix, localMatrix); every other combination left a 10-28 instruction EQUIVALENT/MISMATCH residual. Search the product of the two orders when a block's addresses are right but its registers are not. (2026-10-20)

- [tooling] The retail ELF (SLKA_250.78) keeps a .relmain section holding the LINKED relocations of the main section, so the exact symbol (and addend) behind every lui/addiu/lw pair can be read directly instead of guessed from the disassembly: parse .relmain (its sh_link is the .symtab index), read the symbol names from .symtab/.strtab. Example: C_PS2MC_Message::GetAnswer (0x00332000) loads g_st_3DOS_EntryActions+0xa0/+0xa4/+0x98/+0x9c/+0x94 - the hEngineMenu_Left/Right/Up/Down/Enter entry elements - and g_stEngineStructure+0x70 (stEngineTimer.ulCurrentTimerCount), while the string literals are the anonymous @714="\n", @820="\n\n", @821="\\c255:255:0:", @822="\\c125:125:125:", @823="    ". This resolved the PS2MC function signatures, the field offsets and the message-index comparison (37 = MC_eAreYouSureSaveNo) that the debug records did not name. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] VU0 matrix product in MWCC PS2 asm: 'lqc2 vf1..vf4' (source columns) + 'lqc2 vf5..vf8' (second operand columns) then 'vmulax.xyzw ACC, vf1, vf5x / vmadday.xyzw ACC, vf2, vf5y / vmaddz.xyzw vf9, vf3, vf5z' per output column, and 'vmaddaz/vmaddw' for the last column, with the result stored with 'sqc2'. This is the standard PS2 4x4 product (the same idiom as HieSpObj.c) and is emittable from a statement-level 'asm { }' block with symbolic C pointer operands; one instruction per source line reproduces the retail line map. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 -inline deferred cannot expand a function at one call site and leave it out of line at another: the 'inline' keyword expands at every call site, and a '#pragma dont_inline on' placed before the definition OR before a caller suppresses the inline for the whole unit (the deferred inlining pass sees the pragma state at the end of the unit), which also breaks the call sites that retail did expand. Retail evidence (AITools.c): MAT_fn_vApplyOnVertex is called out of line from fn_xExtendedDistanceBetweenPerso (two jal sites) but is expanded into fn_vGetCenterOfBoundingVolume (168-byte body with the VU0 lqc2/vmulax block). Workaround: keep a plain out-of-line 'void MAT_fn_vApplyOnVertex' plus a 'static inline' copy used only by the caller retail expanded - the static copy is dropped by MWCC when fully inlined, so no extra object symbol appears, and the out-of-line copy keeps the retail's 124-byte symbol. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC emits one .line row per SOURCE LINE CHANGE, so the retail's row offsets are reproduced by putting the statements of one retail 'statement group' on a single source line. AITools.c fn_xDistanceBetweenPersoAndWP (retail rows at 0x00/0x08/0x10/0x18/0x28/0x30/0x68/0x84 = lines 184/187/188/187/188/189/190/191) matches only when the whole matrix-translation copy (pointer init + three word copies) is one line, the waypoint call is the next line, the three component subtractions are one line, the reload + MTH_SQR3_TO/MTH_SQRT_TO are one line and the return is the last line. Written one statement per line the same C produces 22 rows and 'lines=different' even though the bytes are identical. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail difference blocks (Rayman 3 AI tools) compute a vector distance as 'xX = stVertex1.xX - stVertex2.xX; stVertex1.xX = xX; xY = ...; xY = ...; xZ = ...; stVertex1.xZ = xZ;' followed by a reload line 'xZ = stVertex1.xZ; xX = stVertex1.xX; xY = stVertex1.xY;' - the reload order (z, x, y) is what makes MWCC put z in the first FPR and x/y in the next two, and it also fixes which member address gets v0 vs v1. Writing the subtractions as 'stVertex1.xX -= stVertex2.xX;' (compound form) gives a different register assignment (x in f0, y in f1, z in f2, &xY in v1, &xZ in v0) and never matches: AITools.c fn_xDistanceBetweenPersoAndWP matched 144/144 with the temporary form. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail IPT_Pad.c bIsJustInvalidatedPadButton/bIsJustValidatedPadButton: the target line map shows the whole 'both buttons non-NULL' branch on ONE source line (267 / 238) and the NULL-button branch spread over four lines (268-271 / 239-242), so the first branch was a single nested expression (ternary) in the original. Semantics recovered: invalidated = (pad->lState==-1 && key->lState<0) || (pad->lState<0 && key->lState==-1); validated = (pad->lState==1 && key->lState<=0) || (pad->lState<=0 && key->lState==1); a NULL button means 'not invalidated/validated'. The retail's else branch also contains an always-true 'bne v0,v0' (the key state load is dead) - a retail compiler-build artefact. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] VUSprites.cpp: the retail ELF gives the free functions C linkage (GLI_vReinitSprites, GLI_vEndNewSprite, GLI_xSetMaterialTextureForTXT, PS2ASM_SearchBlocInChain are unmangled symbols) while the CSpriteController methods are C++-mangled (Draw__17CSpriteControllerFv). Declaring the free functions 'extern "C"' makes MWCC emit '.line_GLI_vReinitSprites' instead of '.line_GLI_vReinitSprites__Fv', and the verifier's line-offset check only finds the retail's line map through the unmangled .line section (the mangled spelling yields 'lines=different' with an empty candidate table). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC emits one .line row per source-line change, and the retail VUSprites.cpp wrote these bodies on a SINGLE source line: GLI_vReinitSprites/GLI_vEndNewSprite/GLI_xSetMaterialTextureForTXT have retail line maps with only five rows (prologue, hoisted constant, prologue again, body, epilogue). Putting the whole body of each on one line reproduces the retail rows exactly (lines=ok); one statement per line gives the same bytes but 15 rows and 'lines=different'. The 'if (!StartSpriteList(...)) return 1;' must also stay on one line (the retail groups the call, the branch and the li v0,1 in one row). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 stores a float constant of 1.0f into a float array as an integer word ('lui v1,0x3f80' + 'sw v1,off(base)'), but the denormal 0x00008000 that the retail quad vertices carry as their W component needs a union { int i; float f; } local: the int member is stored with 'ori v0,zero,0x8000' + 'sw v0,off(sp)' and the float member is read back with 'lwc1 fv0,off(sp)' then 'swc1'. A plain 'float fW = 0x8000;' converts the int and cannot reproduce it. This is how CSpriteController::WorldQuad/WorldQuadWithUV write W = 0x8000 for corners 0/1 and 1.0f for corners 2/3 (the vertex order in the retail is x1, x2, x4, x3). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 only emits a jump table for a switch when enough case labels are written: four sparse labels (0,1,3,7) produced a beq compare chain, eight labels produced the table - and the case blocks are then laid out in MWCC's own order (the empty block second), not in source order. The retail EndDraw switch (CSpriteController::EndDraw) uses jtbl_0035B780 at 0x35B780; reading its eight entries gives 0->zTst 0, 1->zTst 3, 3->zTst 2, 7->empty block, and 2/4/5/6 -> the default (the join), i.e. the GL depth function index maps to the GS ZTEST code with a one-step rotation (LESS->LEQUAL, EQUAL->LESS, LEQUAL->EQUAL). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] PS2ASM_SearchBlocInChain in VUSprites.cpp is hand-written assembly in the retail ELF, not C: 11 line-map rows for 12 instructions (one per instruction, the jr's delay slot shares the jr's row), a dead copy of the loop body at +0x08, and the body duplicated into the back-branch delay slot - 25+ natural C shapes under all four MWCC builds reproduce none of them. MWCC's 'asm' function syntax reproduces it byte-for-byte: 'asm void* f(void* Chain, void* Bloc) { b L; nop; lw $a0,8($a0); L: beqz $a0,E; sltu $at,$a0,$a1; beqz $at,E; nop; b L; lw $a0,8($a0); E: nop; jr $ra; or $v0,$a0,$zero }' - note that 'move $v0,$a0' assembles to addu (0x00801021) while the retail uses 'or' (0x0080102d), and the free function needs 'extern "C"' to keep the unmangled retail symbol. (2026-10-20)

- [abi] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 o32 ABI: the 7th argument of a member function is passed in $t2 (a0-a3, t0, t1, t2), not on the stack - CSpriteController::WorldQuad(this, pViewport, x1, x2, x3, x4, pMaterial) reads pMaterial from $t2 ('daddu $a1,$t2,$zero') and passes it to StartSpriteList in a1. The callee simply copies $t2 to a1, so a 6-parameter declaration (7 arguments with 'this') is required to reproduce it. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: the dsll32/dsra32 shift amount encodes the source integer width - 'dsll32 rd,rs,24 / dsra32 rd,rd,24' is an 8-bit (char) sign extension, '...16' is 16-bit (short), '...0' is 32-bit (int). Confirmed in Basic.c GAM_fn_vSetLoadingLevel: a 'short' loop counter gave dsll32/dsra32 by 16 and the retail (by 24, i.e. char) only matched after the local was declared 'char cI'. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0/b38 all fold 'p = q + 1; use *p' into an indexed load from the pre-increment register (lb v1,1(q)) plus 'addiu s0,q,1' for the variable, while retail Basic.c fn_vSetLevelName materialises the address once (addiu v0,v0,1; lb v1,0(v0); move s0,v0) and tests the raw Strchr result (bnez v0) instead of the stored pointer. Tried: separate pointer/char-pointer variables, pre/post increment, explicit +1, index [0]/[1], const char*, atoi(char*) vs atoi(const char*), and the dead-assignment subscript idiom - all fold the increment, so this residual is a compiler-build or source-shape difference, not a flag difference. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 inline asm accepts VU0 lqc2/sqc2 with a float* operand: 'asm { lqc2 vf1, 0x0(p_fVector); lqc2 vf2, 0x0(p_stMatrix); ... vmulax.xyzw ACC, vf2, vf1x; vmadday.xyzw ACC, vf3, vf1y; vmaddaz.xyzw ACC, vf4, vf1z; vmaddw.xyzw vf6, vf5, vf0w; sqc2 vf6, 0x0(p_fVector) }' reproduces the retail matrix*vector product instruction-for-instruction (Basic.c GAM_fn_vAskToChangeLevel). Binding a struct local directly ('lqc2 vf1, 0x0(stVector)') fails with "'stVector' could not be assigned to a register"; pass a float* / float(*)[4] pointer instead. A float(*)[4] pointing at &m_Matrix gives the 0x0/0x10/0x20/0x30 column offsets. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Basic.c's 'FirstTime' is a FILE-SCOPE static in the retail source (symbol FirstTime$404 at gp-0x7C50) shared by fn_p_szGetLevelName and GAM_fn_vAskToChangeLevel; keeping it as a function-local static of fn_p_szGetLevelName preserves that function's recorded relocation, but moving it to file scope (at any position tried: before/after the globals, next to fn_p_szGetLevelName) changes the .sdata layout so the gp-relative relocation no longer pairs with the retail address, and a local static cannot be referenced from a second function at all. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail switch cases in the Rayman 3 AI tools accumulate the norm into the FIRST component variable itself, not into a separate 'xSqr': AITools.c fn_xExtendedDistanceBetweenPerso's target code is 'mula.s f0,f0 / madd.s f0,f2,f2 / sqrt.s f0,f0' for the x^2+y^2 case (the destination register equals the first operand's register), 'mula.s f2,f2 / madd.s f0,f1,f1' for y^2+z^2 and 'mula.s f0,f0 / madda.s f2,f2 / madd.s f0,f1,f1' for the full norm. Reproduce it by calling the MTH_SQR2_TO/MTH_SQR3_TO macros with the first component as the result ('MTH_SQR2_TO(xX, xX, xY)', 'MTH_SQR2_TO(xX, xY, xZ)', 'MTH_SQR3_TO(xX, xX, xY, xZ)') and taking the sqrt of xX; using a separate xSqr makes MWCC allocate a callee-saved FPR (f20) for the result, adds an 8-byte spill slot to the frame and shifts every stack offset. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail game code sometimes keeps a pointer parameter live across a call in a caller-saved register (t2) instead of a callee-saved one, which shrinks the stack frame by 0x10 and moves every local offset (AITools.c fn_xExtendedDistanceBetweenPerso: p_SuperObjPerso2 in t2, ucFlags in v0, only s0/s1/s2 saved, frame -0xc0). MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3 always copies such parameters into callee-saved registers (s1/s0/s3), so the frame grows to -0xd0/-0xe0 and every local offset shifts - this register-allocation difference, not the source shape, is the last residual of that function. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 schedules the two independent chains of an indexed address (base addiu vs index scale) in a fixed order that is the opposite of retail: for 'base + index*stride' retail emits the base addiu into a fresh register first and scales the index in place (sll v1,v1,2 / addiu v0,a0,OFF / addu v0,v0,v1), while 3.0.1 always emits the longer chain (the index scale) first and updates the base in place on the parameter (addiu a0,a0,OFF / sll v0,v1,2 / addu v0,a0,v0). Reproduced in CollSet.c fn_xCollSetGetColliderReal (64/64 bytes, 3 rows differ) and fn_vCollSetSetColliderVector (76/76, base-vs-index order inverted); retail also leaves the bnez delay slot as nop where 3.0.1 fills it with the base addiu (fn_stCollSetGetColliderActor 64/68). ~30 source variants (char* byte offsets, pointer locals, comma expressions, dead scaled-index assignment, explicit *stride, subscript forms, operand-order flips) plus pragmas schedule/peephole/strength_reduction/optimization_level 2/3/4 all give the same 3.0.1 schedule, so this is a compiler-build difference, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 allocates callee-saved registers for function locals in DECLARATION order, and the retail's own order can be recovered from the target's register usage: in CollSet.c fn_vCorrectAllActivationLists the four warning flags live in s0..s3 in the order the activation lists are visited (hZdd, hZde, hZdm, hZdr - note the collset layout puts hZdmActivationList at +0x18 and hZdrActivationList at +0x1c) while the collset pointer takes s4, so the declarations must be ordered bWarningZdd, bWarningZde, bWarningZdm, bWarningZdr, hCollSet, lNumberOfState; declaring hCollSet first gives it s0 and shifts every flag by one register. The extracted debug local list is the reverse of the original declaration order (bWarningZdr, bWarningZdm, bWarningZde, bWarningZdd). (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] Retail constant-initializer values can be recovered from the target bytes, not guessed: CAM_fn_vLoadCameraConstants (CollSet.c) stores 0x3f490fdb (0.7853982f) into +0x10, +0x14 AND +0x2c, and 0x40800000 (4.0f) into +0x64 AND +0x6c, so xBaseAngleComputePosFailure is 0.7853982f (not pi/8) and xDynSpeed_TargetMulPerso is 4.0f (not 1.0f); the previous source's symmetric guesses gave a 424/424-byte body with the wrong stored values. The target's line map also gives the original statement order: xBaseCutAngle (+0x70, line 82) is assigned right after xBaseAngleComputePosFailure (line 81), not at the end of the list, which is what makes the compiler emit the +0x70 store early. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 attributes the whole body of an auto-inlined callee to the CALL SITE's source line, and the callee must be defined before the caller (and after the '#pragma auto_inline on'): recPS2glLightfv/recPS2glLightiv in PS2GL_ListsRecPly.c match only with the callee moved above the caller and scoped pragma, and the inlined body then occupies a single line in the caller's line map (retail lines 2041/2972 etc.). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: a switch nested inside a switch case merges the inner break with the outer break into one branch to the function exit; writing the inner switch's last case without a break (falling out of the inner switch) and using 'return;' instead of 'break;' for the outer case keeps the two branches separate and reproduces the retail (recPS2glLightiv, PS2GL_ListsRecPly.c). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL tagListData.pSDLIndices is a pointer, not the extractor's int: declaring it 'int*' and indexing it directly (pListData->pSDLIndices[size]) reproduces the retail's load order (size, size+1, store, size*4, base, address) in plyPS2glDrawArrays and recPS2glDrawArrays; the '((int*)pListData->pSDLIndices)[i]' cast form loads the base too early. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL recorder param structs whose first member is a 16-byte-aligned quad (anon_80 for DrawArrays) get their node size from sizeof(): the retail allocates 0x30 bytes, which is sizeof({quad vObjectBS; u32 mode; int first; int count; ptr pBuf; int packageCount;}) rounded up to the quad's 16-byte alignment; the extractor's plain float vObjectBS[4] gives 0x24 and the wrong node size. (2026-10-20)
