- [source-layout] PS2GL mask constant: the display-list/static-list bits are tested with 0x1000800 (materialised as lui 0x100 + ori 0x800), not 0x100800; the same constant appears in plyPS2glBegin and plyPS2glDrawArrays. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] SND_R3 usnd namespace and reference parameters: the retail PostponedPlay manglings require usnd::CUUID, usnd::CResData and usnd::CEventResData (mangled Q24usnd...), so the file's classes must live in 'namespace usnd' (per-file 'typedef usnd::X X;' + 'struct usnd::X {...}'), otherwise every symbol misses the 'Q24usnd' qualifier and the verifier reports OBJECT_ERROR. The retail's 'usnd::CIdObjPtr<T>' is a template whose const member '__rf' mangles to '__rf__Q24usnd27CIdObjPtr<Q24usnd8CResData>CFv' (the trailing C comes from the const qualifier - a non-const member mangles without it). The retail's fs_bExecute and fs_bPlay take their UUID/CEventResData arguments by const REFERENCE (mangling 'RCQ24usnd5CUUID', 'RCQ24usnd13CEventResData') even though the extracted debug record says pointer. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 short-loop padding differs per build: the retail PostponedPlay::fs_bIsPending list walk is rotated into a jump-to-test loop (b to the test, the initialisation in the branch delay slot) and its loop body is padded with THREE nops before the beqz (the loop body block is 16 bytes). MWCC 3.0.1/3.0 emit ONE such nop (48 bytes vs the retail's 56) and 3.0.3 emits TWO; the 3.0b38/3.0b50/3.0b52 builds instead hoist the null test out of the loop ('sltu/xori/bnez' early exit). The natural source 'for (e = ms_pFirst; e && e->m_pEActor != pEActor; e = e->m_pNext);' reproduces every instruction of the retail except the two extra padding nops, so this residual is a compiler-build loop-padding difference, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 forward a store to a global into a later load of the same global, while the retail reloads it: PostponedPlay::fs_bPlay stores the new list head ('sw s1,ms_pFirst' in the bnez delay slot) and then reloads it ('lw v0,ms_pFirst; sw v0,ms_pLast'), but MWCC propagates the stored register and emits only 'sw s1,ms_pLast' (300 vs 304 bytes). Moving the store inside 'if (!ms_pLast)' makes MWCC emit the reload but then sinks the store past the block, so neither spelling reproduces the retail - a compiler-build store-to-load forwarding difference. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 materialises an equality comparison as a value ('xor rd,rs,rt; sltiu rd,rd,1') when the boolean must exist (the left operand of &&), but uses a direct 'bne/beq' when the comparison is only a branch condition. In PostponedPlay::fs_bExecute the retail compares two CUUIDs word by word with the xor/sltiu idiom ('lw v1,0(s3); lw v0,4(v0); xor v0,v1,v0; sltiu v0,v0,1; beqz v0,...'), so the source must force the value form: an inlined 'bool operator==(const CUUID&, const CUUID&)' in namespace usnd, called as 'roId == e->m_pData->m_pTargetRes.__rf()->m_oId' (operands in the retail's order: the first operand lands in v1, the second in v0), reproduces it exactly (204/204 bytes, line map ok). (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] MWCC debug definition records omit unused leading parameters: MC_AskForOverwrite and MC_AskForOverwriteSettings in mc_menu.c recover with no parameters at all, yet the retail callers pass a0 (a char* buffer) and the retail bodies reserve a 0x10 parameter home slot (frame 0x30 instead of 0x20). Declaring one unused 'char*' parameter reproduced the exact frame and matched both functions byte-for-byte (112/112, lines ok). Check the callers' argument setup before trusting a zero-parameter recovery. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The retail's anonymous string labels in mc_menu.c are elements of the global 'char MC_Strings[32][768]' (base 0x37CB60, stride 0x300): D_37CE60=MC_Strings[1], D_37D460=MC_Strings[3], D_37EF60=MC_Strings[12], D_37F260=MC_Strings[13], D_37FE60=MC_Strings[17], D_380760=MC_Strings[20], D_381060=MC_Strings[23]. Naming MC_Strings[n] in the source reproduces the retail's %hi/%lo(MC_Strings+offset) relocations exactly; the label names themselves are spimdisasm placeholders that the retail ELF does not contain. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Loop conditions that assign inside the test reproduce the retail's raw-register test: 'while (!(Button = MC_XorTriangleButtonsPressed()))' emits the call, the copy to the saved register and 'beqz v0' on the call result, whereas 'while (!Button)' tests the saved register (beqz s0). Matched mc_menu.c MC_PrintMessageAndWaitForYesNo and MC_PrintMessageAndWaitForYesNoMC with the assignment-in-condition form. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Unresolved residual family (compiler-build difference): the retail SVG level-0 build keeps a dead store of a zero-initialised register variable that every available MWCC build (3.0-011126, 3.0.1-020123, 3.0.3-020716, 3.0b38-030307, 3.0.1b44, 3.0b50, 2.4) folds into the later constant/call-result store. Retail sites: mc_menu.c MC_HasEnoughtSpace ('daddu s0,zero,zero' at +0x0c then 'li s0,0xdf') and MC_SaveGameAlreadyExist ('daddu s0,zero,zero' at +0x10 then 'daddu s0,v0,zero'). '#pragma peephole off' keeps the store but duplicates the constant argument moves, and every source shape that keeps the store (conditional assignment) adds a branch. The pattern occurs only twice in the whole retail ELF, so it is not reproducible from C with the available compilers. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MEC engine flag tests use the ternary idiom '(flags & mask) ? 1 : 0' (or '? 0 : 1'). MWCC 3.0.1 encodes '? 1 : 0' as 'addiu rd,1; and rt,flags,mask; movz rd,zero,rt' (rd = 1 iff the mask is set) and '? 0 : 1' as 'movz rd,one,rt' with rd pre-zeroed in the prologue (rd = 1 iff the mask is CLEAR). Both forms are needed to reproduce retail MECOpt.c code; a plain 'if (flags & mask)' compiles to a bare beqz. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Reading MIPS asm: the instruction in a jal's delay slot executes BEFORE the call, so it still observes the PREVIOUS call's return value in f0. Retail MECOpt.c relies on this (e.g. 'jal conv; delay: mov.s f20,f0' captures the result of the earlier call). Misreading the delay slot as 'after the call' makes reconstructed sources look like they shift every value by one. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 has no C form for R5900 min.s/max.s (a float ternary compiles to c.lt.s + branch). The project idiom is an inline asm helper ('inline float MTH_M_xMin(float a, float b) { float r; asm { min.s r, a, b } return r; }', already used in Proj.c and COL/IntersEd.c). The retail register allocation coalesces the result with the FIRST asm operand; MWCC 3.0.1 coalesces with the SECOND, so swapping the operands inside the helper (asm { min.s r, b, a } and calling it as (newValue, oldValue)) reproduces retail register choices. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Concrete instance of the SVG non-prototype rule: in mc_menu.c, 'extern char* strcpy();' makes MC_SaveGameAlreadyExist set up a0 (the address of the local buffer) before reloading a1 (the spilled parameter) from the stack, exactly as the retail does; with a full prototype 'char* strcpy(char*, const char*)' MWCC reloads a1 first and the two calls differ by one instruction. The same non-prototype form also keeps MC_CheckStrings matching. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail MECOpt.c copies a vector into a local with int words and then restores it ('*(int*)&d.xX = *(int*)&s.xX'). In MWCC 3.0.1 the int* cast materializes the local address (addiu reg,sp,off) and makes the store opaque, so the compiler reloads the source afterwards and cannot hoist the three mul.s of MEC_fn_vCancelTilt; retail instead stores the matrix translation with a stack reference (sw v0,0x60(sp)), which keeps the load hoisted. A local whose fields are int (or a union with an int view) keeps the stack-reference addressing. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0.1-020123 inserts one extra nop after an inline asm block that is followed by a separate statement (3.0.3-020716 does not). MEC_fn_vPositionLimit matches 372/372 under 3.0.3-020716 and is 384/372 under 3.0.1-020123 for that reason only; MECOpt.c would qualify for config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt (its four recorded matches still match under 3.0.3). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 statement-level asm: the scheduler moves an asm instruction whose operands are C variables, but keeps it in place when the operand is spelled as the hardware register. In overlib.c's PS2_dl_cosf, 'abs.s x0, val' (the C parameter) was hoisted to +0x64, above the callee-saved FPR saves and the last constant mtc1s, while 'abs.s x0, $f12' stayed at +0x70 exactly where retail has it; the same applies to PS2_dl_sinf's 'sub.s x0, $f12, x0'. Naming the register also stops MWCC from reordering the rest of the block. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2: an inline asm block whose first item and last item are both '.set' directives is emitted as an unschedulable unit, which pins the block and the interleaved callee-saved FPR saves in place. '.set noreorder' ... '.set reorder' (also '.set nomacro'/'.set macro' and '.set noat'/'.set at') reproduced overlib.c's PS2_dl_cosf and PS2_dl_sinf byte-for-byte (204/204, 220/220) with matching line maps; a single trailing '.set' or two leading ones changes nothing, and '.set noreorder' alone also changes nothing. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers 'x ? 0 : 1' with an unsigned-char x into the retail conditional-move form 'move a1,zero; li v1,1; movz a1,v1,a0', while the equivalent 'if (x == 0) y = 1;' becomes a branchy 'andi v1,a0,0xff; bnez' sequence (4 extra bytes). A retail movz/movn on a parameter is therefore direct evidence that the original source used a ternary, and that the parameter was not compared with '== 0'. Reproduced in GLI_xSetViewportLightsOnOff (Light.c, 80/80 MATCH). (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] An 'unsigned short' debug parameter that the retail copies into a 32-bit register without masking must be declared 32-bit (unsigned int): MWCC 3.0.1 emits 'andi s0,a1,0xffff' when copying an unsigned-short parameter into a saved register, which adds 4 bytes the retail does not have. Reproduced in GLI_vRemoveLightForInit / GLI_vStoreLightForInit (Light.c, 180/180 MATCH) where the extracted 'unsigned short eUserFlags' had to become 'unsigned int'. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The MWCC debug definition record for GLI_vAddInternalLightToViewport lists three parameters, but the target ABI also uses a3, so a fourth (omitted leading, unused) pointer parameter has to be restored: a0 unused, a1 = ambient colour, a2 = parallel direction, a3 = parallel colour. The retail caller (SDLLights.c) passes a0=context, a1/a2/a3 = &stIL_AmbientColor/+0x64/+0x70, which confirms the mapping. Treat a recovered parameter list that leaves the last argument register (a3/t0) unused as an omitted-parameter case. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] No MWCC PS2 build (3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38) emits the retail's 'addiu reg,reg,0' add-zero inside a multi-term array address (base + const + index*scale). The retail ELF has 187 of them (usnd_vLeaveCritSect +0x18, usnd_vEnterCritSect +0x38, usnd_vKillTimer +0x48, usnd_uiSetTimer +0x4c and +0xb0, IPT_Pad, INO FFB); none of the 3495 recorded matches contains one, and no source spelling reproduces it (pointer local, char* arithmetic, 2D array view, struct member at offset 0, dead-index idiom, static arrays). Treat it as a retail compiler-build difference that blocks any function using it. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] R5900 accumulator semantics (verified with a controlled MWCC probe): 'mula.s ft, fs' sets the accumulator to ft*fs, and 'madd.s fd, fs, ft' computes fd = ACC + fs*ft (fd is only a destination). MWCC contracts a plain C 'A*B + C*D' into mula.s/madd.s with the first product's operands as fs=first, ft=second and the second product's as fs=second, ft=first, so the operand order of the C source is directly readable from the retail instruction operands (jparticle.c: 'p1->UVs[0] * 1.5564321f + V * 3.4659879f' gives mula.s fK,fU + madd.s fK,fK,fV). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 rotates a while/for loop whose test is a plain register into 'guard + bottom test' (beqz before the body); a load-based test keeps the 'jump to test' form (b to the test). The retail usnd_uiTimerHandler enters its timer-list scan with an unconditional 'b' to the test (a register test), which no while/for spelling produces: spelling the rotation out with goto + labels reproduces it byte for byte (156/156, also the early 'li v0,1' hoist and the branch-target nop). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] '(int)(unsigned int)&_gp' compiles to 'addiu rd,$gp,0' under MWCC PS2 3.0.1, which is exactly how the retail passes the gp register to CreateThread (usnd_uiInitTimers +0xb0, ps2Threads.s CreateTransitionThread). Reading the value instead ('(int)_gp') would be a memory load, so use the address of _gp. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 accepts the full VU0 instruction set inside a statement-level inline asm block, not just lqc2/sqc2: verified vsubx.w, vsubz.z, vopmula.xyz ACC, vopmsub.xyz, vmul.xyz, vaddy.x, vaddz.x, vrsqrt Q, vwaitq, vmulq.xyz, vmulx.xy/xyz/xyzw, vmuly.xyz/xyzw, vadd.xyz/xyzw with C pointer and float operands (jparticle.c face-normal and lerp blocks). The block is emitted verbatim in source order, except that a trailing sqc2 may be pulled into a following jr delay slot. Raw GPR names (t2, a3, ...) are also accepted as operands, but their values are then unmanaged by the compiler. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC numbers function-local statics per compilation, so a candidate buffer can collide with an unrelated retail symbol of the same base name (our sndeethrd.cpp 16 KB thread stack came out as 'stack$41', the spelling of a 4 KB buffer in another unit, and the verifier then bound it to the wrong address). Declaring two unused statics before it moves it to 'stack$43' and the verifier's address-based local-static pairing resolves it to the retail 'stack$221'. Check the emitted name against config/symbols.txt before trusting a static's relocation. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The retail symbols of one SND_R3 unit mix C and C++ linkage: usnd_vLeaveCritSect, usnd_vEnterCritSect, usnd_vKillTimer, usnd_uiSetTimer, usnd_uiInitTimers, usnd_uiTryToEnterCritSect have no ABI suffix (they are extern "C" in the retail) while usnd_uiTimerHandler__Fi, fs_vCleanTheString__FPc and usnd_vTimerLoop__FPv are mangled C++ names. Declaring the unmangled ones 'extern "C"' makes the candidate symbol and its .line_ section match the retail exactly (and lets the verifier check the line map); the mangled ones must keep their plain C++ spelling (and the extractor name 'fs_vCleanTheString__FPc' has to be shortened to 'fs_vCleanTheString'). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail VU blocks are identified by one .line entry per VU instruction (jparticle.c lines 185-202 and 122-145), while the surrounding C statements share single lines (e.g. the J_GetParticleSpriteFromInterpolatedPoints lines 100/101/102/107 interleave instruction-for-instruction in the code). That pattern shows the original source mixed C statements with hand-written asm blocks whose instructions were written one per source line, so the asm block must be reproduced verbatim and the C statements kept as separate lines. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 lower a 64-bit product of two 32-bit values with a __muldi3 call plus dsll32/dsrl32 widening, while the retail uses the R5900 mult/multu/mfhi high-product form (usnd_uiSetTimer +0x4c..+0x6c). The 32x32 'mult' of the first operand is reproduced, but the 64-bit part needs the retail compiler build. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail GLD viewport-array access (DevVpt.c) computes the element address explicitly: addiu base,0x60; addu base,idx*4; lw 0(base). MWCC 3.0.1 folds the member offset into the load displacement, so the source must spell it as ((GLD_stViewport**)&pstDev->pstViewports[0])[idx]; holding the index in a separate int local and applying the (short) cast at the subscript (not to the modulo expression) reproduces retail's exact register allocation and the shift-16 widening at the join. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail GLD 16-slot search loops keep the index sign-extended in a 64-bit register: a 32-bit addiu followed by dsll32/dsra32 re-widen after every increment, and the same widening before the slti compare. No available build (3.0-011126, 3.0.1-020123, 3.0.3-020716, 3.0b38-030307) reproduces it: an int counter emits no widening and a long counter emits daddiu. Treat it as a residual of the retail compiler build, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 chooses the addu operand order of an indexed array element from the source form: 'dev->arr[i].field' emits 'addu rd,index,base' (index first) while 'T* p = &dev->arr[0]; p[i].field' emits 'addu rd,base,index' (base first). The retail FFB.c code (INO_fn_vStopAllFFBEffectForDevice +0x74, INO_fn_vStopPreCreatedFFBEffect +0x7c, INO_fn_vDeleteAllPreCreatedConstantFFBEffectForDevice +0x5c) uses the base-first form, so the array-pointer spelling reproduces the operand order (verified by compiling both forms). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 emits every class vtable with a 2-word null prefix and the object's vptr points at the start of that prefix, so the first virtual declared in a class is reached with 'lw t9, 0(obj); lw t9, 8(t9)' (vptr[2]) and the second with 'lw t9, 0xC(t9)'. Verified on SND_R3 sndvolume.cpp: usnd::CGlobalVarAccess with fv_poGetVar as its first virtual and fv_poCreateVar as its second reproduces the retail's vptr[2]/vptr[3] call sites (impPS2glActiveTextureARB's virtual slot 3 = fv_poCreateVar), while giving the class a base class or extra pure virtuals shifts the slots by one entry per added virtual. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] Several extracted GLD_* signatures in DevVpt.c omit a leading unused ABI parameter: the retail passes the viewport handle in a1 (not a0) and the later arguments in a2/a3, so GLD_bRequestWriteToViewport and GLD_bSetViewportPosition only match with a leading int parameter (the same shape as GLD_bGetViewportAttributes/GLD_bWriteToViewportFinished). GLD_vCloseErr is a function, not a function-pointer variable: the retail takes its address with lui/addiu, so it must be declared 'extern void GLD_vCloseErr(void);' and passed as &GLD_vCloseErr. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] tools/match.py try replaces only the function body, never the signature: it extracts the body of each variant and splices it into the canonical source. A signature change (e.g. adding a leading unused parameter) has to be made in src/ directly before testing, otherwise every variant is compiled with the old ABI. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 allocates a callee-saved register by live range, and a saved register is reused as soon as its previous holder dies: in SND_R3 sndvolume.cpp fn_bSetVolumeModifier the retail keeps the buffer pointer in s0 until it is copied to a1 for the virtual call and then reuses s0 for the unsigned-char result (b_Answer). The source form that reproduces it is to put the result initialisation BETWEEN the two calls (fs_roGetInst then the virtual call), so the assignment is scheduled into the jalr delay slot after the buffer's last use; writing 'b_Answer = 0;' before the first call forces a third saved register and a bigger frame. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail FFB.c keeps every guarded block out of line ('bnez cond,body' with an explicit 'b <shared epilogue>' for the else path) and keeps the effect-number range test separate from the preceding '!= -1' test (slti v1 for '< 20'); MWCC 3.0.1 inlines those blocks and merges the range into 'slti $at' ('<= 19'), so each FFB candidate loses 3-5 instructions. The retail's duplicated range test in INO_fn_vStopFFBEffect (the same test twice, once out of line and once inline) confirms the original source really had two separate tests. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] INO_fn_vDeletePreCreatedConstantFFBEffect's retail code materialises a separate absolute base per FFB member (D_7D2D6C..D_7D2D88, the eight field addresses of the first precreated element) and adds the runtime offsets (wDev*0x33c and effect*0x24) to each, while MWCC 3.0.1 emits the same per-field bases but reloads hDev->m_wPort and re-multiplies for every field (312 bytes either way). A local wDev makes MWCC fold the member offsets into store displacements instead (256 bytes), so the retail kept the device offset in a register across the eight stores: the original source read hDev->m_wPort once. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 with the game baseline (-inline deferred) inlines a 'static inline' helper into its caller, and the inlined copy keeps its own basic-block structure: the caller's if-merge point stays a separate block, so a branch that tests a call result targets the merge block instead of the preceding loop-exit block. In SND_R3 sndvolume.cpp fn_s32GetVolumeModifier the table search had to be a 'static inline' helper (the retail's line map puts the whole search on one source line, i.e. a macro or an inlined helper); with the search written inline the beqz of the null check branched to the loop-exit label instead of the return block (188/188 bytes but a 2-byte branch-offset difference). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail SND_R3 sources use a compact one-statement-per-line style that MWCC's line table records: in sndvolume.cpp the retail's fn_bGetVolumeLineModifierString puts the result test, the if, the sprintf call and the return on ONE source line, and fn_bSetVolumeModifier puts both clamp tests and the f_vSetValue call on one line. Writing those statements on a single source line reproduces the retail's line-offset map exactly (the check only compares the code offsets where the line changes). Note: tools/match.py looks up the candidate line section as '.line_<retail name>' while MWCC names it '.line_<name><parameter signature>' (e.g. .line_fn_bSetVolumeModifier__FPCci__FPci), so the verifier reports lines=different for every C++ function that takes parameters even when the offsets match exactly. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] jparticle.c: the extractor typed the global J_g_pNoiseTable as int, but the retail ELF symbol is 4 bytes and the target loads it with 'lw v1, J_g_pNoiseTable(gp)' (it holds a float* to the 2052-entry noise table). Declaring it float* gives the same relocation and the correct address arithmetic; J_g_NoiseLevel (also 4 bytes) is the float written next to it with swc1. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail playevts.c copies MTH vectors with a word view: 'typedef union { MTH3D_tdstVector_ v; int i[3]; } UV3;' plus 'int *pPos = (int *)&src; dst.i[0] = pPos[0]; ...'. The union's int-array member keeps the destination offset folded into the store displacement (sw v1,0x60(sp)), while the int-cast idiom '*(int*)&local.field = ...' makes MWCC materialise the field address instead (addiu reg,sp,0x60 / sw reg,0(reg)) - the union view is required for the retail's direct-offset copies. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 convert (int)float in place (cvt.w.s $f0,$f0) while the retail playevts.c converts the 3d data's xCurrentFrame with trunc.w.s into the callee-saved $f30 (fn_vPlayLastEventsBeforeRestart, fn_vPlayEventsSinceLastOneNoLoop, fn_vDesInitAllEvents all do). That forces a 0x90 frame instead of 0x80 and shifts every stack offset, so the candidate is 676/680 bytes with all instructions but the frame identical; no natural spelling (short cast, int temp, float local) reproduced the f30 scratch. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 rejects aggregate members as symbolic inline-asm operands, and a union's float array member is rejected as well: 'asm { mula.s stDiff.a_x[0], stDiff.a_x[0] }' fails with 'illegal operand'. The MTH_SQR3_TO/MTH_SQRT_TO macros therefore need plain scalar float locals; the retail's 'madd.s f0,f0,f0' (result and z in one register) only appears when the z operand is assigned from a memory read the compiler forwards from the register that already holds the value (xZ = stDiff.v.xZ after the subtraction), so the assignment order of the three scalars decides the register binding. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail game code strength-reduces only small strides: a 0x80-byte element array keeps its index scaled (sll idx,7) and the address is recomputed as base+idx*128 every iteration, while a 4-byte element array gets a byte induction variable (addiu off,4). MWCC 3.0.1 strength-reduces both, producing an extra register and a different size. Scoped '#pragma push / #pragma opt_strength_reduction off / ... / #pragma pop' around the function restores the unscaled index (verified on fn_vKillAllAlwaysSDLs: 144/144 MATCH) but then also removes the small-stride IV, so a function needing both cannot be reproduced with one pragma. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Always.c's g_stAlways (retail 0x346150, 28 bytes) holds the always list: +0x00 ulMaxNumberOfAlways, +0x04 ALW_h_LstAlwaysModel.hFirstElementDyn, +0x10 a pointer to an array of 0x80-byte HIE_tdstSuperObject_ elements (the always list itself) and +0x18 a pointer to an array of HIE_tdstSuperObject_* generators. The elements are HIE_tdstSuperObject_ (sizeof 0x80): +0x04 hLinkedObject.p_stCharacter (the engine object), +0x14 hNextBrotherDyn, +0x30 ulFlags. The generator index is the personal type minus 0x10000 (the always personal-type base). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail game flag tests are written as (unsigned char)(ulFlags & 0x20): MWCC then emits 'andi v1,v1,0x20' followed by 'andi v1,v1,0xFF' (the 8-bit normalisation of the masked value) before the branch, while a plain 'ulFlags & 0x20' loses the second andi. Seen in Always.c fn_vKillAllAlwaysSDLs/fn_vKillAllAlways/fn_vKillAlwaysByPersonalType and in fn_vKillAllAlwaysInNonActiveSectors. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0.1 duplicates a pointer comparison as 'xor rd,rs,rt; sltiu rd,rd,1' only when the compared pointer is a variable it cannot fold to a constant; when the same source yields NULL the value is folded into the xor operands ('xor rd,zero,rt') and the 8-bit cast of the boolean disappears. In Always.c fn_ucIsAlwaysGeneratedByMe retail materialises the NULL into v0 ('move v0,zero' then 'xor v0,v0,a1'), so the compared value must be a variable whose NULL-ness the compiler cannot prove. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 constant-propagates a local that is only ever assigned 0, so an 'if (... && !flag)' test on such a flag disappears (and the flag's callee-saved register with it). A function-scoped '#pragma push / #pragma opt_propagation off / #pragma pop' keeps the test and restores retail's register count and frame size (readMpeg's isPaused flag: 700 -> 728 bytes, exact size and relocations). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 emits 'andi' for 'x & 0x800' but 'li reg,0x800' + 'and' when the mask is a local variable whose constant value the compiler still knows (e.g. with opt_propagation off, which keeps the variable out of the immediate). The retail code in readMpeg uses exactly the li+and form, so the original source keeps the pad mask in a variable rather than as a literal. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 line tables hold one entry per source line, so two statements written on the same physical line share a single entry (and a statement's code inherits the previous line when the compiler schedules it into the earlier block). Matching retail's line offsets for defMain and movie required putting both statements of a block on one source line. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 uses 'li'+'and' (not 'andi') for an AND whose mask does not fit in 16 unsigned bits, e.g. '& ~0x3F' (0xFFFFFFC0) or '& ~0x7FF' (0xFFFFF800); masks up to 0xFFFF still use andi, and 0x8000 does too (andi 0x8000). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 can schedule a call's result store into the *following* call's delay slot, so 'p[4] = p[5] = malloc(x)' and 'p[4] = malloc(x); p[5] = malloc(y)' look alike in the disassembly: the value stored in a delay slot is the *previous* call's result. Verified on fn_vAllocBuffers64 (retail stores the 0x8003F buffer into p[4] and the 0x403F buffer into p[5]). (2026-10-20)

- [relocation] Taking the address of an *undefined* extern symbol compiles to 'addiu reg, %lo(sym)(%gp)' (a gp-relative address with addend 0), while the same expression on a symbol defined in the TU gets a non-zero addend. This reproduces retail's 'addiu v1, , 0x0' for a thread's gpReg field ('(int)&_gp' with 'extern int _gp;'). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Recurring residual: our MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3 fill a conditional branch's delay slot with a register copy hoisted from a later block, where retail keeps the block's own next instruction in the slot (e.g. readMpeg's 'blez' slot: ours 'daddu a0,s0', retail 'ori a2,0x8000'). The extra copy also shifts every later line-table entry by four bytes. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3: a masked-bit test written as the ternary 'if ((x & MASK) ? 1 : 0)' produces the retail boolean idiom 'li v1,1 / andi / movz v1,zero,v / beqz v1', while a plain 'if (x & MASK)' folds to 'andi / beqz' and a plain 'if ((x & MASK) != 0)' folds as well. Reproduced in DNMDynam.c DNM_fn_vDynamicsCopyClone and DNM_fn_vInitDynamic (17 flag tests) and in the flag-copy blocks of MECFn_vInsertLightInSectorList. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2: a macro that computes a value into a block-local and then assigns it to the field twice (e.g. { unsigned int v = ...; *p = v; *p = v; }) reproduces the retail's redundant store, including the extra store at an if/else join block (MWCC does not remove the second store). Used for DNM_fn_vInitDynamic's 17 id-card flag copies and its three ulEndFlags writes (each expands to load/op/store/store). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] DNMDynam.c (Rayman3 MEC) only matches under MWCC 3.0.3-020716: DNM_fn_vInitDynamic is byte-identical (1316/1316, lines ok) with 3.0.3 but 1232/1316 with 3.0.1 (the difference is the scheduling/allocation of the ternary boolean and the 'li 1' preset). All four other functions in the file match under both builds, so the file belongs on config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 inline asm: symbolic FP operands must be plain scalar float variables - a struct field of a local (local.xY) is accepted, but a field reached through a pointer (v->xY) or a union member (u.stVector.xY) is rejected with 'illegal operand'. The retail's MTH_SQR3_TO idiom materialises the operand addresses (addiu v1,sp,OFF; lwc1 fv0,0(v1)) and the R5900 accumulator barrier 'c1 0x44'+nop follows the madd.s when the madd.s result shares a register with a later write (e.g. 'mtc1 zero,f0'). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Rayman3 MTH vector copies in retail code are word-wise (lw/sw), not lwc1/swc1: a union view with an unsigned int[3] member copied through ((U*)&dst)->auWords[i] = ((U*)src)->auWords[i] reproduces the retail's direct stack offsets, while a plain float component assignment gives lwc1/swc1 and the bare int-lvalue idiom *(int*)&dst.xY = *(int*)&src.xY makes MWCC materialise the destination addresses (extra addiu/saved registers). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 drops an unsigned '<=' (or '>') range test when the same value is also compared with '>=' in the same && chain: 'ptr >= lo && ptr <= hi' keeps the first test and loses the second one entirely (the branch disappears), while the same '<=' alone is kept. Writing the range test as a failure-first else-if chain ('if (ptr < lo) {} else if (hi < ptr) {} else {found}') keeps both '<' tests and reproduces the retail 'sltu at,end,ptr / bnez' form. Reproduced on cpammg.c Mmg_fn_vWhereIs (retail 384/384). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1's C parser rejects a cast of a parenthesised expression: '(unsigned int)(p->field)' fails with "')' expected", and '(unsigned int)p->field' silently parses as a cast of the *pointer* p (the member offset is then added to the cast pointer). Copy the field into a plain local first when a value cast is needed. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 with the game baseline -O3,p unrolls a for/while loop over an unsigned-char index by eight (main loop plus a scalar remainder), while the retail code keeps the loop rolled. A scoped '#pragma opt_unroll_loops off' around the function reproduces the rolled retail loop together with its jump-to-test entry (the loop is rotated: the entry branches to the test). Reproduced on cpammg.c Mmg_fn_v_InitMmg (retail 156/156). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The retail MMG module keeps the last searched module/block pair in function-local statics (retail symbols lastModuleId$580 / lastBlockId$581). Declaring them as 'static unsigned char' locals inside the function gives MWCC the same base names, so the matcher can pair the gp-relative relocations (a file-scope static would be named without the $N suffix and stays unresolved). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2: 'x & ~4' is NOT the same as 'x & ~3' - ~4 is 0xFFFFFFFB (clears bits 0-2) while ~3 is 0xFFFFFFFC (clears bits 0-1). The retail's round-up-to-4 mask is 'li -4' (0xFFFFFFFC), so the source must write '& ~3' (or 0xFFFFFFFC); writing '& ~4' makes MWCC emit 'li -5' and a different value. Reproduced in PS2GL_Lists.c PS2glListAllocNode, where the retail computes the aligned node size as (cbParams+8+3) & ~3. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] PS2GL_Lists.c needs the UNIX-style '-O3,p' flag (config/library-O3p-sources.txt): with it PS2glSearchList and PS2glMergeBB match byte-for-byte including the line map, because the retail has alignment nops at in-loop branch targets (4 mod 8) and does not sink a join block's first instruction into a conditional branch's delay slot. Caveat: -O3,p over-pads for this file - impPS2glPartialDeleteLists then gains a 4-byte nop at +0x44 that the retail does not have, so that recorded match needs a source tweak before the file can move to the -O3,p list. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL tagGLcontext: the two fields the extractor types as int at +0x5cf4 (pCurListVCode) and +0x5cf8 (pCurListTexUnit) are pointers in the retail - PS2glSaveListCall advances them by 1 and 4 bytes and stores a byte and an int through them (the list's vertex-code array and the per-call texture-unit array). Retyping them to 'unsigned char*' and 'int*' in a local copy of the struct is required to match PS2glSaveListCall/PS2glSaveListCallMultiTex. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] PS2GL: the global 'PS2glAllocator' is a function POINTER variable (the retail loads it with 'lw v0,PS2glAllocator(gp)' and calls it with jalr), but the extractor types it as int in every PS2GL file. Declaring it as 'void* (*PS2glAllocator)(int);' is required to match PS2glListSaveParamSpace/PS2glListAllocNode; the same applies to PS2glAllocator64. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (game baseline): an in-class template member is emitted as an out-of-line weak copy when the call site is nested too deeply for the inliner (function -> smap ctor -> stree ctor -> _Init -> _BuyNode = 4 levels), while the same member inlined one level lower stays inline. Reproducing the retail's '_Buynode__Q24usnd215stree<i,Q24usnd32spair<Ci,P19CSoundSemaphoreInfo>,Q34usnd90smap<i,P19CSoundSemaphoreInfo,Q24usnd7less<i>,Q24usnd34sallocator<P19CSoundSemaphoreInfo>>4_Kfn,Q24usnd7less<i>,Q24usnd34sallocator<P19CSoundSemaphoreInfo>>FPQ34usnd9streebase9sTreeLinkQ34usnd9streebase6_Redbl' needs only a function-local object of the matching smap instantiation - the mangled name then matches byte-for-byte, line map included (60/60). (2026-10-20)

- [abi] MWCC PS2 (C++) mangling of the usnd/Dinkumware containers omits the return type: a member function is mangled '<name>__<class>__F<params>' with 'C' in front for a const member and 'v' for 'no parameters'. A 4-byte struct return (the tree iterator) uses the o32 hidden-pointer convention - a0 is the return slot and 'this' moves to a1 - which is why the retail's 'end__Q24usnd215stree<...>Fv' takes two register arguments and its body is 'lw v1,0(a1); daddu v0,a0,zero; sw v1,0(a0)', and why 'lower_bound__Q24usnd215stree<...>FRCi' forwards (iterator& out, const stree& tree, const K& key) as a0/a1/a2. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] SND_R3 usnd containers: MWCC emits an out-of-line weak (linkonce) copy of every in-class template member for each instantiation, and the retail linker keeps only the copies that are still referenced, so the same instantiation's members are scattered over several units (the CSoundSemaphoreInfo map's _BuyNode copy lives in sndclinktypeactorcont.cpp while end/lower_bound/_Key/_Mynode/_Erase live in the Pustl/stree.cpp unit). The DWARF record for such a copy is misattributed (the extractor names the retail's _Buynode copy 'ms_czNil__Q24usnd7sstring'), so take the function identity from the ELF symbol table, not from the debug name. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] SND_R3 usnd containers: the retail's stree::find is 'iterator oIt = lower_bound(key); return (oIt == end() || key_compare(key, _Key(oIt._Mynode()))) ? end() : oIt;'. Written that way and inlined into a caller, MWCC calls iterator_base::operator== out of line (the 'jal __eq__Q34usnd9streebase13iterator_base...' in the retail) but inlines the operator== of the stree's own iterator. The comparison's left operand ends up as an extra iterator_base copy that the retail does not have, so a caller whose source uses 'oIt != end()' still differs by one iterator_base construction. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 compiles a switch label group that merges a case with default ('case 1:' immediately followed by 'default:') into TWO blocks: the case path runs the whole body and falls through to the switch join without a break branch, while the default path re-executes the group's FIRST statement in the branch delay slot and branches to the group's TAIL (the last statement). Reproduced exactly in SoundMenu2D.c fn_vUpdateVolumeLineGameMenu2D (316/316 bytes, 18/18 relocations, line map ok): 'case 1: default: uiLineId = g_lSoundVolumeLineSound; uiSoundID = 7; break;' gives 'lw a0,..; li s0,7' for the case and 'b <li s0,7>' (with the load in the delay slot) for the default. Writing the two labels as separate groups with identical bodies instead duplicates the body and keeps a break branch (+4 bytes, wrong block layout). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The retail .line map is direct evidence for which statements shared one source line, and a single row covering a whole if/switch block usually means the original was a MACRO expansion: in SoundMenu2D.c fn_vUpdateVolumeLineGameMenu2D the row +0xa8..+0x124 (line 168) covers the 'if (uiSoundID < 11 && g_aSoundEvent[uiSoundID])' test, the 8-entry jump-table switch, the union store and the 6-argument SND_fn_s32SendRequestSound call, while the row +0x58 (line 158) covers the volume computation AND its SND_fn_vSetVolumeLineSound call. Writing those groups on one source line each reproduces the retail's line offsets exactly (lines=ok); splitting them onto separate lines adds rows and breaks the check. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 jump-table label count, second data point: a switch with only four labels ('case 5: case 6: case 7: default:') compiles to a beq compare chain ('li v0,7; beq; li v0,6; beq; li v0,5; beq'), while adding the explicit empty labels 'case 0: case 1: case 2: case 3: case 4:' in front of default (nine labels) makes MWCC emit the retail 8-entry jump table with the 'sltiu at,value,8' range check. Confirmed in SoundMenu2D.c fn_vUpdateVolumeLineGameMenu2D (inner switch on the sound id: case 5 -> g_lSoundObjectTypeVoice, case 6 -> Music, case 7 -> Anim, default -> Menu). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MS_Micro.c fn_bGetMicroPosition: the retail copies the 3-float matrix translation with integer word copies (lw/sw into the local, then lwc1/swc1 out to the output vector). Reproduce with 'typedef union { MTH3D_tdstVector_ v; int i[3]; } MSMicroVector;' and 'st.i[0] = ((int*)&h->hGlobalMatrix->stTransformMatrix.stCol_3)[0];' etc. (all three copies on ONE source line, since the target attributes all six lw/sw to a single line). A plain MTH3D_tdstVector_ assignment gives lwc1/swc1 and an extra addiu for the local address. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a float-constant store through a member pointer with the ADDRESS first ('lw v1,0x14(a0)' then 'lui a1,0x3f00' then 'sw a1,0(v1)'), while the retail ELF and MWCC 3.0.3-020716 materialise the constant first ('lui' then 'lw' then 'sw'). SDLRefs.cpp C_SdlRef_TextureMatrix::Process (0x2d7a20) is byte-exact (172/172, 4/4 relocs, line map ok) under 3.0.3 and differs only in that swapped pair under the 3.0.1 game baseline; '#pragma schedule off', an int literal, a volatile lvalue, a float* member type, an inline helper and reversed store order all keep the 3.0.1 order. The retail ELF contains both orders (3 'lui,lw,sw' and 3 'lw,lui,sw' sites), so this is a compiler-build difference in the store-operand order, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 gives a NAMED local the lower argument register and an expression temp the higher one, which is the opposite of what the retail wants when a value is both tested and passed on. SDLRefs.cpp C_SdlRef_LocalMatrixForCheap::ProcessNoInterpol (0x2d7b30) matches 116/116 with the line map when the null test reads the member ('if (m_vpDestinationPointer)') while the copy uses the named local ('PS2_M_vCopyMatrix2(pSrc, pDest, OldMat)'): the destination then lands in $a2 and &OldMat in $a1 as the retail does. Testing the local ('if (pDest)') swaps them to $a1/$a2 (MISMATCH) and the member-only form drops the load's own line-map row. The same 'test the member, use the local' shape keeps the six retail line-map rows. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] SDLRefs.cpp (GliGlou/Drivers/PS2GL/Src/SDLs) was built by a compiler between MWCC 3.0.1-020123 and 3.0.3-020716: C_SdlRef_TextureMatrix::Process needs 3.0.3 (store-operand order, byte-exact under 3.0.3), while C_SdlRef_PointLighting::Process needs 3.0.1 (its short zero-fill loop is padded with three nops before the back-branch in the retail and by 3.0.1, but with two nops by 3.0.3, giving 168/176 bytes). Every other function in the file matches under both builds, so the file cannot simply be moved to config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt (as SDLLights.cpp in the same directory was) until the PointLighting loop padding is resolved. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 if-converts 'if (c) { return 1; } return 0;' into a value (sltu/sltiu), but it does NOT convert 'if (c) { return 0; } return 1;' - the returning-0-first spelling (e.g. 'if ((x & mask) == 0) { return 0; } return 1;') keeps the branch form and reproduces the retail's separate return blocks. Matched GLI_DRV_IsMaterialTransparent in PS2GL/SendMat.c; also note that assigning 'pMat = &h->a_stMultiMaterial[0]' (address-of form) keeps the member access unfolded (addiu base,+off; lw v0,off(base)) while 'pMat = h->a_stMultiMaterial' (array decay) lets MWCC fold the member offset into the load. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 folds the array element scale into the byte induction variable when the index is a direct multiply: 'arr[6*i][0]' compiles to 'addu base,iv' plus 'addiu iv,iv,0x60' (iv = 96*i bytes). Binding the index to a local first ('idx = 6 * i; arr[idx][0] = ...') keeps the induction variable in ELEMENT units: the compiler then emits 'sll v1,idx,4 / addu p,base,v1' and increments the index IV by 6, which is exactly the retail form. Matched GLI_vInitSimpleShadowArrays (u_draw.c, 216/216 bytes, line map ok); the same file's GLI_vAddShadowObject keeps the retail's '(3*i)<<5' byte-IV form without the local. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers 'x == 1' two different ways: used as a VALUE (assigned to a local, e.g. 'bRes = f() == 1;') it emits 'xori v0,v0,1 / sltiu v0,v0,1' (the boolean 0/1), while used directly as an if-condition it emits 'li v1,1 / bne v0,v1'. The retail SND_R3 crit-sect guard (usnd_uiTryToEnterCritSect(...) == 1) uses the value form, so the source must route the comparison through a local; that also reproduces the retail's 'xori/sltiu' + 'beqz' pair. Matched SND_fn_s32SendRequestJingle and SND_fn_s32SendRequestSemaphoreSound in SND_R3/src/snd3d.cpp. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 rejects 'template void X<T>::f(...)' ('illegal explicit template instantiation') and silently ignores 'template class X<int>;' (no member is emitted), so a template member that the retail emits out-of-line can only be produced with an explicit SPECIALISATION: 'template <> void usnd::TTypedVar<int>::f_vSetValue(int oValue) { ... }' emits exactly the retail symbol 'f_vSetValue__Q24usnd12TTypedVar<i>Fi'. Matched in SND_R3/src/snd3d.cpp (8/8 bytes, line map ok). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 copies a union through the type of its FIRST member: a union whose first member is 'float' is assigned with lwc1/swc1, one whose first member is 'unsigned int' with lw/sw. SND_R3's _SND_tduRefEvt therefore needs 'float fTheme;' first to reproduce the retail's 'g_oLastSavedTheme = g_oLastTheme' (lwc1 g_oLastTheme / swc1 g_oLastSavedTheme) while the same union is still used as a pointer (g_oLastTheme.pstPtr + 0x2c) and cleared with 'g_oLastTheme.Id = 0' (sw zero). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] SND_R3: usnd::CResData and usnd::CEventResData must be declared inside namespace usnd (with a global 'typedef usnd::CResData CResData;'), otherwise the CIdObjPtr conversion operator mangles as '__opPQ24usnd8CResData__Q24usnd27CIdObjPtr<Q24usnd8CResData>CFv' only if BOTH the element type and the template live in usnd. Inside namespace usnd the global typedef must be spelled '::CIdObjPtr' (a bare 'CIdObjPtr' names the template and fails with "'<' expected"). The CIdObjPtr template and the usnd stree/smap/less/sallocator/spair templates are reproduced from SwitchMgr.cpp. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 branch polarity for a two-arm if/else follows the SOURCE spelling, and the same logic in '&&' versus '||' form gives different code: 'if (a == -1 || a == 0) { return 0; }' compiles to 'beq a,-1,EXIT / bnez a,BODY' (the second test branches to the body), while 'if (a != -1 && a != 0) { ... }' gives 'beq a,-1,EXIT / beqz a,EXIT'. SND_R3/src/snd3d.cpp SND_fn_bIsSoundPlaying (300/300, line map ok) needs the '||' form; the same file's SND_fn_bIsTypePlayingSound needs the 'if (x == -1) { return -1; }' early-out spelled so MWCC branches TO the then-block ('beq v1,-2,RET'). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 guards a memset whose size is a signed division: 'memset(p, 0, (n + 7) / 8)' compiles to 'bgez v0,SKIP' plus a duplicated 'addiu/sra' in both arms, while the same size written as '>> 3' ('(n + 7) >> 3') drops the guard and reproduces the retail's plain 'jal memset' with 'sra a2,v0,3' in the delay slot. Controlled probe: /8 -> 8 extra bytes and a guard, >>3 -> no guard, for int/unsigned/short parameter declarations alike. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 addressing a LOCAL byte array differs by spelling: 'aBuf[i]' folds the array's constant offset into the load displacement ('addu r,idx,sp' + 'lbu r,0x60(r)'), while casting the array first ('((unsigned char*)aBuf)[i]') materialises the array base in a register in the loop preheader ('addiu v0,sp,0x60') and adds the index to it ('addu r,v0,idx' + 'lbu r,0(r)'). The retail GEO_xComputeIndexUsedByElements uses the materialised-base form in all three of its loops, so the cast spelling is required (also for the memset destination, which stays 'addiu a0,sp,off'). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 '-inline deferred' only inlines a later-defined callee into an earlier caller when a '#pragma auto_inline on' precedes the callee definition; the callee still keeps its out-of-line symbol, so the retail can have both. GEO_vCreateGeometricObject (GEO/GeoObj.c) calls GEO_vInitGeometricObject and the retail shows the init body inlined into the caller (its loop and its eight field initialisations) plus a standalone GEO_vInitGeometricObject symbol; adding the pragma before the init's definition reproduces both exactly (464/464 bytes, line map ok). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 per-file compiler: PS2GL library code that lives under the game source tree (u_drawAGO2.c, GliGlou/Drivers/PS2GL/Src) is built with MWCC 3.0.3-020716, not the 3.0.1 game default: GLI_vDisplaySerialLeaves (600/600), GLI_vDisplaySerialFlow (724/724), GLI_vDisplaySerialFlowBlured (780/780) and GLI_v_CalculateVectorsFor3DRainVU0 (232/232) match only under 3.0.3 (3.0.1/3.0/3.0b38 leave one instruction in a different slot, e.g. the counter increment at +0x58 vs +0x54). The file's three pre-existing recorded matches (GLD_fn_vInitSmoothSynchro, AGO2_vDisplayWall, GLI_v_StopDrawingRain) still verify under 3.0.3, so the file satisfies the per-file 3.0.3 criterion and should be added to config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] GEO element type numbering (from the retail jump table of GEO_xGetParallelBoxIndexOfElement plus the matched GEO_fn_vAddObjectToSphere): 0 = none, 1 = IndexedTriangles (xIndexOfParallelBox at +0x2a), 2 = FaceMapDescriptors (+0xc), 3 = Sprite (+0x6), 4 = TMeshes (+0x18), 5 = Points (+0xe), 6 = Lines (+0x12), 7 = Spheres (+0x6), 8 = AlignedBoxes (+0x6), 9 = Cones (+0x6). GEO_vCreateElementCones stores 9 and GEO_vCreateElementSpheres 7, matching GEO_fn_vAddObjectToSphere's case 3 = Sprite and case 7 = Spheres. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] MWCC PS2 debug records omit unreferenced parameters in the MIDDLE of a list too: GEO_vInitGeometricObject's record lists (GEO_tdstGeometricObject_*, short) but the retail ABI puts the short count in $a2, so an unused second parameter (short xNbPoints, matching GEO_vCreateGeometricObject's signature) must be restored. Declaring it restores the exact code and line map (272/272). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38) internal compiler error 'File: CInline.c Line: 465' when a function contains an inline-asm block before a loop AND a second inline-asm block inside that loop; an array used directly as an asm operand gives "'x' could not be assigned to a register" (and the same ICE when the block is inside a loop). Workaround: pass a float* local instead of the array in the asm operand (e.g. 'float* pV1; pV1 = V1;' then 'lqc2 $vf8, 0x0(pV1)'), and keep only one asm block per function. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 float-register assignment follows the LOCAL DECLARATION ORDER of the source (first declared float local gets the lowest FPR, f0 upwards), and compiler temporaries/constant pools fill the gaps: GLI_vDisplaySerialLeaves declares AlphaVal, PosX, XVX, PosY, PosZ, XVY, XVZ, YVX, YVY, YVZ and the retail allocates f0,f1,f2,f3,f5(const 1/255),f6,f7,f8,f9,f10; GLI_vDisplaySerialFlowBlured needs AlphaVal, PosX, PosY, PosZ, Pos2X, Pos2Y, Pos2Z, XVX, XVY, XVZ, YVX, YVY, YVZ. The DWARF local list does not preserve this order, so the declaration order has to be found by permutation. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 'pref' is not emittable from C; it needs a statement-level asm block, and the operand spelling decides the addressing form: 'asm { pref 0x0, 0x120(p) }' keeps the offset inside the instruction, while the retail form (addiu reg,base,0x120 followed by 'pref 0x0,0(reg)') needs the address materialised into a register first, e.g. 'pPrefetch = (unsigned char*)pParticleArray + 0x120; asm { pref 0x0, 0x0(pPrefetch) }'. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 stores a float constant to memory as an integer word when the value is a compile-time constant (lui/ori of the bit pattern plus 'sw'), while a computed float uses 'swc1' - so a retail 'sw v1,8(t1)' with v1=0x3f800000 is just 'pC[0][2] = 1.0f;' in C, and the three constant components of one vertex are emitted in reverse order (2,1,0) when written as a chained assignment 'pC[0][0] = pC[0][1] = pC[0][2] = 1.0f;'. Verified in GLI_vDisplaySerialLeaves/Flow/FlowBlured. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 does not merge two consecutive stores of the same constant to the same struct field when the destination address is reloaded from memory between them: Clock.c fn_vInitCSOList stores zero to the +0x4 word twice, and a duplicated source statement reproduces both stores exactly (writing the same member twice). The same 8-statement loop body also stays rolled, while the 7-statement version was unrolled by 8 (main loop plus remainder), so the unroller's body-size limit matters when a retail loop is rolled. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.3-020716 lowers signed float-to-int conversions to trunc.w.s and inlines (unsigned int)float as lui 0x4f00/mtc1/c.le.s 2^31/trunc.w.s/mfc1/or 0x80000000, while 3.0.1-020123 emits cvt.w.s and calls fptoui. Retail game code that contains trunc.w.s therefore needs the 3.0.3 build for its file: Clock.c fn_ulDetermineUsefulDT is 540/604 with an unresolved fptoui relocation under 3.0.1 and 600/604 with 21/21 relocations under 3.0.3. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Per-file compiler: CollObj.c (TEMPGRP/COL) needs MWCC 3.0.3-020716 for COL_xGetParallelBoxIndexOfElement - its small switch (case 1/7/8) reproduces the retail base/index register pair ('lw v1,0xc(a0)' then 'dsll32/sll a1' and 'lw v0,0x10(a0)' + 'sll v1' + 'addu v0,v0,v1') only under 3.0.3; under 3.0.1 the base and index registers come out swapped and the sign extension is scheduled differently. All 16 other functions of the file still MATCH under 3.0.3, so the file satisfies the per-file criterion for config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Engine float-constant idiom: the engine's 'infinite' bounding-box sentinel is the literal 3.402822e38f, whose float bits are 0x7F7FFFF8 (and -3.402822e38f is 0xFF7FFFF8). MWCC materialises it as 'lui v1,0x7F7F' + 'ori a2,v1,0xFFF8' and stores it with an integer 'sw', so a parallel-box min/max initialiser reproduces the retail bytes only with that exact literal (3.402823466e+38f gives 0x7F7FFFFF and mismatches). A float constant assigned to memory is stored with 'sw', not 'swc1' (0.0f becomes 'sw zero'). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 only unrolls a counted loop (main loop of 8 stores plus a scalar remainder loop entered by jumping to its test) when the trip count is a plain local; reading the bound straight from a struct field keeps the loop rolled and re-reads the field every iteration. Copying the field into a local first ('xNb = obj->xNbElements; for (i = 0; i < xNb; i++)') makes MWCC treat the loop as counted and reproduces the retail's unrolled main loop + remainder; reaching the array through a local object pointer makes MWCC reload the array field from the object after every store, which is what the retail does. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] MWCC PS2 rejects a pointer argument whose struct type differs (MTH3D_tdstVector_ vs MTH4D_tdstVector_) as an error, not a warning, and the cast form changes the call's argument evaluation order (the retail 'move a0' / 'addiu a1' / 'addiu a2' order becomes 'addiu a1' first). When the retail passes a 4-float vector where the extracted debug signature records the 3-float variant, declaring the parameter with the 4-float type reproduces the retail's register order, while a cast does not. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 does NOT emit an out-of-line copy for an in-class (implicitly inline) member definition: moving CExtraEventActor::CExtraEventActor() and CExtraEventActor::f_vIsolate() into the class body made the standalone symbols __ct__16CExtraEventActorFv and f_vIsolate__16CExtraEventActorFv disappear from the object (two recorded MATCHes regressed to OBJECT_ERROR), even though the functions were inlined at their call sites. Defining them out of line and wrapping the definitions in '#pragma auto_inline on' / '#pragma auto_inline reset' keeps the standalone symbols AND still inlines them into later callers (the call site only needs to come before the callee's definition, with the pragma in effect at the caller). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 and 3.0.3 reproduce the retail's unsound '(x/2)*2 == x for x >= 0' simplification (the non-negative branch becomes a plain float conversion of x), but they lower the signed division itself with the branchy 'bgez; sra; addiu; sra' sequence, while the retail uses the branchless 'srl; andi; or'. Any function whose float layout depends on a signed '/2' therefore cannot be matched byte-exactly with the available builds (fn_vDrawGraduation, fn_vDrawArcadePage in GameMenu2D/DisplayMenu2D.c). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 small switch (compare chain, no jump table): the beq tests are emitted in REVERSE order of the case labels as written (case 0 tested last with beqz), the case BODIES are emitted in source order, and the default body is the chain's fall-through. The constant of the body reached through the beqz lands in that branch's delay slot, so a case whose body is 'g_XTextLeft = 18.0f' and a default of 'g_XTextLeft = 39.0f' produce the two constants in the delay slots of the beqz and of the final b respectively (GameMenu2D fn_vAnimeEndPage). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] GameMenu2D's g_aBinDataForMenu is an array of 105 _tdstBinarizedDataForMenu (one int handle each), not a single struct, and g_aForKeyAlgo is unsigned int[4]: the retail reads g_aBinDataForMenu[39] (fn_vChangeCurrentSelectedChar), [102] and [103] (fn_vDrawRecapPage) and g_aForKeyAlgo[3]. The per-TU extractor emits both as single objects, so the array declarations must be restored before the HI16/LO16 addends match. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3, 3.0b38) always emits a redundant null guard for a placement 'new (p) T', even when p is provably non-null inside 'if (p)': it emits an extra 'beqz p' that skips only the constructor. The retail ELF (CExtraEventActorPool::fs_poGetAnActor) has a single 'beqz' covering both the constructor and the following call, i.e. the retail build folded the redundant guard away. Consequence: a retail function of the shape 'p = malloc(size); if (p) { construct in place; p->call(); }' cannot be reproduced with the available compilers - either the placement new adds a second branch, or 'new T' keeps the new-expression guard before the assignment so the constructor's 'this' stays in a caller-saved register instead of the callee-saved one the retail uses. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Inlined helper calls take the CALLER's line number in the retail line table: CExtraEventActor::~CExtraEventActor's whole teardown block (delete modifiers, clear them, decrement ss32RefreshChannelCount) is one retail line because the destructor calls f_vDesInit() and MWCC inlines it. Writing the same statements out by hand in the destructor splits the retail line map into 16 entries instead of 8 and the function no longer matches on lines. C++ destructors in MWCC also carry an implicit 'deleting' flag: '~CExtraEventActor()' mangles to __dt__16CExtraEventActorFv and the compiler appends the 'blez flag; usnd_free(this)' epilogue, so a class needs 'static void operator delete(void* p) { usnd_free(p); }' to free with usnd_free instead of the global __dl__FPv. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] In a C++ translation unit the verifier's line-map check needs the candidate's '.line_<retail name>' symbol, but MWCC emits the C++ ABI-suffixed '.line_Foo__Fv'; a byte-exact C++ match therefore always reports lines=different. Declaring the retail's unmangled functions 'extern "C"' makes both the function symbol and the line symbol match the retail name and enables the line-offset check: in SDLInterpol.cpp (PS2GL/Src/SDLs) GLI_vAddMatriceOutSDL, GLI_vUpdateAllMatricesOutSDL, GLI_AddNormalInterpolation and InterpolAndSaveCameraMatrix all match with lines=ok that way. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 inline asm accepts the VU vector instructions (lqc2/sqc2, vsub/vadd/vabs/vmax/vmul/vmulx/vmove .xyzw, vmul.xyz, vaddy.x, vaddz.x, vmaxz.x, vmaxy.x, qmfc2.ni) and COP1 compares on $f0..$f31 ('$f0', not 'fv0'), and a statement-level asm block may contain labels and a COP1 branch (bc1t). It does NOT accept %hi/%lo of a symbol ('unexpected token'); use 'la reg, symbol' for an address pair or a C pointer variable and let MWCC emit the lui/addiu. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 always inserts the FP hazard nops after 'mtc1' before a COP1 compare and after the compare before a COP1 branch, even inside a statement-level asm block (probe: mtc1; nop; c.le.s; nop; bc1t). The retail VU bodies in SDLInterpol.cpp (InterpolateMatrixAndStoreWithThreshold, InterpolateMatrixAndTest__FPfPf) contain no such nops, so those bodies were hand-written assembly and cannot be reproduced byte-exactly from MWCC inline asm. The retail also splits a global's %hi/%lo pair (HI16 at one offset, LO16 several instructions later); a C pointer variable used as an inline-asm operand reproduces that split exactly. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] MWCC debug records omit an unused leading parameter: GLI_vFinalUpdateAllNormalMatrix is recorded as void(void) but its only caller passes &GLI_g_SDL_pInterpolatedCameraMatrix in a0 and the retail callee never reads a0. Restoring the leading 'float (*)[4][4]' parameter keeps the callee's own code identical and makes the call site type-check. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] A local array of matrix pointers needs a 16-byte-aligned element type to land on the retail's stack slot: in GLI_vFinalUpdateAllNormalMatrix the retail's pCamMat[2] is at sp+0x30 of a 0x40 frame, but a plain 'float (*)[4][4]' array is placed at sp+0x38; declaring 'typedef float __attribute__((aligned(16))) tMatrix4x4[4][4]' and using 'tMatrix4x4* pCamMat[2]' puts it at sp+0x30 and reproduces the frame exactly. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MS_Micro.c fn_vInitMicrosFunction: the retail builds a 17-pointer _tdstAllRetMicroSound table on the stack (memset(&st,0,0x44) then 17 field assignments, one per source line), passes &st to SND_fn_vSetRetMicroSound, then zeroes the three g_stIndexMicrosList fields with one absolute lui/sw pair per field (all three on ONE source line). The struct field order (X,Y,Z,VX,VY,VZ,DNX,DNY,DNZ,DTX,DTY,DTZ,Filtre,pfnPosition,pfnVelocity,pfnNormal,pfnTangent) matches the store order. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 rejects the C++14 class-scoped 'void* operator delete(void*)' with "illegal 'operator' declaration"; the CodeWarrior form returns void: 'static void operator delete(void* p) { usnd_free(p); }'. Declaring it inline makes the compiler's deleting destructor call usnd_free directly (R_MIPS_26 to usnd_free) instead of the global __dl__FPv, which is what the retail __dt__ bodies do. Matched __dt__19CLinkCallbackResObjFv 100/100 with lines=ok. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 only spills the implicit delete flag of a generated __dt__ into a callee-saved register ($s0) when the destructor body makes a call: with an empty body the flag stays in $a1 and the epilogue's delay slots differ (nop instead of 'move v0,this'). The retail __dt__ bodies always contain a call, so a candidate destructor needs one to reproduce the flags spill and the exact blez/usnd_free epilogue. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The retail ELF symbol at 0x001c99e0 is __dt__19CLinkCallbackResObjFv, but the debug record for that address carries the name 'usnd::streebase::_NilElmt()' and the extractor filed the stub under sndactor.cpp. The address order (it follows sndclinkcallbackresobj.cpp's mfn_bSynchroCallbackResObj at 0x001c9930) and its line numbers (48-54, ahead of that TU's other functions at 49-100) show the body belongs to sndclinkcallbackresobj.cpp; 'streebase::_NilElmt' is the extractor mis-decoding a destructor record, so a stub with that name may really be a ~Class() destructor. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail game code does not cross-jump a conditional branch into its target: MEC_fn_bCanCrashActor (MECTools.c) keeps the NULL-break path as a separate 'b' block and MEC_fn_xConvertScale emits 'bc1t <then>' + 'b <else>' instead of one branch, while MWCC 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 always merge the jump into the conditional branch ('beqz'/'bc1f') and pick the opposite branch sense. No pragma (schedule/peephole/conditional_move/opt_*) changes it, so a residual 'extra b + nop' pair or a flipped branch sense is a retail-compiler-build difference, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 vs 3.0.3 differ on punned stack locals: with the 3.0.3 build MEC_fn_vComputeInertiaTranslation (MECTools.c) is byte-exact (224/224, line-map rows ok) while 3.0.1 materialises the local's address ('addiu a1,sp,0') in the final word copy and shifts the whole register allocation (228/224). The same file also needs 3.0.1 for MEC_fn_hGetFatherActor (3.0.3 drops the retail's loop-head alignment nop, 52/56), so the unit needs both builds and cannot simply move to config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail schedules vector expressions by hoisting every load first: MEC_fn_vNormalMode/vSlowMode (MECTools.c) load all six matrix col_3 components before computing the three differences (f0-f7 live) and rematerialise the cur.* loads for the y/z results, while MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 compute each statement atomically (f0-f3 live). Separate difference temporaries, a difference-vector local, direct member paths instead of pointer locals, and a pragma sweep (schedule/peephole/conditional_move/opt_*/optimization_level 2) all leave the atomic schedule, so this residual is a scheduler difference. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] An inline asm float->int helper's return type decides the mfc1 width: 'inline long MTH_M_xRealToLong(float)' makes MWCC emit 'mfc1' plus dsll32/dsra32 (long is 64-bit on PS2), while 'inline int ...' emits the retail's plain 32-bit 'mfc1 r,' (MEC_fn_vTranslateMatrixOn). Likewise powf must be declared 'float powf(float,float)': without the prototype the arguments are promoted to double and the call becomes jal fptodp. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 assigns float locals to FPRs in DECLARATION order (first-declared gets the lowest FPR: f12, f13, f14, ...), while the scheduler emits their loads rotated by one relative to that order. Declaring the locals and assigning them as separate statements decouples the two: the FPR allocation follows the declaration list and the load order follows the assignment list. Confirmed on u_effects.c GLIPS2_vWaterFXProcess (four float locals in a nested block; declarations fSizeX, fSizeY, fAngleSpeed, fSizeMultiplier with the assignments in the order fSizeMultiplier, fAngleSpeed, fSizeY, fSizeX reproduced the retail's f12/f13/f14/f15 mapping and its exact load order). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail PS2 code sometimes keeps a float value in a caller-saved FPR ($f0) across a call and reloads nothing afterwards, whereas MWCC 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3 assume the callee may alias the pointer's target: they reload the value after the call and spill the pointer into a callee-saved GPR (extra frame). u_effects.c GLIPS2_vAddMatrixDeformationEffect is the reference case (retail 316 bytes, ours 336); the residual is an alias-analysis difference, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The retail array stride of a debug-extracted struct can exceed the sum of its members: u_effects.c Water2DEffect_ has 40 bytes of members (Position3D[4], fSizeX, fSizeY, fAlpha, Type, fAngleSpeed, fSizeMultiplier) but the retail elements are 48 bytes apart (GLIPS2_g_Water2DEffectsList is 24000 bytes for 500 entries). Adding __attribute__((aligned(16))) to the struct reproduces the 0x30 stride and the retail's indexed addressing (index*48 = (index*3)<<4). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2: a byte-sized access through a union member always compiles to a SIGNED load (lb) whatever the member's declared type and the member order are, so retail code that reads GS packet bytes with lbu must reach them through a bit field instead - MWCC narrows a bitfield store to the smallest unit holding the field, so 1-bit/2-bit/3-bit fields in a 64-bit unit reproduce the retail lbu/and/or/sb sequence exactly (PS2GL_Fragment.c internalFullScreenQuadJeffSpecialPAL_512_512, 760/764 bytes). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL GS context ModFlgs is recorded as a 54-bitfield struct in PS2GL_Fragment.c but as a plain 'unsigned long' in PS2GL_ListsRecPly.c. Retail's 8-byte test 'if (g_gsContext.ModFlgs) GsUpdate();' needs the plain view: a direct member access folds the offset into the %lo (lui %hi(g_gsContext); ld %lo(g_gsContext+0x1b0)(at)) while a punned read of the bitfield struct materialises the address (lui; addiu; ld 0(reg)) and cannot match. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.3-020716 emits cvt.w.s (0x46006324), not trunc.w.s, for the inlined float-to-unsigned conversion (lui 0x4f00; mtc1; c.le.s 2^31,x; bc1t; cvt.w.s; mfc1; b; sub.s; lui 0x8000; cvt.w.s; mfc1; or 0x80000000) - exactly the retail sequence. impPS2glFogf (PS2GL_Fragment.c) matches 204/204 under 3.0.3 but can never match under 3.0 or 3.0.1 (both call fptoui); library files have no 3.0.3 list in tools/match.py yet, so this needs a new per-file mechanism. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38, controlled probe with build/probe_fpr_order.c): float LOCALS that must live across a call are allocated to the callee-saved FPRs in DECLARATION order, ascending - 'float x, y, z;' gives x->f20, y->f21, z->f22 regardless of the order in which they are assigned (three definition orders probed). float PARAMETERS use the opposite order: 'f(float x, float y, float z)' gives x->f22, y->f21, z->f20 (mov.s $f22,$f12 / $f21,$f13 / $f20,$f14). Refines the fn_vDecompressTransform note: the 'first-defined lands in f22' rule there was the definition order coinciding with the parameter order, not the allocator's rule. Practical effect in PS2GL_DataInput.c: declaring the locals 'float x, y, z, w;' and assigning them in reverse (w = v[3]; z = v[2]; y = v[1]; x = v[0];) reproduces the retail FPR mapping. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (game baseline 3.0.1-020123, -O3,p): an in-place pointer increment ('pIndexArray += 2;' on an unsigned short*) compiles to 'addiu t0,t0,4' in the pointer's own register, while the equivalent 'pIndexArray = (unsigned short*)((char*)pIndexArray + 4);' materialises a second register ('addiu t0,v1,4'). Reproduced on u_drawVertexArray.c GLI_vUpdateCharacterVARealInterp: the in-place form gives the retail's exact 196-byte body (lw t0,0x20(a1) / bnez / addiu t0,t0,4 in the loop preheader), the reassignment form hoists the add into the earlier branch's delay slot and reorders the whole function. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 int->float conversion lowering depends on where the int lives: a scalar int/short PARAMETER (already in a GPR) converts with 'mtc1 reg,$fX' followed by 'cvt.s.w $fX,$fX' (impPS2glVertex2s/2i/3s/3i/4s in PS2GL_DataInput.c), while an int ARRAY ELEMENT converts with 'lwc1 $fX,off(base)' + 'cvt.s.w' (impPS2glVertex2iv/3iv/4iv) and a float array element needs no conversion (impPS2glVertex3fv). The earlier note only covered the array-element case; the mtc1 form is what the scalar short/int wrappers need. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 register allocation for a run of independent 'base + constant offset' address expressions follows the SOURCE ORDER of the statements, and the retail can keep one extra live copy: u_drawVertexArray.c TestAddAGOLight computes the light-element addresses as separate '((float*)&AGO_g_pLightList[0].Position[k])[idx]' constants (retail a3/t1/t0 for k=0/1/2 and t3 for the colour base, plus a 'move a3,t3' copy for the first colour store); MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 all allocate t1/t0/a3 and reuse t1 for the colour base, so the candidate is 212/216 bytes. Reproduced with ~40 source-shape variants (index local vs inline, subscript vs char* offsets, local base pointers, declaration order). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0.1-020123 game baseline) lowers a counted loop as 'while (n != 0) { n--; body }' into a guard + bottom test with the decrement hoisted to the top of the body, while the do-while spelling ('if (c == 0) continue; do { body } while (--c);') keeps a second, redundant guard. The retail u_drawVertexArray.c GLI_vUpdateCharacterVARealInterp needs the former (single 'beqz' guard plus 'addiu a1,a1,-1' at the loop head), and the same function also needs 'NbPoints--;' written as a separate statement (not 'NbPoints--' in the while condition) so the outer loop keeps the retail's guard + bottom-test shape. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL_DataInput.c vertex wrappers (impPS2glVertex2f/2fv/2iv/2s/2i/2sv/3f/3fv/3iv/3s/3i/3sv/4f/4fv/4iv/4s) all share one template that matches byte-for-byte: (1) the whole body on ONE source line (the retail line map has 5 rows: signature, hoisted lui, prologue, body, closing brace) so MWCC emits exactly one .line row per statement group; (2) 'if (g_PS2glContext.iBufCount >= g_PS2glContext.iBufLimit) { if (g_PS2glContext.eBeginMode == 7) PS2glAssembleQuads(); PS2glFlushBuffer(0); }'; (3) the vertex components are stored one by one through the reloaded pointer - '(*g_PS2glContext.pVBuf)[0] = x; ... (*g_PS2glContext.pVBuf++)[3] = w;' - which forces the retail's repeated 'lw t0,%lo(pVBuf)' reload; (4) the five attribute quads are copied with the explicit-pointer idiom 'q = (quad*)g_PS2glContext.pNBuf++; *q = *(quad*)g_PS2glContext.vcurNormal;' (and pCBuf/pCBuf_b/pTBuf/pTBuf_b), which gives the retail's lq/sq pairs and register allocation; (5) 'g_PS2glContext.iBufCount++' last. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 and all later 3.0.1b builds) lowers a two-armed conditional whose arms are one instruction each (ternary or if/else) to the SHORT form: the branch skips the then-arm and the ELSE value sits in the branch delay slot (beqz at,merge / delay subu / inline daddu = 8 bytes shorter). The retail code uses the CLASSIC form instead: beqz at,else / delay slot holds the THEN value / b merge / nop / else block. Reproduced in Rayman3 Init_PS2GL.c TR_Report (0x002a76c0): the retail is 'beqz at,0x1c; delay daddu v1,a2,zero; b 0x20; nop; 0x1c: subu v1,a2,a3'. About 40 source spellings (ternary, if/else, empty then-arm, initialise+cond, >= / > forms, nested blocks, casts, comma forms) and 8 MWCC builds all produce the short form, so this looks like a retail compiler-build difference in the block-layout/jump-threading pass. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 comparison spelling: 'x == 2' compiles to 'li v1,2; beq/bne', but the retail equivalent range test is the sltiu pair: '(x < 2) || (x > 2)' emits 'sltiu v1,x,2; bnez' plus 'sltiu at,x,3; bnez' (the else arm), and its negation '!((x < 2) || (x > 2))' emits 'sltiu v1,x,2; bnez skip' plus 'sltiu at,x,3; beqz skip' (i.e. x>=2 && x<=2). Rayman3 Init_PS2GL.c TR_Report (0x002a76c0) needs exactly the negated form for its second if. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL driver state restore in the game sources is done by poking the library's contexts directly: g_gsContext is at 0x383290 (fields PRMODE +0xA0, DTHE +0x118, TEST_1 +0x128 and +0x12A, ModFlgs +0x1B2 and +0x1B4) and g_PS2glContext at 0x3869E0 (words +0x174 and +0x17C, byte +0xC2). The game code reads a byte, ANDs it with ~mask and ORs the new bits, then writes it back; the retail uses absolute lui/addiu addressing for these because they sit outside the gp-relative range. Three Init_PS2GL.c functions (GLIPS2_vInitOtherVideoStuff, GLI_vProcessColoredFade, GLI_v_InformJustLoadedLevel) contain such blocks. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC's split large-constant address form (lui $at,hi; addu $at,base,$at; sw v,lo($at)) is emitted only by 3.0.3-020716 and 3.0b38; 3.0-011126 and 3.0.1-020123 instead materialize the whole 32-bit constant (lui+ori+addu+sw 0). Controlled probe: '*(unsigned int*)(p - 0x40420) = x;' compiles to lui $at,0xFFFB/ori $at,$at,0xFBE0/addu/sw 0 under 3.0.1 and to lui $at,0xFFFC/addu/sw -0x420($at) under 3.0.3 (retail PS2Menu2D InitPS2Menu2DTextures/CheckMenu2DTextures use the 3.0.3 form, so those files need MWCC 3.0.3). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MS_Micro.c fn_vRemoveAllMicros/fn_vDeleteMicro: the retail unlinks a micro with 'if (p->hNextBrotherDyn) p->hNextBrotherDyn->hPrevBrotherDyn = p->hPrevBrotherDyn; if (p->hPrevBrotherDyn) p->hPrevBrotherDyn->hNextBrotherDyn = p->hNextBrotherDyn; if (p->hFatherDyn) { p->hFatherDyn->lNumberOfElementsDyn--; if (p->hFatherDyn->hFirstElementDyn == p) p->hFatherDyn->hFirstElementDyn = p->hNextBrotherDyn; if (p->hFatherDyn->hLastElementDyn == p) p->hFatherDyn->hLastElementDyn = p->hPrevBrotherDyn; } p->hNextBrotherDyn = p->hPrevBrotherDyn = p->hFatherDyn = NULL;'. The hLastElementDyn store needs the address-of form ('tdstIndexMicros_** pp = &p->hFatherDyn->hLastElementDyn; *pp = ...') to keep the retail's unfolded 'addiu a0,v1,4 / sw v1,0(a0)'. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 if-converts 'if (c) { return 1; } return 0;' (and the if/else-if/else variant) into a boolean value (xori/sltiu), while the retail keeps two branch tests that both target the 'return 1' block. Writing the same test as a two-case switch ('switch (c) { case 7: case 1: return 1; default: return 0; }') keeps the compare chain and reproduces the retail exactly - the case labels are tested in reverse source order, so the first tested value must be the last case label. Matched GLI_bIsMaterialAdditive (PS2GL/SendMat.c). Also: 'if (A || B) { return 1; } return 0;' is NOT if-converted and gives the retail's 'first test -> true block, negated second test -> false block' shape. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC attributes a loop's increment and back-edge test to the closing brace of the loop body, not to the 'for' statement line. Probe: 'for (i = 0; i < 16; i++) { if (a[i] == 3) a[i] = 4; }' puts the increment/test on the '}' line in the .line table; retail PS2Menu2D/PS2M2D line maps show the same (loop control attributed to the closing-brace line), so source reconstruction must place the body's closing brace on the retail's loop-control line. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC reproduces retail's loop shape for 'while (a && b) i++;' with the conjunction in the condition: the byte-offset induction variable is incremented at the loop head, the range test follows, and the field comparison is last, with the back-edge to the increment ('for (;;i++) if (x) break;' gives the check-first shape instead). Evidence: retail PS2M2D_vCheckMaterialInCache's two cache scans match this shape instruction for instruction. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (game baseline): a jump-table switch whose first case has an empty body keeps a separate block only when that case is written 'case N: return;' (a 'break;' is merged into the default) and the switch also carries a 'default: return;' clause. With both, the retail's empty 'b <epilogue+4>' block (with 'ld ra' in the delay slot) and the unfilled range-check delay slot are reproduced: CSpriteController::Draw matches 144/144 with the line map (case order 0,1,2,4,5,3 = the retail's table order). With 'break;' or without the default clause, MWCC hoists the table's 'lui %hi(tbl)' into the 'beqz' delay slot and the layout shifts by 4 bytes. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Per-file compiler: SND_R3/src/sndcextraeventactor.cpp needs MWCC 3.0.3-020716 for CExtraEventActor::fn_bIsSoundPlaying(int) and fn_bIsSoundPlaying(CResData*): retail evaluates the two arguments of the virtual call apObjects[i]->fv_vExecCmd(4, &oBoolRef) left-to-right ('li a1,4' in the lui delay slot, 'addiu a2,sp,0x60' in the jalr delay slot) while 3.0.1 evaluates them right-to-left; 3.0.3 reproduces the retail exactly. All 17 functions recorded in that file (plus mfn_bExecute) still MATCH under 3.0.3, so the file satisfies the per-file criterion for config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt. (2026-10-20)
