- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 reproduces the R5900 VU0 matrix copy and affine multiply byte-for-byte from statement-level asm: 'asm { lqc2 vf1, 0x0(p); lqc2 vf2, 0x10(p); lqc2 vf3, 0x20(p); lqc2 vf4, 0x30(p); sqc2 vf1, 0x0(q); ... }' with a struct-pointer operand emits the retail words exactly (d8810000, d8820010, f8a10000 ...), and 'vmulax.xyzw acc, vf1, vf5x; vmadday.xyzw acc, vf2, vf5y; vmaddz.xyzw vf9, vf3, vf5z; ... vmaddaz.xyzw acc, vf3, vf8z; vmaddw.xyzw vf12, vf4, vf0w' emits the retail 4be509bc/4be510bd/4be51a4a/... words. Inside a macro the asm statements must be separated by ';' (newlines are lost by the preprocessor and the parser then reports 'end of line expected'). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 comparison idioms (measured with controlled probes): '(x & 4) == 4' on an unsigned char field lowers to 'andi; movz rd,zero,rt; beq/bne rd,1' (MWCC wrongly treats the masked value as 0/1 and compares it with 1), while '(unsigned char)(x & 4) == 4' lowers to 'andi; andi 0xFF; xori 4; sltiu' and '((x & 4) == 4) != 0' to 'andi; bne rd,4'. '(x == a) || (x == -1)' lowers to 'xor; sltiu 1; bnez; not; sltiu 1' (the xor/sltiu equality form) only when the result is used as a value; adding an '(unsigned char)' cast adds a trailing 'andi 0xFF'. A signed 'v / 2' emits the full sign-fixup (bgez/sra), '(unsigned)v / 2' emits bgez/srl, and only the explicit '((unsigned)v >> 1) | ((unsigned)v & 1)' emits the retail's plain 'srl; andi 1; or' with no fixup. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 branch-sense: 'if (x & 1) {A} else {B}' emits 'beqz' (jump to B) while 'if ((x & 1) == 0) {A} else {B}' emits 'bnez' (jump to B, A falls through) - the source polarity, not the logic, picks the sense. Used to match PS2glSwapBuffers' two duplicated plain/non-plain branches (452/452, lines ok). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: the C++ constructor of a class whose members are 'float (*m_pV[2])[4]' needs its locals declared in the order (pV2, pC2, i, MemSize) to reproduce the retail's register allocation - the loop pointers then take $a0/$a1 and the counter $a2, while declaring i first (the DWARF local order) puts the counter in $a0 and shifts every loop register by one. The loop body must also walk the float(*)[4] pointers ('pV2[0][3] = 1.0f; pV2++;') instead of indexing them ('((float*)pV2)[i*4+3]'), which is what produces the retail's pointer-walk code (addiu $a0,$a0,0x10 / sw $v1,0xC($a0)) instead of a base+offset computation. u_newSG.c C_VertexArrayContainer::C_VertexArrayContainer, 308/308 MATCH. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers an unsigned constant division of a 32-bit product to a signed mult plus an unsigned magic multiply: '(a * b) / 100' with unsigned a and b emits 'mult' (the product is always computed with a signed mult, even for unsigned operands) then 'multu 0x51EB851F; mfhi; srl 5', and '/ 1000' emits 'multu 0x10624DD3; mfhi; srl 6'; a signed dividend adds a sign correction ('srl 31; sra; addu') that retail does not have. Verified with controlled probes and matched in DevVpt.c fn_vUpdateViewportSizesInPixels (736/736): the viewport size is '(dwWidthInPercent * (dwWidth - 1)) / 100 + 1' with unsigned struct fields. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The extractor's local-variable list can be the REVERSE of the original declaration order, and MWCC PS2 3.0.1 allocates local registers in declaration order: in DevVpt.c fn_vUpdateViewportSizesInPixels the extracted order (iHeight, iWidth, iClipBottom, iClipTop, iClipRight, iClipLeft, iBottom, iRight, iTop, iLeft) produced iLeft=$v1, iTop=$v0, iRight=$t1, iBottom=$t0, while reversing the list gave the retail allocation (iLeft=$v0, iTop=$v1, iRight=$t0, iBottom=$t1) and a byte-exact match. The other 45 single swaps of that list changed nothing, so the pairs (iLeft,iTop) and (iRight,iBottom) are what the order controls. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] How the original source split a compound expression changes MWCC's register allocation and scheduling, even when the arithmetic is identical: in DevVpt.c fn_vUpdateViewportSizesInPixels the single statement 'iClipTop = (iHeight * pmTop) / 1000 + iTop;' left 112 of 184 words differing from retail, while the two statements 'iClipTop = (iHeight * pmTop) / 1000;' plus 'iClipTop += iTop;' (and likewise for the three sibling clip values) cut that to 37 and, after fixing the local order, produced an exact 736/736 match. The retail line map confirms the split: the division carries the earlier source line and the addition a later one. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The matcher's line map (the code offsets where the debug line changes) reveals the original statement layout, not just the line numbers: two statements written on one source line produce no line change between them, and MWCC tags the branch that leaves a braced if-block with the CLOSING BRACE's line while an unbraced body keeps the statement's line. In DevVpt.c fn_vUpdateViewportSizesInPixels the retail map shows the two clip-zeroing stores of the first if/else-if on one line with the block-exit branch on the same line, which is reproduced by an unbraced body with a chained assignment ('p_stVP->stViewAttrib.dwLeftInPixForClip = p_stVP->stViewAttrib.dwRightInPixForClip = 0;'); two separate statements on one line work too, but the 'else' then needs braces to parse. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 fills a call's delay slot with an instruction from the preceding block, while the retail code fills it with the first statement of the FOLLOWING block (a store moved into the delay slot). Reordering the source so the global store precedes the call reproduces the retail layout: in ActivateInGameMenu2DPhoto, 'PS2MC_g_NbFilesFound = 0;' placed before PS2MC_fn_vGetMemoryCardScreenShotsHeaders (instead of after) moved the store into the call's delay slot and matched 452/452 bytes. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 copy an 8-byte value as two lwc1 + two swc1 only when the source copies a 2-float struct (or an 8-byte struct of two floats); two separate float assignments compile to interleaved lwc1/swc1 pairs instead. Reproduced on MemoryCard.cxx fn_vSpecificManageMenu2D: 'struct {float f0,f1;}* p = (void*)base + i*8; p[0] = q[0];' emits lwc1,lwc1,swc1,swc1 per element and MWCC auto-unrolls the counted loop by 8 (main loop guard 'sltiu i,2' plus a scalar remainder guarded by 'sltiu i,10'). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0.1/3.0.3) evaluates call arguments in two phases: 'complex' expressions (a struct/union member access such as stParam.uParam.lValue) are evaluated first, then the 'simple' ones (plain variables, a deref of a cast address such as *(unsigned int*)&stParam) in forward argument order. Reproduced on FuncRay2.cxx fn_p_stGetScrollSpeed: with 'stParam.uParam.lValue' as the third argument the load is emitted before the first argument's load, while '*(unsigned int*)&stParam' puts it after and reproduces the retail order exactly (196/196 MATCH). The same two-phase rule explains the retail's 'lw a0'/'lw a2' ordering in fn_hGetNearestActorInSectorWithAnyCustombit. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] Rayman 3 retail: fn_p_stEvalTree (0x1fca30) is a dispatcher thunk, not a normal function - it reads the byte at a1+7 (the node's eProcedureId) and jumps through a 256-entry table; the dispatched evaluator returns the NEW tree pointer in v0. So the call form is 'p_stTree = fn_p_stEvalTree(p_SuperObjPerso, p_stTree, &stParam);' - the result must be assigned, otherwise the callee's a1 register is clobbered and the function returns a stale pointer. Reproduced in FuncRay2.cxx fn_p_stCode4PointsDair (the assignment also removes the extra callee-saved register and shrinks the frame from 0x50 to the retail's 0x40). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 'if (x > hi) x = hi; if (x < lo) x = lo;' compiles to two independent tests, while the retail's clamp shape is 'if (x > hi) { x = hi; } else if (x < lo) { x = lo; }': the then-arm ends with an unconditional branch to the join, so the low clamp is skipped (retail FuncRay2.cxx fn_p_stCode4PointsDair: 'bc1t' + 'b <join>' with 'mov.s f1,f2' in the branch delay slot). The same function shows the other retail spelling: a conditional store 'if (x < g) { g = x; }' keeps the compare live with the store on the fall-through only. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The retail 'unfolded element-offset' store form for a struct-array member (lui %hi(base+fieldOffset); addiu %lo; addu base,index*sizeof(struct); sw 0(base)) is reproduced by taking the address of the member of element 0 and adding the byte offset: 'p = (int*)((char*)&a[0].Field + i * (int)sizeof(a)); *p = v;'. A plain '&a[i].Field' or 'a[i].Field = v' folds the offset into the store displacement instead (MWCC 3.0.1). Verified on MemoryCard.cxx fn_vSpecificManageMenu2D with aMenu2DPages (592-byte stride) and on g_stAlbum fields. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL matrix helpers store their 4x4 matrices column-major (m[col][row] at col*16+row*4): impPS2glFrustum's frustum matrix is the classic column-major glFrustum (col0 = 2n/(r-l), 0, (r+l)/(r-l), 0), and impPS2glScaled/impPS2glTranslated scale/update rows 0..2 while leaving row 3 (the translation row) alone. Reproduced byte-exactly with the matrix built column by column. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0 emits an inline sqrt.s only from a statement-level asm or an inline asm wrapper (sqrtf/__builtin_sqrt lower to calls): 'inline float f(float x){float r=x; asm { sqrt.s r, r } return r;}' produced the retail's 'sqrt.s f1,f1' in PS2glGenRotationMatrix, while 'sqrtf(...)' became a jal to sqrtf. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] A commutative FP multiply in MWCC PS2 keeps the source operand order, but a loop unroll changes it: impPS2glTranslated's first mul.s is 'mul.s f20,f1,f0' (element * converted double) when the four columns are written out explicitly, and 'mul.s f20,f0,f1' when a 4-trip loop is unrolled. Same for impPS2glScaled (loop vs explicit statements changes the first mul.s operands), so unrolled loops are not equivalent to hand-written statements here. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0 copies a 64-byte struct with 4 lq/sq quadword pairs (a plain float[4][4] element-wise copy stays element-wise and memcpy stays a call), so a retail lq/sq block copy of a matrix implies a struct-typed object with 16-byte alignment, not a plain array. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3 all identical) allocates the callee-saved FPRs f20..f23 for values live across a call in a different order than the retail PS2GL build: PS2glGenRotationMatrix's retail code puts the angle-in-radians temp in f23 and x/y/z in f22/f21/f20, while every build here puts x/y/z in f23/f22/f21 and the temp in f20. Local declaration order, statement order and 12 pragma variants did not change it; the instruction sequence was otherwise identical (364/364 bytes). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 with '-sdatathreshold 8' puts an 8-byte global in .sbss and then addresses it gp-relative (addiu reg,gp,%gp_rel(sym)); the retail Rayman 3 code addresses the same symbol with lui/addiu. Declaring the global with an incomplete extern array type (extern T sym[];) keeps the compiler from knowing the size, reproduces the absolute addressing, and matches the retail (as in Ipt/ipt_anal.c). Confirmed on IPT_g_hScriptKeyValue in TEMPGRP/Ipt/ipt_main.c (8-byte array, retail lui/addiu at 0x2f6030). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail Rayman 3 code materialises a comparison result as a boolean register inside short-circuit &&/|| chains: 'and; xor; sltiu v1,v1,1; beqz/bnez' (the 'is zero' test of the xor). MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 instead fold the same source into a direct 'bnez/beq' on the xor. Assigning the comparison to an int variable reproduces the sltiu form, but the following '!flag' then needs an extra 'xor rd,rd,zero' unless the negation can be folded, so the retail's exact shape is not reproducible with the available builds (IPT_fn_vActualizeHistoric, TEMPGRP/Ipt/ipt_main.c, four flag comparisons at +0x400/+0x41c/+0x470/+0x48c and +0x6fc). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] INO device state buffer (stDevice.m_p_stState) layout recovered from the retail IPT code: the keyboard view is two rows of 256 key flags selected by a signed short 'active row' at +0x202, and a key is read as state[row*256 + key + 1]; the joystick view puts the direction mask (signed short) at +2 and the button mask (unsigned int) at +4, i.e. the same allocation serves both device kinds (TEMPGRP/Ipt/ipt_main.c IPT_fn_vActualizeHistoric, +0x54/+0x58 and +0x1c8/+0x1fc). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] MultiVP.c (GAM/TEMPGRP): the extractor's 'int' types are wrong for three camera-holding fields - tdstEngineStructure_::a_p_stFixCamera[5] and a_p_stGameViewportCamera[5] hold GLI_tdstCamera_* (retail passes them straight to GLI_xAdjustCameraToViewport / GLI_xSetViewportCamera, and MWCC rejects an int->pointer implicit conversion), and GLD_tdstViewportAttributes_::p_vSpecificToXD is a POINTER (retail does 'lw v0,field; sw cam,0(v0)'). Retyping them locally (keeping the differing copy out of include/r3/ENG.h) was required to match fn_vCreateViewPort, GAM_MultiVP_fn_vInvertOneVP and fn_vChangeViewPortPercent. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 retail block placement: in the PS2GL SDLInstance.cpp unit (GliGlou Drivers/PS2GL/Src/SDLs) the retail emits the loop preheader (the bound load plus the jump to the rotated loop test) as the LAST block before the function return, so the preceding 'if (!m_pList) m_NbInstances = 0;' ends with an unconditional branch whose delay slot holds the store, and the found/not-found blocks branch over the preheader. MWCC 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3 always keep the preheader before the loop; MWCC 3.0b38-030307 reproduces the retail layout but swaps the counter/bound registers and adds alignment nops (it also breaks the other functions of the same unit). No natural-C shape (for/while/do-while, break/continue, null-check or i-init forms, local bound, 360+ variants) or scoped pragma changes the placement, so the retail used an intermediate compiler build for this unit. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 keeps a local whose address escaped to an external call in a register across that call, while the retail reloads it from the stack after every call (MultiVP.c fn_vCreateViewPort: 'lw a0,0x6c(sp)' after each of GLI_xSetCameraAspectAndRatio/GLI_xSetCameraNearFar/fn_vInitCameraMatrix). Declaring the local as a one-element ARRAY ('GLI_tdstCamera_* p_stCam[1];' and using p_stCam[0]) forces the reload and reproduces the retail exactly; the plain pointer form silently passes the stale address. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] A cast on a call argument changes MWCC PS2's argument evaluation order: an uncast third argument is evaluated before the second (retail 'lw a2' then 'lh a1'), but casting the int field to a pointer ((GLI_tdstCamera_*)intValue) evaluates the second argument first. Matching MultiVP.c fn_vCreateViewPort's GLI_xAdjustCameraToViewport needed the camera field to stay an uncast pointer type. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] An unprototyped declaration ('int GLD_bCreateViewport();') lets a call site pass fewer arguments than the definition takes, which is what the retail MultiVP.c does: fn_vCreateViewPort calls GLD_bCreateViewport with 3 args (a3 is never set anywhere in the function) while fn_vCreateGameViewPort passes 4 (a3 still holds the 100 of an earlier store). A 4-parameter prototype makes MWCC reject the 3-argument call. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a 32-bit division by a constant inline with the retail's magic-multiply sequence (mult zero,MAGIC,x / mfhi / sra / addu), but a 64-bit division by a constant becomes 'jal __divdi3'. MultiVP.c fn_vChangeViewPortPercent only matches with the 32-bit form: '(unsigned int)(_iPosX * (int)stViewAttrib.dwWidth / 1000)' (magic 0x10624DD3, shift 38) and '/ 100' for the device-size case (magic 0x51EB851F, shift 37). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MultiVP.c (GAM/TEMPGRP) needs the 3.0.3-020716 build for GAM_MultiVP_fn_vAdjustViewPortSizeAndPosition: the retail inlines the float->unsigned conversion (lui 0x4F00 / c.le.s 2^31 / trunc.w.s / or 0x80000000) and 3.0.1 emits 'jal fptoui' instead (probe: (unsigned int)(100.0f/(float)i)). All 21 other recorded matches in the file still verify under 3.0.3, so the file qualifies for config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt; a draft reconstruction reaches 1340/1364 bytes under 3.0.3 but only 1124 under 3.0.1. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 C++ shape: a class whose first virtual is declared AFTER its data members gets its vtable pointer at the end of the object (C_Sdl: data to +0x44, vptr at +0x48), and 'delete p' on it then expands to the vtable store, the out-of-line destructor call and the class operator delete - matching the retail 'sw v0,0x48(s2); jal Destruct__5C_SdlFv; jal __dl__5C_SdlFPv' sequence. The store must come from an inline destructor ('~C_Sdl() { Destruct(); }') so the vptr store lands in the call's delay slot, and the null check that 'delete' adds is the only test when the source does not also test the pointer. Conversely, when the source tests the pointer first ('if (p) { delete p; }'), MWCC keeps BOTH tests (the if-test and delete's own), which is what the retail DeleteInstance shows. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.3-020716 lowers a punned int store into a global vector member ('*(int*)&g_stCollideParameterPoint.xX = ...') to the split absolute-address form 'lui $at,%hi(g); sw v,%lo(g+off)($at)', re-materialising the %hi for every store; MWCC 3.0.1-020123 instead materialises the whole 32-bit constant into a GPR (lui+addiu) once per member and CSEs it (one lui/addiu pair reused for the three stores). The retail AI code (LNK_Proc.c fn_CollisionRayCastLnKProc, AICommonTools.c fn_CollisionRayCast, Functio1.cxx fn_p_stGetFirstCollisionInfo) uses the 3.0.3 form, so those units need MWCC 3.0.3. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] MWCC PS2 callee-saved register allocation follows the LOCAL DECLARATION ORDER, and the extractor's debug local list is often the REVERSE of the original declaration order: reversing the extracted list for fn_p_stGetLinearSpeedVectorForInterAnim (a3x_intn.c) moved the transitions pointer to s0 and the previous A3dGENERAL to s1 (exactly the retail allocation) and produced the 696/696 MATCH; the same reversal moved fn_vUpdateFrame's first difference from +0x29 to +0x42 (superObject now lands in s0 as in retail). Try the reversed declaration order first when a candidate is register-equivalent but not byte-identical. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail frame block that the DWARF does not show: in mcmngr.c both SVG_fn_s32GetFileAttributes (retail frame 0x110, anon_0 Res at 0x50, iFd at 0xcc) and SVG_fn_s32DestroySaveGame (retail frame 0x570, anon_0[18] table at 0x80, iResult at 0x53c) reserve 48 extra frame bytes between the local aggregate and the scalar home slots; the DWARF local lists are complete (u32RetryCount/i/t in s1/s2/s3, iFd at 0xcc) and no source shape reproduces it: an extra local, a union/struct padding wrapper, by-value vs pointer vs prototype sceMcGetDir declarations, and MWCC 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 all give the smaller frame. A padding wrapper around the aggregate reproduces the retail frame exactly and leaves only retail alignment nops. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 keeps the unsigned-short -> int mask when the comparison is spelled 'x == 0': 'while (Res.AttrFile == 0)' on an unsigned-short member emits lhu + andi 0xffff + beqz (the retail's mcmngr.c SVG_fn_s32GetFileAttributes), while 'while (!Res.AttrFile)' elides the andi because lhu already zero-extends. '!', '== 0', '& 0xFFFF', '(unsigned int)' and '== 0xFFFF' all reproduce the mask. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 allocates one 16-byte frame slot per DECLARED parameter even when the parameter is unused and never stored: the retail mcmngr.c SVG_fn_s32GetFileAttributes reserves four parameter slots (0xd0..0x10f in a 0x110 frame) but stores only a0/a1/a2, and its DWARF record lists just three parameters (unused parameters are omitted). Declaring a fourth unused int parameter reproduces the retail's parameter area. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 sinks a store into a call's delay slot only when the store precedes the call in the source order: 'g_X = 1; f();' compiles to 'jal f; sw g_X' (the store executes before the call) while 'f(); g_X = 1;' keeps the store after the call. Matching the retail's delay-slot stores therefore requires the assignment to be written BEFORE the call in the source. Confirmed on MemoryCard.cxx fn_vSpecificManageMenu2D (g_bAlbumInitialized, g_bAlbumLoadInProgress, MNU_g_SavingScreenshot, PS2MC_g_NbFilesFound, g_CurrentActionMenu2D). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Trigger for the unexplained 48-byte frame block in mcmngr.c: it appears exactly where a LOCAL aggregate's address is passed to a NON-prototype callee (sceMcGetDir is declared 'extern int sceMcGetDir();'): SVG_fn_s32GetFileAttributes (anon_0 Res) and SVG_fn_s32DestroySaveGame (anon_0[18]) both need it, while the same functions' matched siblings do not - SVG_fn_s32GetFileInfo passes a pointer PARAMETER to sceMcGetDir (no block) and SVG_fn_s32GetFileSize passes &Res to the PROTOTYPED SVG_fn_s32GetFileInfo (no block). The block sits immediately after the aggregate (0x90..0xbf for Res at 0x50, 0x500..0x52f for the table at 0x80) and pushes the scalar home slots and iFd/iResult up by 0x30. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 folds the unsigned-int -> unsigned-long widening of a call argument away (the o32 ABI only defines the low half of an argument register), while the retail code materialises it as 'dsll32 rd,rs,0 / dsrl32 rd,rd,0'. To reproduce the retail bytes the caller must widen explicitly: 'SearchSectorCache((unsigned long)(pLevel->sector + i), loadbuf)' produced the target's dsll32/dsrl32 pair, while the plain 'pLevel->sector + i' was widened for free (sceecd.c ReadDirInCache). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The retail PS2 CD-read retry loop in sceecd.c is one loop with two 'continue' paths: 'while (1) { sceCdDiskReady(0); if (!sceCdRead(sectornumber, 1, GLOBAL_LoadBuffer + (MinSector << 11), &CDmode)) continue; sceCdSync(0); if (sceCdGetError()) continue; break; }'. That reproduces both back-edges to the single sceCdDiskReady call; the naive 'while (!sceCdRead(...)) { sceCdDiskReady(0); }' plus a separate error loop emits a different branch layout. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail PS2 directory code assembles 32-bit sector/length fields from raw bytes with '+' (not '|') so MWCC emits addu chains: pFile->sector = rec[2] + (rec[3] << 8) + (rec[4] << 16) + (rec[5] << 24). The same idiom fills the root directory cache entry from the linked path string: loadbufRootDir[0x9E..0xA1] for the sector and loadbufRootDir[0xA6..0xA9] for the length (the retail's .relmain shows HI16/LO16 relocations against loadbufRootDir with those offsets). (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] MWCC debug extraction types the retail's 'pRootDirCache' (sceecd.c) as int although the retail symbol holds a stFile_* pointer; the extracted local 'RootDirAlreadyLoaded8' is a function-local static (the '' suffix in the retail symbol name), so it must be declared 'static' inside the function to get the same gp-relative relocation and the same zero-initialised .bss cell. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (2002-01-23): a double clamp written as a NESTED TERNARY reproduces the retail branch shape, while the if/else-if form does not. impPS2glDepthRange (PS2GL_Matrix.c) is 368/384 bytes with 'if (zNear < 0.0) zNear = 0.0; else if (zNear > 1.0) zNear = 1.0;' under 3.0, because MWCC splits the clamp's merge block, duplicates the merge prologue into the beqz delay slot and drops the two 'b merge' branches; 'zNear = (zNear < 0.0) ? 0.0 : ((zNear > 1.0) ? 1.0 : zNear);' gives the retail 384/384 bytes, 26/26 relocations and the retail line map (one-line switch cases 'case 16: ...; break;'). MWCC 3.0.3 also matches that body but breaks the file's impPS2glLoadIdentity, so this file is a 3.0.1 file (config/library-3.0.1-sources.txt). (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 (all four local builds agree): the callee-saved FPR mapping of PS2glGenRotationMatrix (PS2GL_Matrix.c) cannot be reproduced - the retail puts the radian temp in f23 and the float parameters x/y/z in f22/f21/f20, while every build puts the temp in f20 and the parameters in f23/f22/f21. The candidate is 364/364 bytes with the same instruction sequence otherwise, so only the FPR numbering differs (it also moves the mul.s of the deg2rad product and the branch-delay-slot contents). Tried without effect: the local declaration order (both the extractor's list and its reverse), an explicit local for the deg2rad product, the parameters copied into the locals xs/ys/zs, the mag computation before the sin/cos calls, and the four available MWCC builds. The same one-register shift appears in PS2glBoundingSphereParameters (retail: local fTemp in f20, parameters in f21..f25, temps in f26/f27; candidate: parameters in f20..f24, locals above), so it is a property of the retail PS2GL build rather than of one function. (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL_Matrix.c (library) is built with MWCC 3.0.1 (2002-01-23), not the 3.0 library baseline: impPS2glDepthRange only matches under 3.0.1/3.0.3 (384/384 with the nested-ternary clamp), and 3.0.3 breaks the file's impPS2glLoadIdentity (72/64 bytes) while 3.0.1 keeps all 11 recorded matches. The file is listed in config/library-3.0.1-sources.txt with that evidence. (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a select 'c ? t : e' as 'dst = t; movz dst, e, c' (then-value in the destination, else-value moved in), while the retail game code uses 'dst = e; movn dst, t, c' (else-value in the destination, then-value moved in). To make our compiler emit the retail's movn form, write the select with the arms swapped and the condition negated, e.g. '(x == 0) ? 0 : 1' instead of 'x ? 1 : 0'. The arms must also be typed '(unsigned char)0' / '(unsigned char)1' so the uchar parameter needs no andi 0xff truncation: '(x == 0) ? (unsigned char)0 : (unsigned char)1' compiles to 'li v0,1; ...; movn a2,v0,v1' with the destination in the argument register and no mask (matched CAM_fn_p_stActivate, 164/164). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2: a 1-bit bitfield store (e.g. 'x.flag = 1;') lowers to a byte read-modify-write 'lbu / and 0xFFFFFFFE / or / sb' with the mask as a 32-bit complement and the value materialised as a 64-bit 'daddiu reg,zero,1' - matching the retail byte flag update in PS2GL_BufferControl.c's MySwapBuff3Dc. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2: a 64-bit load from a GLOBAL ('ld rt,%lo(sym+off)(at)') is only produced by a DIRECT member access of a 64-bit member; reading a struct through a cast pointer ('*(unsigned long*)&g.member') materialises the address instead (lui/addiu + ld reg). Adding a 64-bit view to the aggregate (a union member around the element array) restores the folded form (matched MySwapBuff3Dc's g_stDoubleBufAdd reads). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1-020123 and 3.0.3-020716 lower a signed int x/2 as 'bgez x; addiu 1; sra' and never as the retail's 'bltz x; srl rd,rs,1; andi at,rs,1; or rd,rd,at' sign-fix-up form, so the retail's (x/2)*2 idiom (Rayman 3 IPO_RLI.c IPO_fn_vFrameInitForRLITables +0x1c, IPO_fn_vSetAddRLITable, IPO_fn_vSetChangeOfRLITable) is not reproducible. The explicit C form that would emit the srl, '(unsigned)x >> 1 | (x & 1)', is mis-compiled: MWCC models the unsigned >> as sign-preserving (the same bug as the retail), applies the unsound (x/2)*2==x simplification to the OR result and rewrites the branch onto the OR's sign, which duplicates the negative block and drops the float doubling (the live path loses the add.s). With a signed shift the block shape and size match exactly (sra instead of srl, and the or/cvt destination in a different register), so the residual is a compiler-build difference, not a source-shape one. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] A store through a local pointer variable stops MWCC PS2 from hoisting loop-invariant global loads out of the loop: in a3x_intn.c fn_bTestSamePosition, indexing the 256-entry tdstKeyFrame_* stack array directly let MWCC hoist the gp_FramesKFIndex/gp_stKeyFrames loads before the loop, while the same stores through a 'tdstKeyFrame** pPrevKeyFrames = a256_p_stPrevKeyFrames;' pointer kept the loads inside the loop as in retail (848/872 bytes). Useful when a retail loop reloads a global every iteration. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0-011126 emits a direct whole-word member access on a global only through a union/aggregate member ('g_ctx.REG.u64'); a plain '*(unsigned long long*)&g_ctx.REG' materialises the address instead (lui+addiu). Retail PS2GSPackage.c GsUpdateLimited copies the GS register slots (PRMODECONT/PRMODE/ALPHA_1/FOGCOL) into a 16-byte package temp with the union spelling, so the recovered bitfield register types need a whole-word union view. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0-011126 (library baseline) schedules the branch delay slot differently for 'if (x >= 2)' and 'if (x > 1)': with '>= 2' the compiler keeps the compare result in the source register and moves the preceding global store into the delay slot, with '> 1' it compares into $at and leaves a nop in the delay slot. The retail PS2GSPackage.c GsUpdateLimited (984/984 bytes, 76/76 relocs) only matches with the '> 1' spelling. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Deferred inlining (-inline deferred) makes #pragma auto_inline a CALLEE-side property: a callee defined inside an auto_inline region is inlined at every call site in the unit, and caller-side pragma state (auto_inline off/reset, callee defined earlier or later in the file) does not change that. DisplayText.c needs TXT_vAfficheSimple inlined into TXT_vAfficheSimpleWithColor but called out-of-line from TXT_vAfficheSimpleWithShadow, so the auto_inline pragma cannot be used there; the MSWay-style statement macro (one macro invocation = one line-map group) reproduces the inlined copy instead. (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Source-layout: retail functions whose DWARF line map shows several statements in ONE group were written as several statements on one source line (or as a statement macro), because MWCC attributes every instruction of a source line - including constants hoisted into the prologue - to that line. DisplayText.c TXT_vAfficheSimpleWithShadow needs three statements on the shadow-pass line and three on the text-pass line (the load of the old colour belongs to the first group, its restore to the second), so writing one statement per line gives extra line-map changes and a 'lines=different' verdict. (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] MWCC's SCE .line table lists one (line, code-offset) entry per line group, and the scheduler interleaves constant materialisation (lui/ori/mtc1 for literals) into the prologue under the line of the statement that uses the constant while the register saves keep the function's declaration line. That alternating decl/statement pattern in a prologue is a normal MWCC artefact, not a source-layout error. (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (game baseline) cannot reproduce the retail 'loop entry guard': the retail loads the loop bound, tests it with beqz and sends the FALSE branch to the loop's own 'b test' jump (the guard only skips the loop initialisation), keeps the initialisation as separate instructions and leaves that unconditional jump's delay slot empty. MWCC 3.0.1 instead sends the guard's false branch to the block exit and sinks the last initialisation into the unconditional jump's delay slot. Retail shows this shape in u_drawHier.c GLI_PS2GL_PrepareSpecificDataForSPO and GLI_vCleanDispTemporaryDataSPO; every source spelling (if-guard around the loop, plain for, while, init order) and every scoped pragma (opt_propagation/opt_lifetimes/opt_common_subs/opt_dead_assignments/opt_dead_code/opt_loop_invariants/opt_strength_reduction/opt_unroll_loops/peephole/schedule/conditional_move/global_optimizer) and all four available MWCC builds (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3, 3.0b38) compile to the same non-matching code, so it is a compiler-build difference, not a source shape. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0.1 removes a dead local increment, but keeps it when the same variable is read somewhere else in the function. The retail u_drawHier.c children loop (GLI_PS2GL_PrepareSpecificDataForSPO, GLI_vCleanDispTemporaryDataSPO) increments a 16-bit counter that is never read (the addiu + dsll32/dsra32 sign-extension pair), so the original source must have reused that same short variable as a live loop counter elsewhere in the function: declaring one 'short' counter for both the children loop and the objects-table loop reproduces the retail's otherwise dead increment. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The retail PS2GL code uses the R5900 conditional move (spimdisasm 'movn', e.g. word 0x0B186400 in GLI_vDMAPreparePO and 0x0B286400 in the SFX particle code) for a two-arm assignment such as 'if (a != 0) r = b; else r = -2;'. MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 never emit it (the same source compiles to a branch pair) and '#pragma conditional_move on/off' changes nothing, so a C reconstruction cannot reproduce those instructions. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail u_drawHier.c reads the objects-table elements through a walked pointer (p_ObjectsTableElement++ with a 0x14 stride) and the LOD definitions through a byte-offset induction variable (iOffset += 4 over d_p_stLodDefinitions), not through an index; the LOD loop's scaled-index temporary is a separate register, so declaring the byte offset explicitly reproduces the retail register allocation (table in s5, element pointer in s1, LOD counter in s3, byte offset in s4). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a negated && chain '!(A && B)' with A an int test as 'sltu v0,zero,x; xori v0,v0,1; beqz v0,join' plus an 'xori s1,v0,1' in the branch delay slot: the first operand's value is computed as 'x != 0' and negated, the branch tests the negated value, and the true result (1) is materialised in the delay slot. The same expression written as 'x != 0 || B' keeps the non-negated value in the destination register instead, so the retail's xori+delay-slot shape proves the source used the negated form (Demos.c DEMO_fn_bUpdateDynamicHierarchy). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: a float local declared inside a nested block is scalarized and its load+conversion is scheduled before the enclosing conditional branch, while the same conversion written inline in the expression is scheduled after the branch's join. In Demos.c DEMO_fn_bUpdateDynamicHierarchy the retail emits 'lwc1 f0,global; bltz; cvt.s.w f1,f0' (the int->float conversion of a global int in the branch delay slot) only when the numerator is a block-scoped float local; written inline it becomes 'lwc1' after the join, with different FPR allocation. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 places standalone struct locals on a 16-BYTE stack grid, while the retail game code uses an 8-byte grid. In GAM/Actions/SectInfo.c GAM_fn_vSectInfoTestChangeSectorForCharacter the retail keeps four 12-byte MTH3D_tdstVector_ locals at sp+0x88/0x98/0xA8/0xB8 (the array elements stay 12 bytes apart at sp+0x70/0x7C), but 3.0.1 puts them at sp+0x90/0xA0/0xB0/0xC0. Declaring the local type with __attribute__((aligned(8))) does not change it, and array elements keep their natural 4-byte stride. A candidate whose every local offset is a multiple of 16 while the retail's are 8 mod 16 is a compiler-build difference, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 float zero-test chain: '(0.0f != x) || (0.0f != y) || (0.0f != z)' compiles to c.eq.s $f1,$f0 (zero first) plus a bc1f branch to the BODY for the first two operands and a bc1t branch past the body for the last one, while the De Morgan form '(0.0f == x) && (0.0f == y) && (0.0f != z)' branches to the SKIP block instead. Retail MEC_fn_vProposeSpeed / vImposeSpeed / vAddSpeed use the || form (bc1f to the body), so the recovered '&&' reconstruction inverts the branch polarity. Verified by matching all three functions. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] Rayman 3 retail (GAM/Actions/SectInfo.c): the gp-relative globals g_hCharacterLauchingSoundEvents (gp-0x6F68) and gp_stInactiveDynamicWorld (gp-0x6FC8) are extracted as 'int' but are pointers. g_hCharacterLauchingSoundEvents is compared against a HIE_tdstSuperObject_* character pointer (the main character) and gp_stInactiveDynamicWorld is used as an HIE_tdstSuperObject_* list head whose hFirstElementDyn/hLastElementDyn/lNumberOfElementsDyn live at +0x08/+0x0C/+0x10. The 'int' declaration makes the member accesses a compile error, so the type must be restored to HIE_tdstSuperObject_* in the owning TU (the symbol name and its 4-byte size stay the same, so the relocation still matches). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail (MEC_fn_vSpeedLimit, MECBhv) widens a 32-bit float bit pattern to 64 bits with 'dsll32 rd,rs,1 / dsrl32 rd,rd,1' (shamt=1, keeping 31 bits) before a zero test; MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 narrow the same test to 32 bits (lw + beqz) and only emit a widening under a scoped '#pragma peephole off', with shamt=0 instead of 1. The retail shamt=1 form appears in 8 sites in MECBhv.s/MECOpt.s only, so it is a retail compiler-build difference, not a source shape. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2: a local vector that is copied to the matrix with the int-lvalue idiom '*(int*)&dst.xX = *(int*)&src.xX' has its ADDRESS MATERIALIZED (addiu reg,sp,off), so the float store of the same component then uses 'swc1 f0,0(reg)'. Declaring the local as 'union { MTH3D_tdstVector_ v; int i[3]; }' and reading the components through the int view keeps the stack-reference addressing (swc1 f0,0xa4(sp) / lw a0,0xa4(sp)). Confirmed by matching MEC_fn_vProposeSpeed, MEC_fn_vImposeSpeed and MEC_fn_vAddSpeed (Rayman3 MECOpt.c). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC attributes the branch that jumps out of an if/else to the line of the closing brace of the then-block, so 'if (c) { A; } else { B; }' gives the jump the '} else {' line while 'if (c) A; else B;' gives it the A line. When matching a retail line map, drop the braces around a single-statement then-branch to move the join branch onto the statement's own line (matched TXT_GetBoundingBox). (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 and 3.0.3 constant-fold '0.0f * <global>' to 0.0f (the whole '(unsigned int)(0.0f * g)' collapses to a plain 'sb zero'), but the same multiply survives when the 0.0f comes from a float LOCAL assigned 0.0f: the compiler then emits 'mtc1 zero,fN' plus 'mul.s fM,fN,fM' and the full unsigned conversion. Verified with a controlled probe (literal 0.0f folded, float local not folded) and by matching retail GAM/Actions/3dData.c fn_v3dDataInit, whose frame-rate store is '(unsigned int)(xFrameRate * g_fGlobalAccelerationFactorForAnims)' with xFrameRate a local set to 0.0f. Use a float local to keep a 'constant * global' multiply alive. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2: the declaration order of a CALLEE's locals also decides the register allocation and load order of that callee's INLINED copy in the caller. GAM/Actions/3dData.c fn_bTestSubAnim declared as (tdst3dData_* h3dData, tdstSubAnim_* hSubAnimInFamily, tdstSubAnimInUse_* param0) makes the inlined body inside fn_vAddSubAnimation reuse the h_3dData register for the list head (retail: 'lw v1,0x1b4(v1)' after the family load), while the other five orders make the compiler take a fresh register (a0) and swap the two independent loads, so the inlined copy was 8 words off even though the standalone fn_bTestSubAnim matched byte for byte in every order. When an inlined body differs only in register roles or load order, permute the callee's local declaration order, not only its body. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 -O3,p (game speed mode) pads a function's end label to 8 bytes: in AGO3_vProcessAll the 'if (cond) { for(...) {...} }' form emitted an extra 'nop' before the epilogue (116 vs the retail's 112 bytes) because the loop-exit label at 0x5c was moved to 0x60. Writing the same logic as 'if (cond == cond2) { return; } <loop>' (an early return, so the loop is no longer inside a conditional block) removes the padding and matches the retail byte-for-byte, including the line map. The same shape with 'if (a != b) {A} else {B}' still pads. Matched AGO3_vProcessAll 112/112. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The retail DWARF local list of a function is in REVERSE declaration order (read it with tools/extract_debug.py's DebugParser, not only the extracted stub): fn_bTestSubAnim's DWARF lists [tdstSubAnimInUse_*, hSubAnimInFamily, tdst3dData_*] while the declaration order that matches the retail is exactly the reverse, (tdst3dData_*, tdstSubAnim_*, tdstSubAnimInUse_*). The list also proves locals the extracted stub drops (fn_bTestSubAnim really had a tdst3dData_* local), and knowing them is what fixed the inlined copy's register allocation in fn_vAddSubAnimation. fn_v3dDataInit's DWARF (h_SupObject, hCharacter, hFather, hNextSubAnim, hThisSubAnim) likewise omits the tdstObjectsTablesList_*/tdst3dData_*/float locals the matching source needs. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail game code evaluates the FLOAT arguments of a call right-to-left, and MWCC 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/b38 evaluate them left-to-right when the argument is a 'constant + field' expression: AGO3_GrowingUVAnimatedFireBall::Display materialises the -0.25f constant and m_fCurrentV (the V2 argument) before the +0.25f constant and m_fCurrentU (the U2 argument), while every MWCC build emits them in source order; the only difference is the order of the two 'lui'/'mtc1' pairs and the two field loads (the temp registers f2/f3 swap with them). About 20 source spellings (operand order, subtraction form, float cast, parentheses, local temporaries, swapped UV roles) and all four MWCC builds keep the source order, so this is a compiler-build residual, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC's -sdatathreshold 8 puts every static object of 8 bytes or less in .sdata (gp-relative), but the retail's AGO3_ScoreDisplay::m_a_fSize (8 bytes) is addressed with lui/%lo, i.e. it lives in .data. Declaring the array with more than 8 bytes (float[4]) forces .data and reproduces the retail's addressing and code exactly (the extra elements are unused); the retail symbol is 8 bytes, so this is a data-placement workaround, not the original declaration. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The retail's AGO3_vAddPart is a C-linkage function (unmangled symbol) with nine parameters: four integer/pointer arguments in a0-a3, three more in t0-t2, and two floats in f12/f13. The MWCC definition record only lists the referenced ones (PartNumber, pPosition, pDirection, pMat1, fParam1, V1), so the extracted declaration is short; restoring 'extern "C"' plus the full nine-parameter list is required for AGO3_ReInit's call to relocate to the unmangled symbol and for the float argument to land in f13 (f12 carries the caller's own float parameter through unchanged). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] TEMPGRP/Ago/AGO3.cpp is a candidate for config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt: AGO3_ScoreDisplay::Display and AGO3_ScoreDisplay::Init match byte-for-byte only under MWCC 3.0.3-020716 (3.0.1 calls fptoui for the retail's inlined float-to-unsigned conversion, and it allocates the two callee-saved registers s0/s1 the other way round), while 3.0.3 keeps every other recorded match in the file (49/50 under 3.0.3 vs 47/50 under 3.0.1; the one failure, AGO3_ScrollMatUV::Display, is a pre-existing index-ownership problem - the source index attributes that function to Action.c - and fails under both builds). AGO2.c in the same directory is already on the 3.0.3 list. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0 lowers a mask that keeps only the low N bits (N<=28) of a 32-bit or 64-bit value into the 64-bit shift pair 'dsll32 rd,rd,S / dsrl32 rd,rd,S' with S = 32-N (e.g. x & 0x0FFFFFFF -> dsll32/dsrl32 with sa=4, x & 0x07FFFFFF -> sa=5). The masked value keeps its 32-bit type, so no u64->pointer truncation is added at the return. Reproduced in PS2GL_fn_pvEndOfDMABuffer (40/40 MATCH). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lays a loop out differently depending on how its exit test is written: a 'while (A || B) { ... }' loop whose body increments the index gets the rotated layout [increment][test][body] with the increment block placed BEFORE the test and the loop's back-edge jumping to the increment, while the same loop written with 'if (A) continue; if (B) continue; break;' gets [body][increment][test] with the back-edge to the body. The retail form (GAM/Always.c fn_p_stAllocateAlways, the free-slot search over g_stAlways.d_hAlwaysGenerator) needs the compound condition to reproduce the retail block order and branch polarities (bnez back to the increment, not beqz forward to the exit). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 lower a char counter used as a while condition as 'while (cCounter--)' (the test uses the pre-decrement value, the decrement is the char sign-extension 'addiu/dsll32 by 24/dsra32'), while the equivalent 'while (cCounter != 0) { ...; cCounter--; }' emits an extra dsll32/dsra32 pair for the char-to-int promotion of the comparison and a different loop shape. Reproduced in SND_R3 sndeethrd.cpp usnd_uiComputeRelativePath: the '..\\' strcat loop matches the retail instruction for instruction only with the post-decrement spelling (retail 0x118..0x14c: move v1,s4 / addiu v0,s4,-1 / dsll32 / beqz / dsra32 / strcat / move v1,s4 / addiu / dsll32 / bnez / dsra32). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] R5900 lq/sq decoding: in the retail ELF the quadword load/store are op 0x1E (lq, bit24=0) and op 0x1F (sq, bit24=1) with base in bits 25-21, rt in bits 20-16 and a byte offset in bits 15-0; the register pair is (rt = the LOW 64 bits, rt+1 = the high half) - confirmed by a 16-byte copy in the retail MMG code (cpammg.c fn_p_vStaAlloc) and by MWCC 3.0.1 output. rabbitizer's default MIPS category prints these as .word. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 hoists a conditional branch ABOVE the preceding store when the branch condition lives in a register, which makes the store conditional ('sw v1,0(a0)' then 'beqz v0' becomes 'beqz v0' then 'sw v1,0(a0)'). Moving the assignment that feeds the guarded block out of the if (assign the pointer to a local before 'if (flag)') restores the retail order 'store; branch' (GAM/Always.c fn_p_stAllocateAlways). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC's line table records one line change per source statement, and the retail sources often put a whole block on ONE physical line: in GAM/Always.c fn_p_stAllocateAlways the 20-statement 'unlink from the father and insert as first child' block and the 18-statement 'append as last child' block are each a single retail line (679 and 683), and the loop's 'continue' conditions share lines. Reproducing the retail line map needs those statements merged onto one line; putting the loop's closing brace on the same line as the last statement of the body moves the loop's back-edge test onto the increment's line. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 reuses the callee-saved register of an INTERCHANGEABLE variable: after a loop that only compares two same-typed pointers (p1 vs p2), the compiler may assign the recomputed pointer values to each other's dead registers, and the ORDER of the initial assignments decides which value is computed first and therefore which register it lands in. Controlled pair in SND_R3 sndeethrd.cpp usnd_uiComputeRelativePath: with 'p1 = szAbsFP1; p2 = szAbsFP2;' the recompute 'p1 = base1 + idx; p2 = base2 + idx;' keeps the retail's s2 = base1-derived / s3 = base2-derived mapping, while 'p2 = szAbsFP2; p1 = szAbsFP1;' makes the compiler compute base2 first and swap the two registers (s2 = base2-derived). The same swap happens with any spelling of the recompute (&arr[i], char* temp, int base cast). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 reproduces a retail 16-byte quadword access (lq/sq) only when the value is a 16-byte type kept in a register pair: a '__int128' scalar does it (load/store of the whole value), while a 16-byte-aligned struct copy is spilled to the stack and plain member accesses use ld/sd. cpammg.c fn_p_vStaAlloc models the MMG static allocator's 16-byte free cells with '__int128' and reproduces the retail's lq/sq, the 'dsll32/dsrl32' zero-extension of a 32-bit cell size and the 'dsll32/dsra32' narrowing of the 64-bit link pointer. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] MWCC's DWARF function record can omit an UNUSED parameter in the middle of the list, not only leading ones: GAM/Always.c fn_p_stAllocateAlways's record lists (otObjectModelType, p_stFatherSuperObject, _hGenerator, p_stMatrix) but the retail ABI has five parameters - the 5th arrives in $t0 (o32 passes the 5th integer argument in t0), so the matrix pointer is read from t0 and the omitted 4th parameter (uwAction) sits in a3 unused. The sibling fn_p_stDemoAllocateAlways, whose record lists all six parameters, shows the real order: (int, HIE*, HIE*, unsigned short uwAction, MAT*, short). (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] HIE_tdstSuperObject_.hVisualBoundingVolume (+0x34) and hCollideBoundingVolume (+0x38) hold COL_tdstBoundingSphere_* pointers in the retail, not the 'int' the extractor emits: GAM/Always.c fn_p_stAllocateAlways stores the result of fn_vGetBoundingVolumeOfFamily() into them, so the field must be typed as a pointer (the size is unchanged, 4 bytes). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail add-zero family, extra data point: the retail's 'addiu reg,reg,0' inside a multi-term array address always comes with a BASE-FIRST final 'addu rd,base,idx*scale' (SND_R3 sndeethrd: 'addu a1,v1,a2' with v1 = base-4 and a2 = uiCS*4, and 'addu s3,v1,s0' in usnd_vEnterCritSect), while MWCC 3.0.1 renders the same sum index-first ('addu a1,a2,v1') for every source spelling tried (plain arr[i-1], pointer to first element, char* temp with a separate -4 step, int base cast, 2-level array view, struct array with a member at offset 0, dead scaled-index subscript). MWCC 3.0b38-030307 produces the base-first 'addu' but folds the -4 into the %lo addend instead of keeping 'li at,-4; addu base,base,at', so no available build reproduces the retail pair. The retail ELF has 124 such add-zero sites (usnd_vLeaveCritSect +0x18, usnd_vEnterCritSect +0x38, usnd_vKillTimer +0x48/+0xbc, usnd_uiSetTimer +0xac/+0x110, usnd_vTimerLoop +0x48/+0xb0). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MMG static allocator data model (recovered from cpammg.c fn_p_vStaAlloc/fn_vStaFree and Mmg_fn_vInitSpecificBlock): a block's free list starts at tdstBlockInfoPriv_.p_cFirstFree; each free cell is 16 bytes, the low quad word holds the free size in 16-byte units and the second quad word (at cell+0x10, i.e. the user area) holds the link to the next free cell; the user pointer is cell+0x10, a cell spans at least two cells, the allocation count is (size>>4)+1 units and the new cell starts at cell + count*16. The MMG error calls report g_ucMmgModuleId (gp-0x79F0), not g_ucErmModuleId, and the debug fill byte is 0xAA. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Rayman3 AI/AIGame/CAM_Act.c needs the MWCC 3.0.3-020716 per-file compiler: fnv_MoveAToB (680/680) and fn_p_stCamCineInit (1156/1156) are byte-exact only under 3.0.3, while all nine previously recorded matches in the file still match under both builds, so the file satisfies the per-file 3.0.3 criterion for config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Unfolded-offset recipe confirmed on cpammg.c fn_p_vStaAlloc: the retail computes the new free cell's link address as (cell+0x10)+index*16 ('addiu v0,cell,0x10; addu v0,v0,index<<4') and the cell address as cell+index*16; the source spelling '((T*)((char*)cell + 0x10))[index]' reproduces the first form while 'p[index+1]' and 'p[index]' fold it back to (cell+index*16)+0x10. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2: a norm macro over a 3-float vector reproduces the retail's accumulator code (mula.s/madda.s/madd.s + sqrt.s) only when the temporaries are declared in the order Z, X, Y: the first-declared temporary takes the lowest FPR (), so the third component can keep the register value the subtraction left in  (the store/load pair is forwarded instead of reloaded), the first component takes  and the second . MTH_VECTOR_NORM_TO/MTH_VECTOR_NORM2_TO/MTH_VECTOR_NORM_SYNC_TO in CAM_Act.c match exactly with that order; the natural X, Y, Z order reloads the third component (12 extra bytes) and mis-allocates the callee-saved registers. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail CAM_Act.c fn_p_stCamCineInit writes the moved position into the DESTINATION vector: the final three statements store stTempVector + the current position into the destination pointer (_p_stDest), while the current position is only read. The extractor's parameter names keep the same order (a0=super object, a1=source, a2=destination, a3=current), so the source must store through _p_stDest, not _p_stCurrent. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 inline asm 'cvt.w.s f30, x' (R5900 encoding 0x460007A4, printed as trunc.w.s by rabbitizer) plus 'mfc1 r, f30' reproduces the engine's float-to-int conversion: the callee-saved  is saved/restored by the prologue, so the frame grows by 0x10 and every stack offset shifts. An 'inline int' helper (not long) is needed for the plain 32-bit mfc1 with no dsll32/dsra32. Confirmed in CAM_Act.c fn_p_stCamCineInit (1156/1156). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 set up the two float arguments of a call in ARGUMENT-REGISTER order (f12 then f13), so PS2glTexCoord2f(0.0f,1.0f) compiles to 'mtc1 zero,$f12' before 'mtc1 v0,$f13'; the retail u_drawSprites.c DrawAllIndexedSprites has the opposite order ('mtc1 v0,$f13' first). The order is not a source property: it flipped when an unrelated function in the SAME translation unit changed, so the residual is a whole-TU compiler-context effect, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] The PS2GL driver's GS context needs byte accesses for TEST_1.ZTE/ZTST (g_gsContext+0x12a) and ModFlgs.TEST_1_Modified (g_gsContext+0x1b4): split the bitfield containers so ZTE/ZTST live in an unsigned char container at +0x2 of the 8-byte test record and the ModFlgs flags 32..39 in an unsigned char container at +0x4 of the 8-byte bitmap (the rest of the bitmap stays in unsigned long). Reproduced in u_drawSprites.c (DrawAllIndexedSprites, GLI_vDisplayWorldTriangle) exactly like the sibling driver u_SPGArray.cpp. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] The retail PS2GL driver copies MTH vectors word-wise: the indexed sprite's centre point is copied into the 4-float VU vector with '*(int*)&v.xX = *(int*)&c.xX' (lw + sw), and the transformed vector is read back the same way ('*(int*)&x = *(int*)&v.xX' then lwc1). A plain float assignment gives lwc1/swc1 and does not match. (2026-10-20)

- [relocation] [relocation] The retail GLI_vDisplayWorldTriangle/DrawAllIndexedSprites reach the viewport's world matrix through two levels of indirection: 'lw v0,0x68(vp)' then 'lw v0,0(v0)' (the viewport's p_vSpecificToXD points to a struct whose first word is a pointer), then +0x10 (the transformation matrix) or +0x20 (the other matrix used by the VU0 block). GLD_tdstViewportAttributes_.p_vSpecificToXD must therefore be a pointer, not the extractor's int. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC compiles a small switch as a comparison chain that tests the case values in DESCENDING order while laying the case bodies out in ASCENDING source order (JFFTXT_vExtendText case 7 and case 8); a dense 9-case switch instead uses a jump table. An if/else-if chain over the same values produces a different test order (bnez on the first condition), so write the branches as a switch to reproduce retail code. (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail materialises the innermost array-element offset even when it is zero: 'addiu rX,rX,0' appears between the array base and the scaled index of every inner element access (IPT_Pad.c: a_stDevice[ucPadNumber+2].hJoystickButtons[ucButtonNumber] and .hKeyButtons[ucButtonNumber] in all five of IPT_fn_vSetKeyAndPadButtonForPadNumber, IPT_fn_bIsValidatedPadButton, IPT_fn_bIsInvalidatedPadButton, IPT_fn_bIsJustInvalidatedPadButton, IPT_fn_bIsJustValidatedPadButton). No MWCC build (3.0-011126, 3.0.1-020123, 3.0.3-020716, 3.0b38-030307) emits an addiu with a zero immediate from C: a zero char* offset is always folded, while a non-zero one becomes 'addiu base,base,OFF'. Tested ~40 spellings (plain arr[i], &arr[i], *(arr+i), ((int*)arr)[i], ((int*)((char*)arr+0))[i], struct-element .member at offset 0, pointer-to-element locals, dead-index idiom, volatile, peephole off, opt_* pragmas, -O2/-O4/speed): the addiu 0 never appears. The same idiom also makes retail add the scaled index base-first ('addu base,base,idx') where MWCC emits 'addu idx,base'. Treat the pair as a compiler-build residual. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0-011126, 3.0.1-020123, 3.0.3-020716, 3.0b38-030307) always branches a value-producing if/else to the SECOND (else) source block and keeps the first block as the fall-through (probe: 'if (x<0){return 1;} else {return 2;}' -> 'bgez x,end' with 'li v0,2' in the delay slot; 'if (x<0){return 1;} return 2;' -> 'bgez x,next' with 'li v0,1' in the delay slot). The retail Rayman 3 code sometimes does the opposite: it branches to the THEN path with the true value in the delay slot (IPT_fn_bIsJustValidatedPadButton: 'blez ks,ret1 [delay: li v0,1]', 'beq ks,1,ret1' where the then block is the first source block). Inverting the source condition reproduces the retail branch sense but then the shared tail block is duplicated instead of tail-merged, so the retail shape is not reachable from plain C with the available builds; it is a compiler-build difference, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] For an unsigned-char boolean helper, 'if (cond) { return 1; } else { return 0; }' makes MWCC PS2 materialise the comparison as a boolean ('nor rX,rX,zero; sltiu rX,rX,1') instead of comparing with a constant, while 'return (cond) ? (unsigned char)1 : (unsigned char)0;' keeps the comparison form ('li v0,-1; bne rX,v0,...'). The retail IPT_Pad.c else-branches (else if (hPadButton != NULL) / else if (param0 != NULL)) use the comparison form plus two unreachable value copies that MWCC deletes, so the ternary spelling matches the retail structure best. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: taking a global's address through a char* base with a +0 addition ('(T*)((char*)&g + 0)') turns the address into a computed, non-rematerialisable value, so MWCC keeps it in an argument register and materialises it in the prologue (lui a1,%hi(g) / addiu a1,a1,%lo(g)) instead of rematerialising the plain address at the call. It also stops the 'addiu' from being coalesced in place on the pointer parameter. Matched CollSet.c CAM_fn_vLoadCameraConstants byte-for-byte (424/424, lines ok); the plain &g / local-pointer forms give 424 bytes with the memcpy source address computed at the call site instead. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] Correction to the 2026-10-20 CollSet note: CAM_fn_vLoadCameraConstants stores 1.0f (0x3f800000), not 4.0f, into xDynSpeed_TargetMulPerso at +0x6c - the retail keeps the same register (v0 = 1.0f, materialised at +0x164) for +0x6c, +0x74 (xCutAngleFactorPos) and +0x78 (xCutAngleFactorTgt). Only +0x64 (xDynSpeed_TargetFactNorm) holds 4.0f. With the correct constant the function matches 424/424. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail code (PS2GL_BufferControl.c PS2glMotionBlur) lowers a signed 'x/2' as 'srl rd,rs,1; andi at,rs,1; or rd,rd,at' (logical shift plus a sign fix-up) and only on the negative path, while MWCC 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3 emit 'addiu rd,rs,1; sra rd,rd,1'. This is a retail-compiler-build difference that blocks any function using a signed division by 2. (2026-10-20)

- [relocation] A large member offset inside a global (e.g. g_PS2glContext+0x5FA8) is encoded by the retail as 'lui at,0x39' plus a NEGATIVE 16-bit displacement (-0x3678) - that is just the ordinary HI16/LO16 pair whose sign-extended low half is negative, so the matcher's symbol+addend relocation patch reproduces it; no special source spelling is needed. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail 'select one of two pointers, then store it' code (one load + one store at a join) comes from a NESTED TERNARY, not from an if/else-if/else chain: 'p_stValue->uDummy.uParam.p_stEngObjValue = h == 0 ? 0 : (h->ulTypeOfLinkedObject != 2 ? 0 : h->hLinkedObject.p_stCharacter);' is if-converted into one pointer phi plus a single shared store, while the equivalent if/else-if/else with three assignments emits three separate stores. Verified in CAM_Fnct.c: CAM_fn_p_stGetTargetedSuperObject (296/296) and CAM_fn_p_stGetCameraOfViewport (284/284) matched with the nested ternary, and the if/else chain version produced 3 stores / wrong branch polarity (beqz vs bnez). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail code that reads a vector through a NULL pointer ('lw a0,0($zero); lw a0,4($zero); lw a0,8($zero)' - the PS2 scratchpad at 0 reads as 0) comes from an int-word copy through a null pointer: '((unsigned int*)((MTH3D_tdstVector_*)0))[0..2]' or '((unsigned int*)p_stNullVector)[i]' with p_stNullVector initialised to 0. Assigning literal 0 to the components instead ('*(int*)&x.xX = 0;') compiles to 'sw zero' and does NOT match (cf. Fields.c fn_ucGetFieldAbsoluteAxisZ). Matched CAM_Fnct.c CAM_fn_p_stComputeTargetWithTgtPerso (268/268) with the null-pointer read form. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The extracted local type can be narrower than the retail's: CAM_Fnct.c's 'unsigned short uwMemoFlags' (a saved copy of tdstInternalStructurCineinfo_.uwIAFlags) must be a 32-bit local ('unsigned int'), because the retail has no sign-extension around the '| 1' (lhu s0 / ori v0,s0,1 / sh) while MWCC emits dsll32/dsra32 for a 16-bit local. Check the retail's load/store widths before trusting the extracted local type. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 picks the load width of a masked unsigned short by the mask: 'x & 0xEFFF' compiles to a SIGNED 'lh' (the mask clears the sign bit, so the zero-extension is provably redundant), while '(unsigned int)x & 0xEFFF' keeps the unsigned 'lhu'. Retail CAM_Fnct.c CAM_fn_p_stComputeTargetWithTgtPerso uses 'lhu' plus 'andi 0xEFFF' and matches only with the explicit unsigned cast. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The splat listing's per-function boundaries can be wrong: asm/.../TEMPGRP/IPO/IPO_RLI.s lists 0x198 bytes for IPO_fn_vSetAddRLITable although the function is 0x11C bytes, so the tail of that block (the .L002F2D04/.L002F2D20 code) actually belongs to IPO_fn_vSetChangeOfRLITable, which starts at 0x2f2d80. Reading the listing as-is makes SetAdd look like it takes three extra parameters (a3/t0) and does a modulo on them. The authoritative listing is the one the verifier prints as TARGET in 'match.py check FUNCTION --diff', which is decoded straight from SLKA_250.78; the .relmain section of that ELF also gives the exact symbol of every relocation (e.g. SetAdd's +0x50 is 'c.eq.s $f0,$f12' and its +0x84 store is IPO_g_fCurrentIncrementRatio). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 cannot define a conversion operator out of line: 'CResData* CIdObjPtr<CResData>::operator CResData*() const {...}' fails with 'illegal operator' (also for an explicit template<> specialization), so a class template's conversion operator can only be defined in the class body - and then it is inlined into every use and NO out-of-line copy is emitted (an explicit 'template class X<Y>;' instantiation emits nothing either, in 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/b38). The retail does ship an out-of-line copy of usnd::CIdObjPtr<usnd::CResData>'s conversion operator (0x00187A60) in a unit that never calls it, because the retail compiler emits a copy of every member of an instantiated class template. Taking the ADDRESS of the member forces the copy: 'typedef usnd::CResData* (usnd::CIdObjPtr<usnd::CResData>::*PFN)(void) const; static PFN p = (PFN)&usnd::CIdObjPtr<usnd::CResData>::operator usnd::CResData*;' makes MWCC emit the out-of-line body with the exact retail name __opPQ24usnd8CResData__Q24usnd27CIdObjPtr<Q24usnd8CResData>CFv (matched 32/32, lines ok, SND_R3/sndeeldbin.cpp). The same cast trick works for a pointer-to-member of any conversion operator. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] CodeWarrior mangles a nested class used as a template argument as '<parent-mangling><index>_<name>', where the index is 4 for the first nested class, 6 for the second and 8 for the third (the number of preceding DATA members and of base classes does not change it; verified with a compile probe). The retail usnd STL therefore needs: usnd::stree<K,V,KFN,CMP,ALLOC> with three nested classes (_Kfn, const_iterator and iterator, so that iterator is '8iterator'), usnd::sset<K,CMP,ALLOC> with one nested class _Kfn ('4_Kfn'), usnd::sallocator<K>, a GLOBAL (not usnd) comparator class CLesser, and usnd::streebase with the nested sTreeLink and iterator_base. That declaration set reproduces the retail names byte-for-byte: _Lmost__Q24usnd156stree<Q24usnd5CUUID,Q24usnd5CUUID,Q34usnd63sset<Q24usnd5CUUID,7CLesser,Q24usnd25sallocator<Q24usnd5CUUID>>4_Kfn,7CLesser,Q24usnd25sallocator<Q24usnd5CUUID>>Fv, _GetHead__...CFv, erase__...FQ34usnd156stree<...>8iteratorQ34usnd156stree<...>8iterator (the two iterators are passed BY VALUE - a pointer parameter would mangle with an extra 'P'), __ct__Q34usnd9streebase13iterator_baseFPQ34usnd9streebase9sTreeLink and _Inc__Q34usnd9streebase13iterator_baseFv. MWCC needs 'class' members declared public and cannot spell a nested type of a typedef'd template ('typedef X::_Tr T;' is a syntax error), so spell the template type out. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] SND_R3 usnd::CPS2FileSrcFlow is 0x28 bytes in the retail: virtual-base-offset pointer at +0x0, vtable pointer at +0x4, members m_uiFileHandle..m_bUseCache at +0x8..+0x1f and the virtual base CFlow at +0x20. Declaring it as 'class CSrcFlow : public virtual CFlow' plus 'class CPS2FileSrcFlow : public CSrcFlow' with a CFlow that is 8 bytes (vptr + one int) reproduces that layout exactly (sizeof = 0x28, m_uiFileHandle at +0x8, m_bUseCache at +0x1c), while the extracted flat struct gives 0x24 and shifts CTextFile.mostringName (and therefore CTextFileSrc.mchBuffer) 4 bytes low. CFlow is 8 bytes because the extractor shows 'struct CFlow { int data[2]; }' in sndeectextfilesrc.cpp. (2026-10-20)

- [relocation] MWCC PS2 -strings reuse merges identical string literals, but a literal with an explicit trailing NUL ("%s\n%s\0", 7 bytes) becomes a SEPARATE anonymous object. Giving two identical format strings distinct spellings lets each HI16 pair with its own LO16 in the verifier: the verifier pairs an anonymous HI16 with the NEAREST FOLLOWING LO16 of the same symbol, so two HI16s sharing one literal leave the second LO16 unresolved and double-patch the first (the LO16 addend is applied twice). Reproduced in PS2MC FillIconSys (MATCH 432/432, 36/36 relocs, lines ok). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 attributes the branch that leaves a single-statement if/else arm to the arm's closing-brace line: writing the if and else arms with braces on their own lines adds the retail's line-table row at the branch (the arm's code stays on the 'if' line). PS2MC FillIconSys matches with braces, and the same body without braces loses the row at the branch. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 aggregate copy depends on the TYPE's alignment, not the address: a 16-byte struct declared __attribute__((aligned(16))) is copied with one lq/sq quadword pair, but the same struct with 4-byte alignment is copied as four lwc1 loads into f3/f2/f1/f0 followed by four swc1 stores through a materialized destination base pointer (addiu vX,sp,off). Reproduced in matsprot.c MAT_fn_vRotateAroundAxisNoTranslation (the retail's stack temporaries are 4-byte aligned, so the local needs its own unaligned 4-float type while the transformation's vector type stays aligned(16) for the other functions in the file). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 homes a MODIFIED floating-point parameter into a callee-saved FPR at the function entry, and the copy is attributed to the function's declaration line: source '_xAngle = 0.5f * _xAngle;' compiles to 'mov.s f20,f12' (entry, line of the signature) followed by 'mul.s f20,f0,f20', while reading the parameter directly gives 'mul.s f20,f0,f12' with no entry copy. Reproduced in matsprot.c MAT_fn_vRotateAroundAxis(NoTranslation); the 1.0f*angle operand order (constant first) matches the retail mul.s. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The retail's switch-case ORDER is the SOURCE order, not the enum order, and the compiler lays the case blocks out in that order while the jump table stays indexed by the enum value: in matsprot.c MAT_fn_vRotateAroundAxis the ComplexRotationScale case is written before the Rotation/RotationZoom/RotationScale group, so its block sits between the Scale block and the shared Rotation block even though its enum value (8) is the highest. Reading the target's block order off the jump table and reordering the source's case labels reproduces the retail layout exactly. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: a local declared with __attribute__((aligned(16))) occupies a whole 16-byte stack slot (the frame grows by 16 per such local), so a function with N aligned locals lays them out at 16-byte strides. The retail Rayman 3 COL/CsSphElt.c functions have exactly that layout (locals at 0xa0, 0xb0, 0xc0, 0xd0 ... in a 0x140 frame), and the attribute reproduces it; without it MWCC packs the locals densely (2-byte shorts next to each other, 4-byte ints next to each other) in every 3.0.x build. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 inline asm: an operand must be a plain pointer variable. A deep member address (&p->a.b.c) is rejected with 'illegal addressing mode', and taking the address of a local directly in an asm operand ('lqc2 vf1, 0x0(&local)') fails with "'local' could not be assigned to a register". Both work when the address is first stored in a pointer local (p = &local) and the asm uses that pointer; the pointer form also keeps the local in memory, which is what the retail VU0 code does. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 variable-length byte decoding: 'if (byte < 0x80) { v = byte & 0xFF; p++; } else { l = (short)(byte & 0xFF); l = (short)((int)l << 8); l = (short)((int)l + p[1]); v = (short)(l & 0x7FFF); p += 2; }' reproduces the retail's dsll32/dsra32-by-16 sign extensions, the 32-bit sll shift and the addu of the two bytes; the same shape with a plain int shift emits a 64-bit dsll and a daddu instead. The retail stores the first decoded value as a short and the second one as a long, and converts the long back to a short at the use site (dsll32/dsra32 by 16). (2026-10-20)

- [abi] Retail COL/CsSphElt.c ABI detail: COL_fn_bCollideStaticSphereWithStaticIndexedTriangle takes an unused second argument (both retail call sites pass a zero in a1 and the callee overwrites a1 immediately), so the debug-extracted one-parameter signature is incomplete; the call site needs an explicit second argument to match. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 picks the addressing form of a stack local from the SYNTAX of the access: a struct member access folds the offset into the load/store displacement (sw v1,0xa4(sp)), an array-element access with a constant index materialises the element address in a callee-saved register (addiu s8,sp,0xa4 / sw v1,0(s8)) and a punned access '*(int*)&local.member' materialises it as well but with a fresh address per use (addiu a1,sp,0xa4 / lw a0,0(a1)). A local that retail reads both as floats and as words therefore needs several views of one object: CAM_fn_vChangeAVectorParameter matches 456/456 with 'union { struct { unsigned int uiX, uiY, uiZ; } stWords; struct { float fArray[3]; } stFloats; unsigned int uiArray[3]; } stActualValue;' where the float case uses stFloats.fArray[] (materialised addresses), the word copies use stWords.uiX/Y/Z (folded) and the output copies use uiArray[] plus punned reads. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The extracted local list of an MWCC debug record is often the exact REVERSE of the original declaration order, and the order decides the callee-saved register assignment: in CAM_Proc.c all four reconstructed procedures (CAM_fn_p_stActivate, CAM_fn_p_stShowInfo, CAM_fn_p_stChangeChannel, CAM_fn_vActivateCamera) matched byte-for-byte only with the locals declared in reverse of the extracted order (e.g. hCineInfo, hCineInfoCurrent, lChangingMode, lTypeOfChange, cChannelNumber, stValue), which reproduces the retail's s0=hCineInfo, s1=lChangingMode, s2=lTypeOfChange, s3=cChannelNumber allocation. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 register allocation: a read-modify-write through a pointer field keeps the value in the load's register ('lw v1,0x30(v1); and v0,v1,v0; sw v0,0x30(v1)'), while routing the value through a named unsigned-int local copies it into a fresh register first ('lw v1,...; lw a0,...; and v0,a0,v0; sw v0,...'), which is the retail shape in AI/AIGame/Procedu1.cxx fn_p_stActivationZDR. The local must be named in the source for the copy to appear. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 copies a 96-byte 16-byte aligned transformation with six lq/sq quadword pairs only when the type is a single nested struct of 16-byte aligned vectors ('struct { MTH4D_tdstVector_ v[6]; }'); a float[24] member is copied with an 8-byte loop and a multi-member struct is copied member-wise (the 12-byte padding array is even copied byte by byte). Reproduced in AI/AIGame/Procedu1.cxx AI_vProcessSetPersoWithPerso (whole-transformation copy of the global matrix). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] Rayman 3 AI_tdstBatchOrder_ (AI/AIGame/Procedu1.cxx, 36-byte stride) is a union at +0x4 of {HIE_tdstSuperObject_* hRefPerso; unsigned char ucRefChannel;} and {six orientation floats}, followed by unsigned short uwChannel (+0x1c), unsigned char ucChannel (+0x1e), a padding byte (+0x1f) and unsigned char ucBatchOrderType (+0x20); the batch order pointer itself is at +0. fn_p_stBatchSetPersoOrientation writes the orientation floats while fn_p_stBatchSynchroPersoWithPerso writes the reference perso into the same union. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 needs a float* view for a component of a union member: 'stParam.uParam.stVectorValue.xY' makes the compiler materialise the field address (addiu reg,sp,off) and read through it, while '((float*)&stParam.uParam.stVectorValue)[1]' folds the offset into the load displacement (lwc1 fd,4(base)) as the retail does (AI/AIGame/Procedu1.cxx fn_p_stBatchSetPersoOrientation). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] The (unsigned int)float conversion is inlined by MWCC 3.0.3-020716 (lui 0x4f00/mtc1/c.le.s 2^31/trunc.w.s/mfc1/or 0x80000000) but lowered to a jal fptoui by 3.0.1-020123, so a game file that contains one such cast needs the 3.0.3 build while a file whose loop back-branch needs the 3.0.1 8-byte loop padding needs the 3.0.1 build - AI/AIGame/Procedu1.cxx currently needs both (fn_p_stReleaseAnalogJoystick is 3.0.3-only, AI_vProcessSetPersoWithPerso is 3.0.1-only). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0.3-020716 vs the 3.0.1-020123 game baseline (MEC/TEMPGRP sources): two retail code shapes need the later build. (1) A '%hi(global)' address setup hoisted into a conditional branch's delay slot: MEC_fn_lComputeCall has 'lui at,%hi(g_stEngineStructure)' in the beqz delay slot; 3.0.3 reproduces it, 3.0.1 leaves the lui at the branch target (4 extra bytes). (2) A float constant materialised as 'lui v0,0x3f33 / ori v1,v0,0x3333' (temp + move) instead of 'lui v1 / ori v1,v1'. With both shapes MEC_fn_lComputeCall is byte-exact (312/312, 4/4 relocations, retail line map ok) under 3.0.3 and 316/312 under 3.0.1; MEC_fn_vGroundGuess is 420 bytes under 3.0.3 vs 424 under 3.0.1 (target 416). MECBhv.c therefore looks like a 3.0.3-built unit. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail flag updates in MEC/TEMPGRP are stored TWICE per update, with a single load: MEC_fn_vBaseObstacleManagement writes 'ulEndFlags |= 0x10' as lw/ori/sw/sw and the else-branch 'ulEndFlags &= 0xFFFF0000' as lw/lui/ori/and/sw/sw. Routing the value through an unsigned-int local and assigning the field to that local twice ('ulFlags = field | 0x10; field = ulFlags; field = ulFlags;') reproduces the duplicate store; a plain 'field |= 0x10' produces a single store. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The retail DWARF local lists of the MEC/TEMPGRP units are incomplete and must not be used as the full local set: MEC_fn_lComputeCall lists only lCall and lNbFrame although three float differences have to stay live across the fn_ulStandardGameGetCustomBitsSO call in -, and MEC_fn_vGroundGuess lists only xNorm/stVector/p_stNormal/hCurrentMatrix although the delta and normal components also live in FPRs. Reconstructing the missing locals as plain floats is what reproduces the retail register allocation. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail PS2GL PS2GSPackage.c writes whole 'if (g_gsContext.ModFlgs.bits.X_Modified) { ... }' register-update blocks on ONE source line: the retail line map has a single entry per block (e.g. GsUpdateLimited +0x00d8 -> line 524), so a candidate must put the entire block (and the following reset statements) on one physical line to reproduce the retail line offsets. GsUpdateLimited matches 984/984 bytes, 76/76 relocs AND the retail line map with that layout. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 packs 1-bit bitfields into BYTE storage units regardless of the declared base type (unsigned char/int/long all give the same 'lbu'/'sb' access), and a 15-bit field like the GIF tag NLOOP gets a 16-bit unit. The retail PS2GSPackage.c therefore accesses g_gsContext.ModFlgs through byte accesses at +0x1b2/+0x1b3/+0x1b4; the recovered 'unsigned long : 1' declaration reproduces the retail's masks exactly (e.g. ~0x8, ~0x10, ~0x2, ~0xF0 for PRMODECONT/PRMODE/ALPHA_1/FOGCOL). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 inline asm accepts the R5900 COP2 GPR-mode instructions and emits the exact retail encodings: 'pand $t2, $t2, $s0' -> 0x71505489, 'por $t2, $t2, $s2' -> 0x715254A9, 'qfsrv $t5, $t3, $t2' -> 0x716A6EE8, 'mtsab $zero, 0xC' -> 0x0418000C, plus the trapping 'add $s3, $s3, $v1' -> 0x02639820 and the quad lq/sq. All verified against the raw ELF of GLI_vUpdateCharacterVAReal (SLKA_250.78, 0x002bdcd0). WARNING: the splat-generated asm/*.s files print those instruction words BYTE-SWAPPED (e.g. 0x89545071 instead of 0x71505489), so compare against the ELF bytes, not the .s listing; spimdisasm then mislabels the operand names. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] The retail 'unfolded element offset' form (lui/addiu base,OFF then addu base,base,idx*stride, load at 0) is reproduced by taking the field ADDRESS as a char* and adding the scaled index: '*(PO_tdstPhysicalObject_**)(void*)((char*)&pTable[0].h_Target + iIndex * sizeof(tdstObjectsTableElement_))'. A direct 'pTable[iIndex].h_Target' read folds the +OFF into the load displacement (lw 4(base)), while the char* form keeps the offset separate (MOR/TEMPGRP/specif/morph.c MOR_fn_xStopMorph, retail 472/472 bytes with only a v0/v1 temp allocation difference left). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 narrows a 16-bit loop counter twice: at the increment (dsll32/dsra32) and at the comparison (dsll32/dsra32).
