Casting the loop BOUND to short as well ('i < (short)GLI_lGetVisualSetNumbertOfLOD(...)') adds a THIRD narrowing pair and 8 bytes that retail does not have; cast only the loop variable, leave the bound an int (MOR_fn_vMorphTo2 in MOR/TEMPGRP/specif/morph.c, 588/588 MATCH). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 keeps a redundant nested test and emits a SECOND branch for it: 'if (cond) { if (cond) { body } }' produces the outer test plus an identical inner test with its own delay slot (retail TBF_vClose 0x002be6a0: bltz a0 at +0x0c and a second bltz a0 at +0x14; matched 108/108). This reproduces the 'two tests of the same global' shape that also appears as two consecutive 'if (TBF_g_UseTBFWriteCache != 0)' statements in TBF_vCreateEmptyTexture/TBF_vWriteTexture (the second test reloads the global after TBF_ChangeCacheSize). Prefer an explicit redundant nested if over a single merged if when the retail re-tests the same value. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The retail's PS2GL driver code in u_texture.c (GliGlou/Drivers/PS2GL/Src) uses ROLLED loops: the 100-trip GS wait loops in GLI_vUpdateCharacterVAReal (0x002bdcd0) are 7 instructions per iteration, while the project baseline ('-pragma optimize_for_size off', speed) makes MWCC unroll them by 8 into a main loop plus a remainder. A function-scoped '#pragma optimize_for_size on' restores the rolled shape (candidate 1552 -> 1164 bytes). This file is PS2GL library code living under the game source root, so it inherits the game speed flags; the same per-file compiler/flag question as u_VertexBuffers.c applies. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 does NOT fold '2.0f * x' into 'x + x': it materialises the constant with lui 0x4000/mtc1 and multiplies. Retail's add.s f,f,f doubling means the original source spelled the doubling as an addition ('x = x + x;' or 'x += x;'), not as a multiply by 2.0f (MOR/TEMPGRP/specif/morph.c GEO_fn_vMorphAddInMainLoop, the half-frame factor of the morph interpolation). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] The two spellings of a boolean ternary select different conditional moves in MWCC PS2 3.0.1: '(p != NULL) ? 8 : 1' compiles to 'movz rd,rs,rt' (the true value in rs) while '(p == NULL) ? 1 : 8' compiles to 'movn rd,rs,rt' (the false value in rd). They are semantically identical, so the retail's movz form tells you which spelling the original source used (MOR_fn_xStopMorph in MOR/TEMPGRP/specif/morph.c). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a 4-case switch on a masked value ('switch (Flags & 0xF)') as a beq compare chain whose tests run in REVERSE case order (retail TBF_vWriteTexture 0x002be500 tests 4,3,2,1) and generates NO default code: the default path branches directly into the post-switch block, so the case locals stay uninitialised (a real retail bug, reproduced by omitting the default case). The retail's case bodies also set the palette size only inside the 'Flags & 0x1000' branch. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 64-bit materialisation widths: a short->long widening is 'dsll32 rd,rs,0x10 / dsra32 rd,rd,0x10', an int->long widening is the same pair with shift 0x0, and an unsigned int->short conversion is 'dsll32/dsra32 0x10' with a SIGN extension (dsra32) - the low 16 bits are kept and sign-extended, so 'short b(unsigned int u){return u;}' compiles to exactly that pair (verified with a controlled probe). A short loop counter compared against an unsigned int bound gives 'sltu' while the counter stays 16-bit (addiu, no normalisation) - that combination reproduces the retail edge-search loop of ModifierVol.c. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail PS2GL shadow code (ModifierVol.c) lowers a short-circuit && used as an if-condition into a branch-based condition register ('beqz v0,L_false; ...; beqz v0,L_false; b L_true; L_false: move v1,zero; L_true: beqz v1,skip'), with the true value free in the register that already held 1. MWCC 3.0.1 cannot produce that form; the explicit-variable emulation ('flag=0; if(a){if(b){flag=1;}} if(flag){...}') gives the same shape but a different instruction count, so functions with this pattern (GLI_vComputeEdges, GLI_vDrawDynamicShadow) cannot be matched with the available builds. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 copy a 96-byte MAT_tdstTransformation_ (enum + 12 pad + 64-byte matrix + 16-byte vector) BLOCKWISE (6 lq/sq pairs) in C but MEMBER-WISE in C++ (lw/sw for the enum plus a byte-copy loop for the padding array, then lq/sq for the matrix). The retail's blockwise copy therefore proves the unit was compiled as C: add '#pragma cplusplus off' at the top of a .cxx unit (and drop the extern "C" blocks, which are then a syntax error). Verified on GAM/specif/ps2/initeng.cxx fn_vDesInitLevelLoop (0x262f30): with the pragma the four matrix copies match the retail's lq/sq sequence byte for byte, without it they are a byte loop. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 picks the comparison destination register from the spelling: 'x < N' gives 'slti v0,x,N' while the inclusive form 'x <= N-1' gives 'slti $at,x,N' (the scratch register). Retail GAM/specif/ps2/initeng.cxx fn_vInitLevelLoop (0x263390) compares the unsigned-char viewport counter with 'slti $at,v0,5', so the source must be 'uc_ViewPortIndex <= 4', not '< 5'. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a signed division by 2 to 'bgez x,+12 / sra rd,x,1 / addiu rd,x,1 / sra rd,rd,1' (both paths), while the retail compiler lowers it to a LOGICAL shift plus a conditional sign fix-up ('srl rd,x,1' then, only for x<0, 'andi at,x,1; or rd,rd,at') and additionally rewrites '(x/2)*2' to plain 'x' for x>=0. All four available MWCC builds use the bgez/sra form, so the retail's half-value idiom (six occurrences in GAM/specif/ps2/initeng.cxx fn_vTVS, 0x263e00) cannot be reproduced from the natural expression. An explicit 'if (x >= 0) f = (float)x; else { int h = (int)(((unsigned)x >> 1) | (x & 1)); f = (float)h; f = f + f; }' does reproduce the retail's instruction shape exactly (verified in a probe), but it does not fit inside the retail's surrounding expression. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] IPT_Pad.c boolean helpers (IPT_fn_bIsJustValidatedPadButton / IPT_fn_bIsJustInvalidatedPadButton) are a two-part expression, not the extractor's nested else-if guess: an early 'return 1' for the exact state (lState == 1 / lState == -1, guarded by the partner's state) followed by ONE shared tail expression 'return (hPadButton->lState > 0) ? 0 : ((param0->lState == 1) ? 1 : 0);' (resp. '< 0' and '== -1'), with the two else-branches written as unsigned-char ternaries 'return (hPadButton->lState == 1) ? (unsigned char)1 : (unsigned char)0;'. That shape reproduces the retail block for block (one shared tail block, the separate 'li v0,1'/'move v0,zero' materialisations and the andi at the exits); the nested else-if guess duplicates the tail, misses the andi and never reaches the retail's code size (236 vs 272 bytes). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail line tables map whole statement blocks to a single source line: SND_R3 PostponedPlay::fs_vSynchro's list-unlink block is one line (retail line 71) and fs_bPlay's whole else-block is one line (retail line 54). Writing those blocks on one source line in the candidate reproduces the target's line-table offsets exactly (fs_vSynchro 13 entries [0,0xc,0x14,0x50,0x54,0x5c,0x70,0x74,0x84,0xa0,0xac,0xd0,0xd4], fs_bPlay 9 entries) instead of one entry per statement, so the line map can be used as a source-layout constraint before tuning the code. (2026-10-20)

- [abi] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 keeps a call's argument registers untouched when the callee is declared with NO parameter ('fn_bDoTransition();' leaves a0 holding the previous call's argument). A retail call that passes a value to a callee whose debug record shows no parameters (fn_bDoTransition, fn_bLevelHasTransitionMemory, fn_lGetTransitionBloc) must therefore be declared without parameters, otherwise the candidate emits an extra 'daddu a0,v0,zero'. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail two-arm if/else branch polarity in MWCC PS2 3.0.1 is driven by the source spelling, not the logic: 'if (x != K) {A} else {B}' compiles to 'bne x,K,B_block' with A as the fall-through, while 'if (x == K) {A} else {B}' compiles to 'beq x,K,B_block'. GAM/specif/ps2/initeng.cxx fn_vInitLevelLoop (0x263390) branches with 'beq v0,a0,target', so the source is 'if (fn_lGetTransitionBloc() == 1) fn_vSetTransitionBloc(1); else fn_vSetTransitionBloc(2);'. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 sink a register copy to the END of its basic block (the exit branch's delay slot) when the copy's consumer is far away, while the retail build emits the same copy FIRST in the block (the entry branch's delay slot). In PostponedPlay::fs_vSynchro the inner then-block is 'move s0,v1' + 'sw zero,0(v0)' in retail and 'sw zero,0(v0)' + 'move s0,v1' in every available build, which costs two extra nops (240 vs 232 bytes). The source shape (a second pointer variable that is 0 until the inner branch assigns it) reproduces the retail's v1/s0 split, so the residual is a scheduling difference, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 register allocation follows the LOCAL DECLARATION ORDER: the first declared local gets $s0, the next $s1 and so on (GAM/specif/ps2/initeng.cxx fn_vDesInitLevelLoop: declaring 'int i;' before the two HIE_tdstSuperObject_* locals puts the counter in $s0, the 'next' pointer in $s1 and the 'current' pointer in $s2, exactly as the retail does; the extractor's debug local order is the reverse). The same order also decides which of two same-typed locals is swapped when a candidate is only EQUIVALENT. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0.x forwards a store to a global into a later load of the same global (PostponedPlay::fs_bPlay: 'ms_pFirst = e; if (!ms_pLast) ms_pLast = ms_pFirst;' compiles to one 'sw e,ms_pLast'), while the retail code reloads the global ('lw v0,ms_pFirst' + 'sw v0,ms_pLast' after 'sw e,ms_pFirst'). Retail 304 bytes vs our 300: the difference is exactly this redundant reload, and the rest of the function is byte-identical. Adding a duplicate 'ms_pFirst = e;' reaches 304/304 with a matching line map but different last instructions, so the reload cannot be produced by a natural source shape in these builds. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The PS2GL extractor renders every bitfield container as 'unsigned long' even when the retail source used unsigned char/unsigned short containers: the PS2GL tagGScontext PRMODECONT (8 bytes) and ModFlgs (8 bytes) are byte-accessed in retail (lbu/sb RMW of g_gsContext+0x98 and +0x1b2), which needs unsigned char containers plus explicit _extractor_padding bytes to keep the 8-byte stride. Redeclaring anon_18/anon_4 that way reproduced PS2glInit byte-for-byte (252/252). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 loop-bound spelling: writing an upward viewport loop as 'for (i = 0; i <= 4; i++)' instead of 'i < 5' makes the loop-back test compile to 'slti $at, i, 5' (the $at form) and rotates the loop so the increment 'addiu i,i,1' is hoisted to the top of the body and the test is scheduled between the body statements. Matched CAM_fn_vReInitCameras and CAM_fn_vInitCameras in ToolCam.c (256/256 and 336/336 bytes, exact line maps). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 deletes a local counter that is only incremented and never read, so a dead 'i++' in a loop produces no code. The increment survives (as 'addiu tX, tX, 1') when the same variable is also used as another loop's index somewhere in the function, because the compiler's dead-code pass is not flow sensitive. Used to reproduce the otherwise dead camera counter in CAM_fn_vReInitCameras (ToolCam.c) by reusing the viewport index variable 'i' for both loops. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail 'jump to test' list walks (the loop test at the top of the body, the back edge branching back to the test, the loop step executed on the exit path) are reproduced by 'if (p) { do { if (p) { body } step } while (p); }': an outer null guard, a do-while whose condition is the same pointer, and an inner null test around the body. Matched the camera-list walks in CAM_fn_vReInitCameras and CAM_fn_vCameraManagement (ToolCam.c). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Rayman engine bounds-check idiom for the 5-entry g_stEngineStructure.a_hViewportArray: 'if ((i >= 5) || (i < 0)) p = NULL; else p = &g_stEngineStructure.a_hViewportArray[i];' compiles to the retail's 'slti $at, i, 5; beqz $at, <null>; bgez i, <compute>' guard plus the strength-reduced 'base + i*16' address. The same idiom appears in ToolCam.c (5 camera functions) and ToolMatr.c (CAM_fn_hGetActiveCamera, CAM_fn_vAssignCameraToAViewport). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2: the debug local-variable list is in REVERSE declaration order, and both the frame slots and the callee-saved registers are allocated so that earlier declarations get the LOWER stack address and the LOWER s-register. Reversing the extractor's local list in fn_p_stSetSPOCoordinates (eProcId, stParam, hSpo, stCoordinatesBefore, p_stCoordinatesAfter, stTranslation, ucNbArrays) fixed the slot assignment (stParam 0x50, stCoordinatesBefore 0x60, stTranslation 0x70) and the s0/s1 choice (eProcId in s0, hSpo in s1) at once. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 materializes the destination address of a word-wise vector copy written through int lvalues (*(int*)&d->xX = *(int*)&s->xX) into a scratch register (addiu reg,sp,off + sw v,0(reg)), while the retail code folds the frame offset (sw v1,0x60(sp)). Declaring the destination local as a union with an unsigned int[3] view (auWords[0..2]) and copying through that view makes 3.0.1 fold the offsets and reproduces the retail bytes exactly; the float components are then read through the union's float view. Confirmed on fn_p_stSetSPOCoordinates (456/456 MATCH under the 3.0.1 baseline). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2: on an unsigned expression, 'x <= 3u' emits 'sltiu $at, x, 4' with 'bnez $at' while 'x < 4u' reuses the operand register ('sltiu $v0, x, 4'). The scratch-register choice therefore distinguishes the two spellings of the same range test; fn_p_stSetSPOCoordinates needs the '<= 3u' form for the retail's sltiu $at. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 callee-saved allocation flips parameter order when an extra named local carries a value across a call: in Objinit.c fn_vSnifThePlayerIsDeadWithResurrectionAt the retail prologue is 'daddu $s2,$a1; daddu $s1,$a0' (forward parameter order) while plain C gives the reverse ($s2=$a0, $s1=$a1). Adding 'MAT_tdstTransformation_* hGlobalMatrix;' and using it as '*(MAT*)SAI(...) = *(hGlobalMatrix = p->hGlobalMatrix);' makes the local share the dead parameter's saved register ($s1) and flips the parameter allocation to the retail order, matching 664/664 bytes with the exact line map. The assignment must stay inside the same statement (or on the same source line) so no extra line-map entry appears. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 stores the float constant 0.0f with an integer 'sw zero' instead of 'swc1' (verified with a standalone probe: 'p[0]=0.0f' gives 'sw zero,0(a0)'). This is how the retail zeroes float struct fields, e.g. tdstMSLight_.stOffsetVect.xX/xY/xZ and the GEO_tdstColor_ components in MSlight.c (fn_vLightAlloc, fn_pCreateCone). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 rounds float literals to nearest, so the epsilon '1.175495e-38f' becomes the denormal bit pattern 0x00800005 that the retail compares against (fn_xComputeAngleOfPerso); the same source with '1.0e-38f' would give 0x006CE3EE and mismatch. Probe-verified: 'lui 0x0080 / ori 0x0005'. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The retail's MSlight.c groups several statements on one source line and puts a block's closing brace on the same line as its last statement, so the retail line table has few entries. Reproducing the retail line offsets needs the same grouping: fn_vLightAlloc's 12 SAI/light calls share one line, fn_vLightInit's case body is one line, and fn_xComputeAngleOfPerso's closing braces sit on the statement's line (otherwise the compiler attributes the block's trailing branch to the brace line and the offset list differs). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 encodes an equality test against a non-zero constant as 'beq' (branch to the else arm) and never as the inverted 'bne'; the retail build emits the 'bne' form for the same source (fn_vLightInit's light-type test at +0x140). The two encodings are semantically identical, so a byte match needs the retail's form and cannot be produced by this build. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The retail tdstMSLight_ holds MTH3D vectors, not ints: stOffsetVect at +0x4 and stAngle at +0x10 (12 bytes each, as in GameDisp.c's copy of the struct). The extractor's 'int stOffsetVect + padding' variant gives the wrong size and breaks the SAI table registrations (SAI_fn_bInsertMTHVectorInTable(&hMSLight->stOffsetVect,...) needs the 12-byte vector at +0x4). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 cannot lower a signed 'x / 2' the way the retail build does (retail: srl/andi/or, evaluated only on the negative path, with the (x/2)*2 = x simplification for x >= 0; 3.0.1: addiu/sra on both paths). The natural C that reproduces the retail bytes under 3.0.1 is 'if (x >= 0) f = (float)x; else { int h = (int)(((unsigned int)x >> 1) | (x & 1)); f = (float)h + (float)h; }' - the unsigned shift keeps the logical srl, the 'else' arm keeps the block out of line, and '(float)h + (float)h' gives the retail's add.s instead of MWCC's mul.s. Verified in camera.cxx (GLI_vGet3DVertexFromScreenPos, GLI_xAdjustCameraToViewport2, GLI_vAdjustCameraToMirror all use it). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lays a two-arm if/else out as [then][else] and branches OVER the then-block ('bne cond,else'), while the retail camera.cxx emits 'beq cond,K,<case>' with the case block out of line and the code after the test as the fall-through. Writing the test as a one-case switch ('switch (x) { case 103: { ... } break; }') reproduces the retail's beq + 'b <after>' layout exactly; an if/else and a goto both keep MWCC's bne form. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 float quirks hit while reconstructing camera.cxx: (a) 'x * x + y * y' is contracted into the R5900 accumulator (madd.s/madda.s), so the retail's mul/mul/add needs the two squares in separate named locals; (b) a float constant division is NOT folded ('1.0f / 0.5f' emits div.s) but '4.0f / 3.0f' IS folded to 0x3FAAAAAB (1.3333334f), so a retail constant that looks like a ratio can be either; (c) 12 permutations of 11 float local declarations produced byte-identical code, so the local declaration order is not a lever for FP register-allocation differences. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The retail VU0 matrix*vector product (lqc2 vf1 / lqc2 vf2..vf5 / vmulax.xyzw / vmadday.xyzw / vmaddaz.xyzw / vmaddw.xyzw vf6 / nop nop / sqc2 vf6) appears verbatim in MatSp.h's MAT_fn_vApplyOnVertex (asm/Rayman3/.../public/Mat/MatSp__part4.s, word 0x4BE111BC..) AND in camera.cxx's GLI_vGet3DVertexFromScreenPos. It is not emittable from C and needs a statement-level inline asm block with float* operands (pVec = the float4 vector, pMat = the matrix columns); the retail even keeps the original's '' operand of vmaddw. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] PS2GL_VU1_Pipeline.c: the retail PS2GL library object for this file was built with MWCC 3.0.1, not the 3.0 library baseline. Evidence: PS2glFlushBuffer (0x184ff0, 596 bytes) compiles to 592 bytes under 3.0 (the switch setup loses the redundant 'lui $at,%hi(g_PS2glContext)' and the trailing nop) but to 596 bytes under 3.0.1, and every already-matched function of the file (VU1SetCurrentFrameInDMABuffer, VU1UpdateCullFaceParam, VU1ReUpdateMatrice, VU1UpdateDiffusePointLightingParam, VU1_RE_UpdateMatriceOutsideSDL) still matches under 3.0.1, while 3.0.3 breaks VU1SetCurrentFrameInDMABuffer (204/208). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 materialises the address of a punned stack local when the local is read as both a float and an int ('*(int*)&v.xY'); declaring the local as a union with an int[3] view ('union { MTH3D_tdstVector_ v; int i[3]; }') makes the integer reads fold to sp-relative displacements (lw v1,0x30(sp)) instead of addiu/lw through a register, which is exactly the retail code. Verified in MECCol.c MEC_fn_vReportCollideActor (228/228 MATCH): the retail copies a negated MTH vector into the report with 'swc1' stores and reads the bits back with 'lw v1,0x30(sp)'. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The retail MEC build copies instructions into an empty branch delay slot instead of moving them, and it copies the join block's first instruction into a branch delay slot while retargeting the branch +4. Evidence: MEC_fn_vCrashAnalysis (0x30CF70) keeps the flags store 'sw v1,0xc(a0)' AND duplicates it in the beqz delay slot, exits with 'b +0x130' whose delay slot holds the join block's 'ld ra', and repeats the flag store 'sw v1,0xc(s5)' twice; MEC_fn_vEdgeAnalysis (0x30D0C0) likewise stores ulEndFlags twice around its first branch. MWCC 3.0-011126, 3.0.1-020123, 3.0.3-020716 and 3.0b38-030307 all produce the same 316-byte body (store moved into the slot, single exit block), so no available build reproduces it - treat it as a retail-compiler-build residual rather than a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 register allocation follows the LOCAL DECLARATION ORDER, and the extracted debug local list is often the REVERSE of it. In MECCol.c MEC_fn_vCrashAnalysis the debug list is (p_stCeilObstacle, p_stGroundObstacle, p_stTmpObstacle, lCounter) but the retail's callee-saved assignment is counter=s0, tmp=s1, ground=s2, ceil=s3, i.e. the reversed order; declaring the locals as (int lCounter; DNM_stMecObstacle* p_stTmpObstacle; DNM_stMecObstacle* p_stGroundObstacle; DNM_stMecObstacle* p_stCeilObstacle;) reproduces the retail's s0..s3 exactly. The same reversal applied to MEC_fn_vReportCollision's uchar list fixes the 10 stack byte slots. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.3-020716 emits a store through an int-cast lvalue on a global as a one-shot constant address ('lui at,%hi(sym); sw v1,%lo(sym+off)(at)', one lui per member) while 3.0.1-020123 materialises the address in a register ('lui t2,%hi(sym); addiu t2,t2,%lo(sym+off); sw v1,0(t2)'). AICommonTools.c fn_CollisionRayCast (the word copy of two vectors into the engine's collision globals) matches 100/100 with the line map only under 3.0.3; all six previously recorded matches in that file still verify under 3.0.3, so the file meets the config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt criterion. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MECCol.c's retail code uses three R5900 FP idioms that are not emittable from C and need file-local MTH-style macros: (1) a 3-term dot product via 'mula.s/madda.s/madd.s' (MEC_fn_vReportCollision computes the normal's Z component as xX*0.0f + xY*0.0f + xZ*1.0f, with the 1.0f in f3 and the 0.0f in f4); (2) 'abs.s' for the |x|<0.001f null-vector tests (MEC_fn_vComputeAdjustVector, MEC_fn_vEdgeAnalysis); (3) 'sqrt.s' after a squared norm. The retail MTH_DOT-style helper writes the result back into the first operand's register (the retail reuses the xX register f0 for the dot result), so the macro's mula destination must be the result variable and the source must pre-load it with the first component. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3, 3.0b38) folds a negated float constant into one literal: 'x * -0.5f' becomes 'lui v0,0xBF00; mtc1' and 'mul.s f20,f0,f20', while the retail code materialises the positive constant and negates it at runtime ('lui v0,0x3F00; mtc1; neg.s f0,f0; mul.s f20,f20,f0'). Putting the half in a plain local float ('float xHalf = 0.5f; x = x * -xHalf;') reproduces the retail sequence exactly and needs no stack slot (MWCC folds the local into the register), so the negation survives; every constant-expression spelling tested (-0.5f, -(0.5f), -(1.0f/2.0f), (0.0f-0.5f), 0.5f*-1.0f) folds back to 0xBF000000. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 keep tracking a float constant that was stored to memory as an integer word: 'v.z = 0.0f' is emitted as 'sw zero' (the documented constant-float-as-int store) and the optimizer still folds the later 'v.z == 0.0f' test, deleting the store as dead (AICommonTools.c fn_xAngleVectorXOY drops to 412/584 with a plain assignment). The retail build loses the value at the integer store, so the test and the store survive; keeping the member opaque with '*(int*)&v.z = 0' reproduces the retail 'sw zero' plus the surviving test (588/584), but it makes the struct address-taken, which changes the alias analysis and the register allocation (the retail keeps the y components in callee-saved FPRs across the acosf call, our build keeps the component addresses in callee-saved GPRs instead). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 inline asm rejects a struct member as a floating-point operand even when the struct is a plain local: 'asm { mula.s v.xX, v.xX }' fails with 'illegal operand' (a pointer field gives 'illegal addressing mode'). The components must be copied into plain scalar float locals first - the engine's MTH_* macro style ('float _mthX = (_v).xX; ... asm { mula.s _mthX, _mthX }'). This refines the 2026-10-19 note that a struct field of a local is accepted: only the plain-variable form compiles, so the MTH_SQR3_TO / MTH_CROSS_COMPONENT_TO macros of the Rayman 3 engine must take the components through local floats. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 folds the member offset of a doubly-indexed access into the store displacement ('g[i].List[j].cActivity' -> 'sb a3,0x34(base)'), while the retail AI code keeps it unfolded ('addiu base,base,0x34; addu base,base,idx*stride; sb a3,0(base)'). The idiom that reproduces the retail form is a char* temp on the element: 'pc = (char*)pCheapList; pc = pc + 0x34; ((char*)pc)[idx * 48] = v'. Confirmed on ProcOptim.cxx fn_p_stActivateCheapCharacter and fn_p_stSetCheapCharacterPos; the plain member access and every address-of/cast spelling fold the offset back into the displacement. (2026-10-20)

- [tooling] m2c cannot decompile a function whose switch uses a jump table that the splat .s file does not define ('Unable to determine jump table for jr instruction' / 'jtbl list must not be empty'). Recover the table from the retail ELF (the .relmain relocations name the anonymous table symbol, e.g. @2128 or jtbl_0035A000, and its words are in .rodata), then run m2c on a TEMP copy of the .s file with the table appended as 'jtbl_XXXX: .word .L00XXXXXX' entries, adding the missing case labels (MWCC's .s labels do not cover every case entry) before each target instruction. Both Demos.c switch functions then decompile (m2c-seeds/fn_vDemoReadCompressedTransform.c and m2c-seeds/DEMO_fn_vReadAndTreatAllExtendedFlag.c). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lays out 'if (cond) { body }' as 'bnez cond,skip' with the body as the fall-through, while the retail AI code uses 'beqz cond,body' with the skip as the fall-through ('b epilogue' whose delay slot already holds 'ld ra'). Writing the guard as an early return ('if (cond) return p_stTree;') reproduces the retail layout exactly: ProcOptim.cxx fn_p_stSetCheapCharacterPos then matches the retail's branch, its epilogue and its 432-byte size (only the 843 branch's register allocation differs). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The retail AI code inlines a small callee into its caller and keeps the WHOLE inlined body on the call statement's source line (ProcOptim.cxx fn_p_stAddCheapCharacter: the inlined AddCheapCharacter body is all source line 543, and the inlined code is attributed to that line). A candidate that spells the body out must keep it on one source line or the verifier's line-offset check fails, and the 12-byte vector copy must be a point-of-use initializer ('MTH3D_tdstVector_ stPosition = stParam.uParam.stVectorValue;') so MWCC keeps it in memory (ld/sd + lwc1/swc1) instead of scalarising it into callee-saved FPRs. (2026-10-20)

- [relocation] The retail ELF's .relmain section (parsed with the symtab in .strtab) gives the exact symbol behind every lui/addiu/lw pair of a function, which resolves the retail's anonymous @N literals and its struct-member accesses. In Demos.c DEMO_fn_vGetACompleteCharacterPosition it proved that the IPT_fn_bIsValidated argument is g_st_3DOS_EntryActions + 0x7C (the hFlyingModeDoubleSpeed member of tdst_EntryActions_) and that the function's 16-byte matrix copy uses the retail's lq/sq form, and in DEMO_fn_bUpdateDynamicHierarchy it proved that the '-0x6E68()' float load is really g_iFrameTimeOffset (an int global read as a float) and that the 'sltiu at,v1,0x3D' test comes from the source spelling 'x > 0x3C'. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] CollSet.c: a 'char* base + dead scaled-index assignment' idiom ('char *pc = (char*)_hCollSet; pc = pc + 0x64; ... *(float*)((char*)pc + (iIndex = index * 4))') reproduces the retail's UNFOLDED indexed address (base addiu + index shift, addiu v0,a0,OFF / sll v1,v1,2 / addu v0,v0,v1) and its register choice (the widened short index stays in v1 and the shift is done in place, the base lands in a fresh v0). Plain array subscripting folds the member offset into the load instead (60/64 bytes). It leaves a 2-instruction order residual: retail emits the index scale before the base addiu, MWCC 3.0.1 emits the base addiu first; defining the index chain first in the IR hoists the scale into a branch delay slot, and moving the dead assignment to the base makes MWCC materialise the base as li+addu instead of addiu. fn_xCollSetGetColliderReal reaches 64/64 bytes with lines ok on this residual; fn_stCollSetGetColliderActor reaches the retail's register allocation but is 4 bytes short because MWCC sinks the base addiu into the bnez delay slot. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0-011126 vs 3.0.1 on ProcOptim.cxx fn_p_stActivateCheapCharacter: 3.0-011126 produces the retail's 176-byte body (beqz to the body, the first step of the index computation shared in the bne delay slot, the else branch reusing it) and the verifier calls it register-EQUIVALENT to the retail, while 3.0.1 duplicates the whole index computation in the else branch (180 bytes) and leaves the delay slot as nop. Another data point that the retail game build sits between MWCC 3.0 and 3.0.1, and that the 3.0 build is not reachable for game files with the current config. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail COL_tdstCollideObject_ element lists are a short* type array (d_xListOfElementsTypes, +0xc) and a pointer array (d_stListOfElements, +0x10), not the extractor's int fields: retyping them as short*/void** reproduces the retail 'addu base,offset' (type array) and 'lw element' (pointer array) addressing in INT_fn_bGetInclusionPointInCollideObject (MATCH 808/808). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 materialises a boolean flag stored in an 'unsigned char' local with 64-bit constants: 'flag = 0' becomes 'daddu vX,zero,zero' and 'flag = 1' becomes 'daddiu vX,zero,1' (a 64-bit register pair), while the same assignments in a 'long' local are narrowed to 32-bit 'addiu vX,zero,1'. Retail MAT code (matspscl.c MAT_fn_vSetScale/vSetZoom/vScale, matsp.c MAT_fn_bAreEquivalent, GAM/Engine.c fn_bEndOfGame) uses the 64-bit form, so the flag must be an unsigned char (an int/long flag does not reproduce the bytes). A bare 'if (flag)' test keeps no andi 0xff; an explicit '!= 0' adds one. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC's unsigned-int to float conversion is emitted as 'srl v,v,1' (logical) plus a 'bltz' correction that ORs in (v & 1) and multiplies the float by 2, i.e. a float expression whose operand is an unsigned int (not a signed division). The same 'bltz/srl/andi/or' shape in retail code is therefore an unsigned->float cast, not a division by two: writing (float)x where x is an unsigned field reproduces it, while (float)(x / 2) * 2.0f produces a different (signed division) sequence. (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 folds the address of the FIRST member of a local struct into the displacement (e.g. 'lwc1 f0,0x40(sp)' for &TmpMat.stCol_0) but materialises the other members' addresses in a register (addiu reg,sp,0x50 / 0x60 / 0x70), and it also materialises one address per component when the copy goes through a matrix pointer (p_stTmp->stCol_N.xX -> addiu per float). Retail MAT code (matspscl.c MAT_fn_vSetScale/vSetZoom/vRemoveScale ComplexRotationScale case) keeps ONE base register per column (addiu t0,sp,0x70 / a3,sp,0x40 / a2,sp,0x50 / a1,sp,0x60) with the four components at offsets 0/4/8/0xc, so the first column's base is never folded there. Tried: column pointers, float* column pointers, a matrix pointer, a 16-byte struct copy and 4 separate column pointers - none reproduces the retail base register, so this is a compiler-build difference in the first-member folding. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a two-arm float if/else whose arms are single register assignments by sinking the ELSE arm's assignment into the branch delay slot (bc1f -> join, 'move vX,zero' in the delay slot, the THEN assignment in the following branch's delay slot); the retail code instead keeps the else assignment in its own out-of-line block and puts the THEN assignment in the branch delay slot (bc1f -> else block, 'daddiu vX,zero,1' in the delay slot, 'b' to the join). Reproduced as a source difference in matspscl.c MAT_fn_vScale: with the arms in a memory (sb) the layout matches the retail, with register assignments it does not; the retail shape is not reachable from C with MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 or with the schedule/peephole/opt_* pragmas. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2MC/TXT line breaks are the two-character escape '\l' (backslash + 'l', bytes 5c 6c 00), NOT a newline: the retail .rodata at 0x367EA8 holds '\l', 0x367EB0 '\l\l', 0x367EB8 '\c255:255:0:', 0x367EC8 '\c125:125:125:', 0x367ED8 four spaces. PS2MC_Messages.cpp's Display and CalculateMessagePosition must therefore use "\\l" / "\\l\\l" in C (an escaped backslash), otherwise the candidate literal is 2 bytes instead of 3/5 and the HI16/LO16 relocation never resolves. This corrects the earlier note that read the same literals as "\n"/"\n\n" - the bytes are 5c 6c, not 0x0a. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2MC's message alpha is a constant, not a conditional: C_PS2MC_Message::Display calls TXT_vAfficheSpecialWithTransparency with 255.0f on both paths (the retail materialises 'lui v0,0x437F' once in the beqz delay slot and once in the jal delay slot and never passes 0.0f), so the extractor's guess 'cChoice3 != 0 ? 255.0f : 0.0f' is wrong - it costs 8 bytes (a mtc1 zero + a branch) and breaks the tail. The 20.0f/0.0f colour factor, in contrast, IS a conditional on m_pCurrentMessage->iNbChoice (retail: 'beqz v1' with 'lui v1,0x41A0' in the delay slot, then 'mtc1 v1,fv0f'). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail add-zero family, new evidence: the 'addiu reg,reg,0' is the materialised MEMBER/ELEMENT offset of the inner array, not a constant in the source. Retail AI/AIGame/ProcOptim.cxx ActivateCheapCharacter (0x229da0) computes 'g_aListOfCheapCharacterList[ulInd].List[ulIndChar].cActivity' as 'addu v1,v1,a1; sll a0,v1,4; addiu v1,a2,0x0; addu v1,v1,a0; sb a3,0(v1)' - the row address plus the 'List' member offset (0 in the retail) plus the scaled index; DefineMenu2D fn_vNextSelection (0x299d74) has the same 'addu v1,v1,a1; addiu v1,v1,0x0; addu v1,v1,a0; lb v1,0(v1)' shape. A controlled probe shows MWCC 3.0.1 DOES materialise a nested-array member offset when it is non-zero ('struct Row { int pad; Msg m[46]; }' gives 'addiu v1,v1,0x4') but folds it when it is zero - all four available builds (3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38) and ~25 spellings (2D/1D subscripts, char* and void* casts with an explicit +0, pointer-to-row locals, struct-of-array rows, nested-array members at offset 0, dead-index tricks) fold the zero. The add-zero also correlates with the operand order of the final add: the retail renders the address base-first ('addu v0,v0,v1' with the accumulator in rs) while MWCC renders it index-first ('addu v0,v1,v0'), so a missing add-zero also costs the operand order. Treat it as a retail compiler-build difference that blocks every 2-level array access. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail signed-division-by-2 semantics: the retail's 'srl rd,rs,1; andi at,rs,1; or rd,rd,at' fix-up computes FLOOR(x/2), not C's truncating x/2 (for x=-5 it yields -3 where C gives -2), and the retail additionally cancels the matching '*2' to plain x whenever x >= 0 - an unsound transformation for odd x. The value of '(float)(x/2)*2.0f' in the retail is therefore x for x >= 0 and 2*floor(x/2) for x < 0 (PS2MC CalculateMessagePosition 0x00331f20), while MWCC 3.0.1 computes trunc(x/2) in both paths ('bgez x,+12 / sra rd,x,1 / addiu rd,x,1 / sra rd,rd,1') and keeps the '*2' as a separate mul.s, so the two compilers disagree on the value itself and no source spelling bridges them. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 register allocation in an if/else depends on the ORDER of the assignments in the two arms, not only on the local declaration order: in ps2Threads.c's PS2_fn_vFlipThreadsPriority, reordering the four assignments (ft_id/ft_priority/bt_id/bt_priority) moved the address temporary from $s0 to $s1 and the first priority value into $s0, which made the first 58 of 216 bytes match the retail. A sweep of all 720 local-declaration orders and all 576 arm orders left a cyclic permutation of the four output variables over $s0-$s3, i.e. a register-allocation (not source-shape) residual. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 allocates the float temporaries of 'x = a + b * c' differently from the compound form: the longhand 'x = a + b * c' gives the temporaries f1/f0/f2 (product in f0, a in f1, b in f2) while 'x += b * c' gives the retail's descending f2/f1/f0 (b in f2, c in f1, a in f0, product in f1). Confirmed on Material.c GLI_vRefreshMultiTexturePart: switching the three 'x = a + b * coef' statements to 'x += b * coef' turned a 15-row register-allocation residual into a byte-exact 252/252 match with the retail line map. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] [debug-format] A retail call whose argument register is never written means the original source passed no argument for it: ps2Threads.c's sceTtyInit() is called with $a0 untouched even though the string "thpri=9,10" is loaded into a variable (s1) on the previous line - that variable is only used later by strlen(). Writing sceTtyInit(mes) adds a move and breaks the match. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 lowers a float while-loop test 'A > B' to 'c.le.s A,B' + 'bc1f <body>' (the body is the branch TARGET and the loop exit is the fall-through), so a retail 'bc1f' into the loop body means the source used '>' and not '<='. The '<=' spelling gives 'bc1t' to the exit instead. Reproduced in Material.c GLI_xRefreshAnimatedTexture (two 'while (coef - time > fFrames)' loops). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] A local that the retail keeps in the stack frame (spilled at its assignment and reloaded at its use) can be reproduced by declaring it 'volatile': MWCC then stores it to a stack slot instead of keeping it in a register, and the frame grows by the slot size (0x10 for a 4-byte local in Material.c GLI_xRefreshAnimatedTexture, frame 0x20 -> 0x30 with the local at sp+0x2c). Without it the value stays in a register (a1) and the reload of the other field forces an extra load. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 picks the delay-slot filler of a call with a nested call in its argument list: the setup that depends on the nested call's result (e.g. 'addiu a1,v0,1' after strlen) is moved into the jal's delay slot, while the independent setup (e.g. 'move a2,s1') stays before the call. In ps2Threads.c's PS2_fn_lInit the two identical sceSifLoadModule blocks (PADMAN, MCSERV) follow that rule and match, but the MCMAN block (the one right after a 'mes = "thpri=9"' reload) keeps the independent setup in the delay slot instead; no source spelling (argument order is fixed by the ABI, declaration orders, casts, a separate length local) reproduced the retail choice, so it is a compiler-build scheduling difference. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 line attribution for a braced block: the block's FIRST instruction is attributed to the line of the block's opening ('{' or 'else {') and its LAST instruction to the line of the closing brace. A retail row that starts one line after the block's opening row and ends on the closing-brace row is reproduced by putting the block body on the 'else {' line and the closing brace on the next. In Material.c GLI_xRefreshAnimatedTexture this produced the retail's 44-row map exactly (rows at +0x0cc on the body line and +0x0d8 on the closing-brace line, and the +0x154 alignment nop on the closing-brace line of a braced else). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail add-zero family, cleanest proof that the zero is a materialised member offset: DefineMenu2D fn_vNextSelection (0x299ca0) reads a char out of a page struct as 'lui v1,0x5D; addiu v1,v1,-0x61D2; sll a1,a2,3; addu a1,a1,a2; sll a1,a1,2; addu a1,a1,a2; sll a1,a1,4; addu v1,v1,a1; addiu v1,v1,0x0; addu v1,v1,a0; lb v1,0(v1)' - the +0 is the offset of the char array inside the 576-byte page struct (0 in the retail), it sits between the row address and the scaled selection index, and the element's own offset is 0 too (the 'lb 0(v1)' displacement). A non-zero member offset is materialised the same way ('addiu base,base,OFF'), so the retail always keeps the member address explicit while MWCC folds it when it is zero. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 inserts the sqrt.s hazard nop by itself when the following instruction is an FPU op, so a hand-written 'asm { sqrt.s }' helper must NOT carry an unconditional nop: CAM_Shooter.c fn_CreateRaycastDirection (retail 'c1 0x84; nop' before an mtc1) and fn_p_GetViewFinderDistance (retail 'sqrt.s' directly followed by a GPR move, no nop) are both matched by one nop-less MTH_SQRT_TO macro, while an explicit nop broke the latter. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC (game baseline -O3,p) materialises the address of every '*(int*)&local.member' target instead of folding it into the store offset: a three-word word-copy into a local struct costs one addiu per member (sp+off) and the stores then use 0(reg). A plain float member assignment or a struct assignment keeps the frame-base+offset form (0xNN(sp)) but copies with lwc1/swc1 instead of lw/sw, so retail code that copies floats as words while keeping folded stores is not reproducible with either spelling. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The MWCC debug extractor typed CAM_Shooter_.apMainActors/apSkins (offsets +0x74/+0x84) as int[4], but the retail loads and compares them as pointers. Declaring them as HIE_tdstSuperObject_*[4] restored the natural source (no char* casts needed), enabled MWCC's strength reduction of the loop index into a byte offset (retail 'addu a0,a1,a3' + 'lw v1,0x74(a0)'), and matched fn_p_RemoveMainActor byte-exactly while keeping fn_p_AddMainActor matching. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC folds a struct member read to frame-base+offset only when the member offset is 0; for the other members it materialises the element address into a GPR (addiu sp+off) and loads with 0(reg). Observed in MAT_fn_vRotateVector/MAT_fn_vApplyOnVector, where the retail loads the first copied float as 0xNN(sp) and the other two as 0(sp+off) with the two addresses computed up front. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC inlines float expressions with the R5900 accumulator ops adda.s/madd.s for a*b + c*d + e*f, so a matrix-vector product written in plain C reproduces the retail's adda.s/madd.s sequence (matsprot.c and matsp.c both rely on this); the 'transpose' element order (stCol_1.xX with src.xY) is a source-level indexing choice, not a layout issue. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 keeps a flag local in a callee-saved register only when it is live across a call: in CAM_Shooter.c fn_p_RemoveMainActor the retail saves s0/s1/s2 (frame 0x50) and holds 'bFound' in s0, which only happens when 'bFound = 0' is initialised BEFORE the first fn_p_stEvalTree call (the zeroing is then scheduled into that call's delay slot). Initialising it after the calls left bFound in a GPR (a2), dropped the frame to 0x40 and swapped the whole loop allocation. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail 'slti , reg, 3' in a branch delay slot (with the following nop) is the inclusive C spelling 'k <= 2', not 'k < 3' (MWCC uses a normal temp register and fills the slot for the latter). Reproduced in CAM_Shooter.c fn_p_RemoveMainActor's 'if (bFound && k <= 2)' test, which retails as 'beqz s0; slti at,a2,3; beqz at'. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 allocates callee-saved registers from the local DECLARATION ORDER (earlier declaration = higher register s6..s0) and stack slots from the reverse order; reversing the extractor's debug local list reproduced the retail register assignment and frame layout for INT_fn_bGetInclusionPointInCollideObject and INT_fn_bIntersectSegmentWithFaceOfGeometricObject (both MATCH). Start from the reversed debug local order, then tune the order of the first statements that touch a member. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Compiler-build residual (CAM_Shooter.c fn_p_GetViewFinderDistance, 212/212 bytes, EQUIVALENT): for the FIRST floating-point subtraction of a function the retail loads the first operand into the destination's register (target 'lwc1 f4,viewfinder; lwc1 f5,target; sub.s f4,f4,f5') while MWCC (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3 and 3.0b38 all identical) loads the SECOND operand there; the y/z pairs match exactly. A sweep of 216+ source variants (direct-difference vs preloaded components, 6 statement orders, 6 local-declaration orders, pointer-local forms, register keyword, four compiler builds) never reproduced the swap, so this looks like a retail-build FPR coalescing difference rather than a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38 all treat an explicit constructor call 'p->C()' (C = the class name) as constructing a TEMPORARY: the constructor is called with a0 = a stack temporary of the class size, and the object at p is never initialised. The retail build calls the constructor on the existing object (a0 = the pointer). Reproduced with a controlled probe (4 builds, identical output). Workaround that does call the constructor in place: 'new (p) C' plus 'inline void* operator new(unsigned int size, C* place) { return place; }' - MWCC then emits 'move a0, p; jal ctor' (plus the new expression's own null check and a callee-saved register for p, where the retail keeps p in a temp across the call). See u_LoadingScreen.cpp LS_Create (PS2GL driver). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2's unsigned x/2 lowering differs from the retail's by one halving: MWCC emits {t = x>>1 (srl); if (t<0) {r = 2*((t>>1)|(t&1))} else {r = t}} and multiplies by 2 afterwards, while the retail lowers '2.0f*(float)(x/2)' as {if (x<0) {r = 2*((x>>1)|(x&1))} else {r = (float)x}} - i.e. the retail tests the sign of the ORIGINAL x, uses the original x in the non-negative path (the '(x/2)*2 = x' simplification) and needs only one shift. All four MWCC builds (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3, 3.0b38) agree on their own form, so an unsigned division by 2 that is immediately doubled cannot be matched with the available compilers; the signed form uses the sra-based sequence. See u_LoadingScreen.cpp LS_FinallyIThinkIOnlyHaveThisSizeLeftToLoad. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL tagGScontext (the game's copy, e.g. u_LoadingScreen.cpp): the retail accesses g_gsContext+0xA0 (PRMODE) and g_gsContext+0x1B2 (the third byte of ModFlgs) with lbu/sb, so PRMODE's bitfields (TME = bit 4, ABE = bit 6, so the byte holds PRIM/IIP/TME/FGE/ABE/AA1) and the ModFlgs flags 16-23 (PRMODE_Modified = bit 20) must live in unsigned-char containers; with 'unsigned long' containers MWCC emits word accesses instead. The ModFlgs flag names come from the PS2GL source's anon_36 (PS2GL_EnableDisable.c) and the retail's 0x1B2 byte is set twice in DisplayWheelWork. See also the existing note about the ModFlgs flags 32-39. (2026-10-20)

- [relocation] The PS2GL driver file u_LoadingScreen.cpp is C++ but its free functions (LS_Create, LS_Destroy, LS_Process, LS_FinallyIThinkIOnlyHaveThisSizeLeftToLoad, LS_fn_iRemainingSizeToLoad) have C linkage in the retail ELF (unmangled symbols) while the C_LoadingScreen methods are mangled ('__ct__15C_LoadingScreenFv'). Declaring them 'extern "C"' makes the candidate's symbol names match the retail's, which is required when one of them is referenced from another function: the verifier resolves a relocation by symbol NAME, so a reference to a mangled 'LS_Process__Fv' would stay unresolved. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Rayman 3's camera code (CAM_Base.c) computes vector norms with the R5900 accumulator ops mula.s/madda.s/madd.s plus sqrt.s; MWCC 3.0.1-020123 lowers the plain C 'x*x+y*y+z*z' to mul.s/adda.s/madd.s and sqrtf() to a call, so the original source used MTH-style asm macros (the same MTH_SQR3_TO/MTH_SQRT_TO shape as MEC/DNMCame.c). Reproduced CAM_fn_vComputeRefAxis (768/768, lines ok) with file-local CAM_MTH_SQR3_TO / CAM_MTH_SQRT_TO / CAM_MTH_NORM3_TO / CAM_MTH_COPY3_TO macros. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 does not model the write of a statement-level inline-asm block to its operand: after 'asm { mula.s _x, _x }' the compiler still believes the register holds the pre-asm value, so a later load of the same address is CSE'd away (the retail reloads it). Copying a vector with a struct assignment ('*dst = *src') avoids the CSE and also yields the retail's load-load-load / store-store-store shape, which per-component assignment does not. Confirmed on CAM_fn_vComputeRefAxis (CAM_Base.c): the retail's else-branch copies the three floats with three lwc1 then three swc1, while component-wise assignment produced lw/sw or a reused register. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail Rayman 3 camera code keeps a z-difference in a register across a normalisation: the norm's third component is the value the difference computation just produced (madd.s f0,f0,f0), not a reload of the stored vector. Reproducing it needs a named local (e.g. float xDiffZ) that the difference is assigned to and that is then fed to the norm macro as a scalar argument (a macro variant taking _x,_y,_z instead of a vector pointer). Plain MWCC never forwards the swc1 to the later lwc1 (checked on 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail Rayman 3 camera code materialises a '!(x & 1)' flag as a value ('li v1,1' + 'movz v1,zero,a0' + 'beqz v1') where MWCC folds the same source into a direct branch on the masked value; a named float local holding the 0.0001f epsilon also changes the call-argument register allocation and was needed to pick the retail's v0/v1 split in CAM_fn_vComputeRefAxis. Both are small source-shape effects that a retail-vs-candidate register diff can hide. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0 bitfield RMW mask in an 'unsigned char' container: a 1-bit field at byte bit N is written with mask ~(1 | (1<<N)) and value (1<<N)*v (mask ~0x11 for N=4, ~0x21 for N=5, ~0x41 for N=6, ~0x9 for N=3). A 2-bit field at byte bits 1-2 uses mask ~0x6 and shifts the value by 1; at bits 2-3 mask ~0xC and shift 2. Verified by probe on MWCC 3.0-011126; reproduces the retail PS2GL byte RMWs on g_gsContext.PRMODE/TEST_1/TEST_2/COLCLAMP/ModFlgs. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail AI net-event code (LNK_Proc.c fn_NetSendEvent) stores the constant 1 into the bSendData global unconditionally: the 'li $v1,1' sits in the delay slot of the first conditional branch, so the flag is set to 1 even when it was already non-zero, and the same register value is forwarded into the LNE_CPsStatus path as the loop's initial counter. The source shape is 'if (!bSendData) { if (!bSendStatus && LNEvent != LNE_Kill && LNEvent != LNE_CreateCP && LNEvent != LNE_DeleteCP) return; } bSendData = 1;'. The three LNE_* comparisons form one short-circuit && chain whose failure branches to the same join. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail LNK_Proc.c fn_NetSendEvent addresses a_NetBuffer[] members through a separate materialised base per field (&a_NetBuffer[idx].Event / .Time / .iValue / .fValue each get their own lui/addiu pair, with the index*28 byte offset added to each) instead of one base with folded displacements. MWCC 3.0.1 reproduces this from plain 'a_NetBuffer[idx].member' member accesses when the index is a plain local; the same spelling under 3.0.3 folds the offset into the load/store displacement instead. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1-011126 parser bug: 'typedef struct tagX tagX;' followed by 'struct tagX { ... } __attribute__((aligned(16)));' leaves the typedef tagX UNDEFINED (the forward typedef is dropped once the aligned definition follows), and it also breaks every typedef declared before it in the same unit. Use the one-line form 'typedef struct tagX { ... } __attribute__((aligned(16))) tagX;' or refer to the struct by tag. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 switch lowering: a switch with sparse case labels is emitted as a beq compare chain in REVERSE source order (labels inside one multi-label entry are also reversed), and the case bodies are laid out in forward source order. Recovering the source therefore means reversing the chain; a single entry 'case A: case B: case C:' yields the chain C, B, A (verified with a 4-case probe on 3.0-011126). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 conditional-move form: 'c ? A : B' is lowered to movz (movz rd, else, c) with A in the destination register, while the retail PS2GL code uses movn (movn rd, then, c) with the else-value in the destination. Writing the same selection as '(c == 0) ? else : then' reproduces the retail's movn (see PS2GL_Array.c impPS2glDisableClientState, recorded MATCH, and impPS2glIsEnabled). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL tagGScontext in PS2GL_EnableDisable.c needs unsigned-char bitfield containers for the GS registers that the library touches byte-wise (PRMODE/anon_2, COLCLAMP/anon_28, TEST_1+TEST_2/anon_24, ModFlgs+GlobalModFlgs/anon_36); the retail's byte RMWs (lbu/sb at g_gsContext+0xa0/+0x120/+0x128/+0x132/+0x1b2/+0x1b4) only appear with 'unsigned char' bitfields. Wide padding bitfields must become byte arrays ('unsigned char pad11[6];') because MWCC rejects a >8-bit field in a char container; adding __attribute__((aligned(4))) plus explicit tail bytes keeps the 8-byte member sizes and the whole tagGScontext layout (sizeof 0x2230) unchanged. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Compiler profile: DisplayText.c's TXT_vAfficheSpecialWithTransparency needs MWCC 3.0.3-020716 - it is the only function in the file with an (unsigned int)float conversion, and 3.0.1-020123 emits 'jal fptoui' where retail inlines 'c.le.s / bc1t / trunc.w.s / or 0x80000000'. Under 3.0.3 the reconstructed source is byte-exact (1068/1068, relocs 44/44, line map ok), and every other function of the file is byte-identical under 3.0.1 and 3.0.3, so the file can move to config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt without affecting the recorded matches. (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] PS2GL_EnableDisable.c (impPS2glHint) only matches byte-for-byte with MWCC 3.0.1-020123 / 3.0.3-020716 / 3.0b38-030307; the PS2GL library baseline 3.0-011126 leaves the function 20 bytes short (it fills the F10 case's beq delay slot with a nop instead of 'andi v1,s0,0x200' and hoists the 0x439 case's 'addiu v1,s0,-0x800' into the switch chain). The file's other recorded matches (impPS2glFrontFace, impPS2glCullFace, impPS2glIsEnabled) match under all four builds, so the file qualifies for config/library-3.0.1-sources.txt. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.3 (SDLLights.cpp GLI_vSetSphericalLight, 400/400 MATCH): when a float local is read by a statement-level asm block (asm { mfc1 iVal, fNorm; } / qmtc2.ni iVal, $vf9), the FP allocator gives that local f2 and the unrelated comparison temporary f1, while the retail keeps the local in f1. The order is only reproduced when the asm operand is NOT a live C float (the same source with a raw $f1 source reserves f1 and shifts everything else), so the retail's FPR choice for a value that is read inside an asm block is a compiler-build difference, not a source shape. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.3: 'x = x * 2.0f;' and 'x *= 2.0f;' are NOT equivalent - the plain assignment compiles to 'mul.s $f2,$f2,$f3' (the constant's register becomes the destination and the variable is re-bound to it) while the compound form keeps the variable's own register: 'mul.s $f3,$f3,$f2'. SDLLights.cpp GLI_vSetSphericalLight only reproduces the retail 'mul.s $f3,$f3,$f2' (xDamp = xDamp * 2.0f) with the compound spelling. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] [debug-format] SDLLights.cpp GLIPS2_vManageLightsForThisCharacter: the extracted parameter order (..., int bBoundingSphere, float xSphereRadius, MTH3D_tdstVector_* p_stSphereCenter) is wrong. The retail reads the sphere-centre pointer from a3 and the radius from f12, so the source order must be (..., int bBoundingSphere, MTH3D_tdstVector_* p_stSphereCenter, float xSphereRadius). A controlled MWCC probe (5 integer/pointer parameters + a trailing float) shows the float still arrives in f12, so the float's register follows the float's own order, not the argument slot. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] SDLLights.cpp: the two VU0 light functions reconstruct with the retail line map only when the whole VU0 block is wrapped in '.set noreorder' ... '.set reorder' - without it MWCC hoists the C-code 'lwc1' of the comparison constant INTO the asm block, so the 'mtc1' that defines the distance lands after it and the FP registers shift (the retail has mtc1 then lwc1). The .set block also pins the block against the scheduler, which is what the retail's code shows. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (C): a zero test on a short parameter depends on the spelling. 'if (x != 0)' sign-extends the parameter first (dsll32/dsra32 by 16) and branches on the promoted register, with the extension hoisted ABOVE the test; the bare truth test 'if (x)' branches on the raw incoming register (beqz s4) and leaves the extension inside the branch body. Retail GEO_p_stAllocElementIndexedTriangles (GEO/Element.c) uses the truth-test form for all three short parameters (xNbFaces, xNbUVStages, xNbElementUV); with '!= 0' the promotion is hoisted and every branch target shifts. This is the C/short counterpart of the C++ unsigned-char finding. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: when a sizeof(T)-derived size is needed twice around a call, parenthesising it as 'n * (faces * sizeof(T))' keeps the inner product in a callee-saved register across the call - the retail 'sll s5,v0,1' + 'mult a0,v0,s5' with s5 = faces*6, five saved registers and a 0x70 frame - while the flat 'n * faces * sizeof(T)' multiplies the two counts first ('mult v1,v0,s1'), needs one fewer saved register and a 0x60 frame. Reproduced in GEO_p_stAllocElementIndexedTriangles (GEO/Element.c). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: an int-typed struct field that the retail treats as a pointer-to-array must be read through its VALUE, not its address, when storing: '(*(T***)&obj->field)[i] = p' emits 'lw v1,off(obj); addu v1,v1,index; sw p,0(v1)' (retail), while '((T**)&obj->field)[i] = p' takes the field's address and emits 'addiu a0,obj,off' first. The value form is what the already-matched GEO_vSet*OfIndexedCone/Sphere setters use; Element.c's GEO_vCreateElementCones/Spheres needed the same form for their 'd_stListOfElements[i] = p_stElement' store. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 folds a constant offset into the store displacement relative to the base register: '((struct V*)&m->buf[5])->Pos[0] = x' compiles to 'sh v1, 0x14(m)' (the offset of buf[5] from m), and '((struct V*)&m->buf[77])->f = x' likewise. This is how PS2MC CreateIconFile (0x331780) writes a PS2 icon template into the manager's m_pUserIconFile buffer with every store at 'this+const' (the header at +0x428, 12 vertices at +0x43C with a 0x18 stride, the anim section at +0x55C) - MATCH 644/644, lines ok. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail 'unfolded element offset' shape (base+member-offset materialised with addiu, index scaled in place in the parameter register) is not reproducible with MWCC 3.0.1: the compiler always hoists the index scaling above the global load and copies the parameter to a temp, and folds the member offset into the store displacement. PS2MC_vClearFlag (0x32aab0) needs exactly that shape; the best natural C gives 24/28 bytes, and a dead-index idiom ('*(unsigned int*)((char*)pFlags + (iIndex = FlagID*4)) = -1') gives 28/28 with only a consistent register renaming (EQUIVALENT). Compiler-build difference, not a source-shape error. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 call delay-slot choice follows the source order of the neighbouring statements: with 'A; call(x); B;' MWCC may sink A into the call's delay slot, while with 'B; call(x);' the call's delay slot holds the next statement and B stays a separate store. Reproduced in PS2MC AutoSaveGame (the m_Flags[1] = 0 store had to be written BEFORE strcpy(m_aGameName,...) to match the retail) and in ManagePreBootCheck (g_EngineHasBeenPausedBecauseOfMC = 0 and m_IPausedTheEngine = 0 before fn_vUnpauseFromMemoryCard). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 treats 'char' and 'signed char' as distinct types for register allocation: in SAI_fn_bRestoreAnElementFromList (SAI_Priv.c) the boolean restore '*(char*)p &= ~mask; *(char*)p |= mask;' produced the retail bytes except for the second statement's register assignment (address in a0 vs the retail's v1); writing the same statement with 'signed char*' reproduced the retail exactly (268/268, lines ok). Both spellings load the byte with lb. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 keeps the 'return NULL' of a search helper as an explicit register assignment when the helper is INLINED into a caller: SAI_Priv.c SAI_fn_bInsertAnElementInList calls fn_hSearchAnElementInList (same TU) and the inlined copy keeps the helper's 'return NULL' as a 'daddu s0,zero,zero' on the not-found path, which is exactly the retail's separate exit block before the join. The inlining needs '#pragma auto_inline on' before the (later defined) callee under the -inline deferred baseline; the caller's own code then matches byte for byte. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 does not accept 'while (cond) { ... } else { ... }' (syntax error), so a retail case whose fallback statement is only executed on the normal loop exit (not on break) must be written with a goto over the fallback store; 'if (pos == 8)' after the loop is not equivalent because MWCC's range analysis then drops the mask test and restructures the loop (SAI_Priv.c SAI_fn_bInsertAnElementInList's 0x8000 boolean case). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 places the THEN block of an if/else chain out of line and branches TO it when the source condition is negated, but keeps the then block inline and branches around it when the condition is positive: 'if (a == b) {X} else {Y}' gives 'bne a,b,Y_inline; X; Y:' while 'if (a != b) {X} else {Y}' gives 'beq a,b,Y_out_of_line; X; Y_out_of_line:'. DefineMenu2D.c fn_vNextSelectionNoWrap's inner test ('CurrentFirstEntry != NbEntry-NbSelection') only produced the retail's 'beq v1,v0,<sound=10 block at the end>' after inverting the condition, so the polarity of the source comparison decides whether the retail's out-of-line block shape is reachable at all. (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a switch with a few sparse cases by emitting the case comparisons in the REVERSE of the source order and laying the case bodies out in that same reversed order: 'switch (r) { case 1: ...; case 2: ...; }' produces 'beq r,2,<case2 body>' first and 'beq r,1,<case1 body>' second, with the case2 body placed before the case1 body. DefineMenu2D.c ReturnFromSubMenu needs the case tests in the order (2,1) with the bodies in the order (1,2), which one switch spelling cannot give; an if/else chain keeps the source order but emits the opposite branch polarity, so the switch's test order and the if/else's block order have to be traded against each other. (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 narrows a 'long' local whose only use is a zero test back to 32 bits ('li v0,1' / 'addiu v0,zero,1') and then keeps it in a callee-saved register because it is live across a call, while the retail materialises the same flag 64-bit ('daddiu v0,zero,1' / 'daddu v0,zero,zero') in a caller-saved register. DefineMenu2D.c ReturnFromSubMenu shows both: the 'bPlayVideo' flag and the 'szLevelName' pointer that has to survive CloseInGameMenu2D (the retail loads it into s0 in the call's delay slot and moves it to a0 afterwards). (2026-10-20) (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 folds an enclosing 'if (p->ulTypeOfLinkedObject == 2)' into a nested 'p->ulTypeOfLinkedObject != 2 ? 0 : p->hLinkedObject.p_stCharacter' ternary and drops the redundant re-test, while the retail keeps it (the outer beq, then a second beq on the same register before the load, plus the extra 'b' to the else block). Putting the ternary condition in a local 'type' keeps the re-test, but the local is then live across the recursive call and takes a callee-saved register, so the retail's allocation (s4 = argument, 0x60 frame) is lost. Reproduced on AlwAct.c fn_vDestroyAllHierarchy and on the same idiom in Objinit.c fn_vPartiallyDestroyHierarchy; the scoped pragmas opt_propagation/peephole/opt_dead_code/schedule off do not restore the re-test. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] The 8-byte-alignment nop that keeps a function's epilogue 8-aligned is placed AFTER the loop by MWCC PS2 3.0.x, but the retail (AlwAct.c fn_hGetNextAlwaysActiveCharNode) places it INSIDE the loop at the loop head, so the loop's back-branch is the 8-aligned instruction; both forms have the same instruction count and size. All four available builds (3.0-011126, 3.0.1-020123, 3.0.3-020716, 3.0b38-030307) place it after the loop, and -pragma padloop on/off does not change it. Probe: the double nop after a loop's load only appears when the loop body contains a store or a call, so the placement difference is not reachable from a natural source shape. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] A braced 'if' block makes MWCC emit an extra line-table entry for the block's closing brace (at the following epilogue) that the retail does not have. Writing the guarded list walk as the only statement of an unbraced 'if' (AlwAct.c fn_hGetNextAlwaysActiveCharNode) or as a 'for (init; cond; incr)' loop (AlwAct.c fn_vChangeDynamicAlwaysActiveState, which then matches 152/152 with the line map ok) reproduces the retail's statement-boundary offsets exactly. The verifier compares the line-entry OFFSETS only, so a candidate can match the bytes and still report lines=different. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3 evaluate a call's FLOAT arguments in source order, and a controlled probe (six float args, the last three being products) reproduces that order exactly. Retail SND_R3 code can evaluate them in a different order: CLinkMicro::mfn_bApplyPositionAndVelocity computes the three cross-product arguments as arg6, arg4, arg5 while the argument registers stay positional (arg4->f15, arg5->f16, arg6->f17). When a candidate's mula.s/msub.s sequence is right but the order is not, permuting the source argument order reproduces the retail's arithmetic sequence but permutes the destination registers, so a residual there is an evaluation-order/scheduling difference, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3 spill a link-time constant (the address of a function-local static) to the stack when it has to survive a call inside an inlined constructor, and then use the base register plus displacement for the member stores. Retail instead rematerialises the constant as four separate lui/addiu pairs (static+0x4, static+0xc, static+0xd and the object itself) and keeps 'this' in a0 only for the callee call. Deleting the call from the constructor makes our build emit exactly the retail's four-lui prologue, so this difference is a register-allocation/rematerialisation decision for constants across a call, not a source-layout one. (2026-10-20)

- [abi] PS2 o32 ABI: a class with a non-trivial copy constructor is returned through a HIDDEN pointer in a0 (the first argument slot), so the callee stores the result into the caller's stack slot and the caller reads it back - no store instruction appears at the call site. In retail SND_R3 code this is why stree::lower_bound's result appears at sp+0x88 with no visible store, why stree::end() writes to sp+0x84/sp+0x70/sp+0x6C, and why the iterator copy constructor is called with a1 pointing at such a slot. When reconstructing, do not look for the missing store; put the call's destination in a0. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] SND_R3 stree template: the retail's stree does NOT redeclare sTreeLink; the nested iterator's constructor takes streebase::sTreeLink* (mangled __ct__Q34usnd179stree<...>8iteratorFPQ34usnd9streebase9sTreeLink). A template that declares its own nested sTreeLink mangles the same constructor as ...8iteratorFPQ34usnd179stree<...>9sTreeLink, so the relocation never matches even when the code is otherwise identical. The retail's stree::end() is also out of line (symbol end__Q24usnd179stree<...>Fv) and is called, not inlined, from sndclinkmicrocont.cpp's mfn_bRemoveMicroInstance. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] Extractor 'D_XXXXXXXX' extern declarations are usually anonymous string literals in the retail ELF, not globals: the retail's D_0035ADA0 is the anonymous symbol @959 at 0x35ADA0 containing 'VIGNETTE\\FRA\\%s.RAW'. Declaring 'extern char D_0035ADA0[]' leaves the HI16/LO16 relocation unresolved; writing the literal directly ('sprintf(TempString, "VIGNETTE\\\\FRA\\\\%s.RAW", levelname);') lets the verifier infer the retail address and compare the literal bytes - GLI_vLoadCarton in u_drawInterf.c then matches 64/64 with the line map. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0 bitfield insert: assigning a value to an N-bit field always emits the field-width mask (e.g. 'andi v1,v1,0x3' before 'sll a3,v1,1' for a 2-bit field at bit 1), even when the source value is already masked ('x & 3'); the retail PS2GL code omits that redundant mask, so a bitfield write of a masked value is a 4-byte mismatch. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL GS register bitfield containers (byte access): the retail PS2GL library touches g_gsContext.PRMODE(+0xa0), COLCLAMP(+0x120), TEST_1/TEST_2(+0x128/+0x132) and ModFlgs(+0x1b2/+0x1b4) with byte read-modify-writes, which requires 'unsigned char' bitfield containers; the first 16 bits of the TEST register must stay in 'unsigned short' containers (a 8-bit AREF does not fit in the 4 remaining bits of byte 0 and would shift ZTE from byte 2 bit 0 to byte 2 bit 4). Verified pattern: 'unsigned short ATE:1; ATST:3; AREF:8; AFAIL:2; DATE:1; DATM:1; unsigned char ZTE:1; ZTST:2; pad19:5;' plus 4 padding bytes, with __attribute__((aligned(4))) to keep the 8-byte member size. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0 switch with contiguous labels: 'switch (x) { case 0x200: ... case 0x207: ... }' compiles to 'addi v1,v1,-0x200; sltiu at,v1,8; lui/addiu table; jr' - the offset uses addi (not addiu) and the table index is the raw value minus the first label. The PS2GL retail uses exactly this for the GL depth-func switch (GL_NEVER 0x200 .. GL_ALWAYS 0x207). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 folds a bitfield store value (1 << bitpos) into a literal and then common-subexpression-eliminates it with an equal literal from another store, while the retail keeps a runtime shift of the already-materialised constant 1: u_drawInterf.c ClearScreenAfterInit sets PRMODE.FGE (byte 0, bit 5) with 'sll v1,t3,5' where t3 holds the constant 1 used for the ATE store, but our build emits the literal 0x20 (daddiu t2,zero,0x20) and shares it with the ModFlgs bit-5 mask. All four builds (3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38) fold it, so this is a compiler-build difference, not a source-shape one. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3/3.0b38) deletes an unused local array and its stack frame completely, so a function whose retail body only calls one library routine cannot reproduce the retail frame: u_drawInterf.c AskForLevelStart keeps a 0x110 frame (unused 'char GamedataDir[255]' at sp+0x10) and calls strcpy with jal, while every build turns it into a tail call (j) with no prologue. No spelling keeps the frame without extra code: unused/address-taken/volatile arrays, dead code after a return, if(0) blocks, zero-size libc calls, self-copies, the scoped opt_* pragmas and size mode all drop it. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a conversion of an INT union member to float as 'lwc1 f0,addr; cvt.s.w f1,f0' (the int bits are loaded as a float and reinterpreted), while a FLOAT union member is loaded with a plain lwc1. Retail fn_p_stScalarOperator (Operator.c) tests p_stValue->eType == E_vt_Float and converts the int member with exactly that pair, so the two-member union tduGetSetParam_ (xValue/lValue both at +0) reproduces it byte-exactly. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 materialises the float literal 1.1754951e-38f as 0x80000005 (lui 0x80; ori 5) but the literal 1.1754944e-38f as 0x80000000 (lui 0x80 only), so the two spellings are NOT interchangeable in a divide-by-zero guard: retail Operator.c fn_p_stScalarOperator and the matched fn_p_stVectorScalarOperator both use the 0x80000005 form, i.e. the source literal must be written 1.1754951e-38f. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The current tools/extract_debug.py local list is INCOMPLETE for some functions: the extracted scaffolds in AI/AIGame/Operator.c list a local 'tdstNodeInterpret_* p_stNext' for fn_p_stVectorDotOperator (and ucOk/p_stNext for fn_p_stDotOperator) that the parser no longer reports, and the byte-exact match of fn_p_stVectorDotOperator needs that local. Reconstruct from the scaffold's local list, not from a fresh parser run. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (Operator.c fn_p_stVectorDotOperator, exact 168/168): a switch whose case bodies 'return' a value held in a callee-saved register while the 'default:' case only 'break's reproduces the retail's copy pair - the copy into a1 is sunk into the preceding bne's delay slot, the default branch's delay slot holds the copy back to v0, and the case bodies' returns elide it (v0 == a1), so v0 stays reserved and the case bodies use v1 as scratch. The copy only appears when the returned value is also reachable through a live copy of the call result (a parameter reassigned from fn_p_stEvalTree plus a local copy of it), otherwise the compiler drops both copies. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The R5900 instruction spimdisasm prints as 'c1 0x44' is the COP1 SQRT.S (word 0x44000046): the retail's MTH_SQR3_TO + MTH_SQRT_TO pair (mula.s/madda.s/madd.s accumulator then sqrt.s) reproduces it exactly, while a bare MTH_SQR3_TO emits no such instruction. The same word 0x84000046 ('c1 0x84') is the same SQRT.S with a different destination field (MEC_fn_lComputeCall, matched). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.x: a switch whose case values are a short run of consecutive integers compiles to a comparison chain (beq per case) in DESCENDING order of the sorted values, so the source must list the cases in ascending order to reproduce the retail chain. A 20-case switch uses a jump table instead (fn_p_stSPORotate: 5-case chain at 0x1FD..0x201, 20-case table at 0x1EE..0x201). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 folds float literals to the nearest float32: the retail's degrees-to-radians constant is the word 0x3C8EFA35 = 0.017453292f (PI/180), while 0.017453f folds to 0x3C8EFA39 and does not match (fn_p_stSPORotate). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.x never copies a 12-byte float-vector aggregate as ld/sd + lwc1/swc1 (a 12-byte struct assignment is always three lwc1/swc1 pairs, even when the type is 8-byte aligned). The retail's 12-byte copy form is reproduced by copying the first 8 bytes through an unsigned long long* and the third component through the same pointer ((float*)p)[2]), which yields ld/sd plus lwc1/swc1 addressed off the base registers. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] Verifier/tooling: tools/match.py finds a function's .line_ section by the exact name '.line_<name>'; for a C++ (.cxx) translation unit the section is '.line_<mangled>', and when the retail name is a prefix of another symbol (fn_p_stSPORotate vs fn_p_stSPORotateToIdentity) the fallback prefix search is ambiguous, so the line check reports 'lines=different' with an empty candidate map. Declaring the definition with C linkage (extern "C") makes the candidate's .line_ name match the retail's exactly (the retail object exports these symbols unmangled). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail COL octree face-index lists are variable-length little-endian integers (a byte < 0x80 is a 1-byte value, otherwise 2 bytes with the 0x7fff mask) and the octree's face list is an unsigned short* (COL_tdstOctree_.d_xElementBasesTable) while COL_tdstOctreeNode_.d_xFaceIndexList is a short*; the extractor's int fields must be retyped for the pointer arithmetic to match retail. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 matches the retail sorted-insert idiom when the list is walked backwards with 'for (i = *pCount; i > 0 && xDist < aList[i-1].xDist; i--)' and the element is copied with memcpy(dst, src, sizeof) inside the loop (same shape as the matched COL_fn_vInsertNodeSortedByT in COL/OctreeGO.c); retail additionally guards the copy with an extra 'slt at,a1,v1' whose bound is the pre-loop 3*count value, which no natural source shape reproduced under 3.0.1/3.0.3. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 compiles a bare 'return;' inside a NON-void function as 'return 0': the path is folded into the function's shared 'v0 = 0' exit block ('blez v0, <exit>' with 'move v0,zero' in the exit). A retail early exit that returns WITHOUT setting v0 (a 'b <epilogue>' whose delay slot holds 'ld ra') is reproduced by returning a local that still holds the value in v0: 'lReturn = fn_lCheckVMSConnexion(1); if (lReturn <= 0) { return lReturn; }' keeps lReturn in v0, so the early exit becomes a separate 'b <epilogue>' block with no v0 store. Controlled pair in GAM/specif/ps2/gamopt.c fn_bLoadGameOptions: 'return;' gave 'blez v0,408' (v0 = 0), 'return lReturn;' gave the retail's 'bgtz v0,24' + 'b 40c' + 'ld ra' delay slot. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 does not merge two identical 'return 0;' tails: each error block keeps its own 'move v0,zero' (a 'b <epilogue>' with the store in the delay slot). The retail's single shared 'v0 = 0' block that two different error branches jump to comes from ONE 'return 0;' statement at the end of the function that both paths reach - the 'if (p != NULL) { ... } return 0;' shape. GAM/specif/ps2/gamopt.c fn_bLoadGameOptions: with two separate 'return 0;' statements the candidate emitted 'bnez s0,skip / b <exit> / move v0,zero' per error path; rewriting it as 'if (p_stTempFile != NULL) { ... if (VMS_fn_bFclose(p) != 0) { ... return 1; } } return 0;' produced the retail's 'beqz s0,<shared exit>' and 'beqz v0,<shared exit>' and matched 1048/1048. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 hoists the OPERAND of a test that lives in a branch-target block into the delay slot of the branch that reaches it, so the later test reads the delay-slot value; this also fixes the register allocation.
