GAM/specif/ps2/gamopt.c fn_vManageControlerDisconnection: 'if ((PAUSE_g_ucPauseCause & 5) == 0) { A } else { if (PAUSE_g_ucPauseCause & 1) { B } else { C } }' compiles to 'lbu a0,cause; andi v1,a0,5; bnez v1,<else>; andi v1,a0,1' with the 'andi v1,a0,1' in the bnez delay slot, and the inner test at the else block is a plain 'beqz v1' (no reload). The retail matches this exactly; with the inner test written as '& 5' instead of '& 1' the delay slot stayed nop, the global was kept in v1 instead of a0, and the body differed by a register swap. (2026-10-20)

- [relocation] The retail ELF's .relmain section resolves the exact symbol and addend behind every lui/addiu pair of a function, which is the fastest way to identify unnamed globals in a disassembly: parse the section whose sh_link is the .symtab index and print (offset, sym) for the function's address range. GAM/specif/ps2/gamopt.c fn_bLoadGameOptions: the 'D_587630'/'D_587744' spimdisasm placeholders are g_stGameOptions + 0x100 (szCurrentFileName) and + 0x214 (ulScreenSize), the '0x587750' absolute addresses are g_stGameOptions.s32VolumeSound (+0x220) .. s32VolumeMenu (+0x230), and the '@698' literal is the 8-byte string 'OPTIONS' at 0x359d68 (spimdisasm's '%lo(@698)' actually resolves to 0x359d68, not to the anonymous symbol's own address). Note that the retail's '@N' names are NOT unique - the ELF has several '@698' symbols in different objects. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2's float-call-argument constant materialisation depends on the translation unit's constant pool, so one function's code can change when an unrelated function in the same TU changes: in disp.c, fn_vFeedTexToGl only matched 728/728 once fn_vConvertIpuToTex had a body (its first TexCoord2f constant moved from the merge block to the statement's first basic block); restoring the empty fn_vConvertIpuToTex body restored the 724/728 mismatch. Verify a function's match after editing any other function of the same file. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a negative float literal (-1.0f) into the constant pool after the following positive one, while the retail materialises it before: spelling the near plane as the expression (0.0f - 1.0f) reproduces the retail's PS2glOrtho argument order (100.0f, -1.0f, 1.0f) and gives an exact 576/576 match for DesinitGlDevice in disp.c. (2026-10-20)

- [relocation] The verifier resolves a candidate GPREL16 relocation by symbol name and prefers a global definition over a same-named local symbol: a file-scope static in one TU whose name collides with a global of another TU (disp.c's static StartFrame/EndFrame versus Init_PS2GL.c's globals of the same name) is patched to the global's address (gp-0x6B1C instead of the retail's gp-0x78C4), so fn_vDisplayImage cannot byte-match until the matcher prefers the local symbol for a candidate's local static. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail PS2 code converts an unsigned int to float with the branchy 'bltz / srl / andi / or / mtc1 / cvt.s.w / add.s' sequence (MWCC 3.0 emits the same shape for (float)unsigned), and a signed int compared against a limit with sltiu (not slti) is reproduced by spelling the comparison '(unsigned int)x > limit' while the variable itself stays a signed int. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 argument evaluation order for a call depends on the declared pointer type of the parameter: with 'void* pointer' the compiler emits the 3rd/4th arguments first (a2,a3 then a0,a1), while the same call with 'unsigned char* pointer' emits them left to right (a0,a1,a2,a3). impPS2glInterleavedArrays (PS2GL_Array.c) matches 1188/1188 bytes, 62/62 relocations and the line map under MWCC 3.0.1 only when PS2glVertexPointer/TexCoordPointer/NormalPointer/ColorPointer are declared with 'unsigned char* pointer'; with 'void*' the 3.0.1/3.0 builds order 52 instructions differently, and 3.0.3 happens to emit the retail order for both spellings. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.3-020716 no longer emits the retail's branch-target alignment nops: impPS2glDrawElements is 268/268 under 3.0.1 and 264/268 under 3.0/3.0.1 plain (one missing nop at the switch join) and 252/268 under 3.0.3 (four missing). Under 3.0/3.0.1 '-pragma padloop on' adds the loop-head padding and '-O3,p' adds the rest; 3.0.3 ignores padloop entirely. A library file that needs those nops must be listed in config/library-O3p-sources.txt. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 16-byte block copies: a struct copy of {int w[4]} lowers to lwc1/swc1 and of {long long q[2]} to ld/ld/sd/sd, while the same struct declared with __attribute__((aligned(16))) lowers to a single lq/sq pair. PS2glInternalDrawSpriteArray (PS2GL_Array.c) needs the lq/sq form to copy the 16-byte MY_MSCNT global into the GS packet; memcpy with a constant size 16 is not inlined (it stays a call). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] The callee's LOCAL prototype decides the caller's argument conversion and therefore its register allocation: a3x_cach.c fn_vInitAllEvents calls fn_p_GetEventsAddress (a retail wrapper that ignores its 2nd/3rd args). Declaring that parameter 'unsigned int' made the retail's 'andi a1,s5,0xffff' appear (the unsigned-short argument zero-extended straight into a1); 'unsigned short' dropped the mask and 'int' materialized it in an extra saved register (532 bytes instead of 528). The prototype type, not just the argument, is part of the match. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] The extractor's local list order is often the reverse of the original: in a3x_cach.c fn_vInitAllEvents, declaring the locals in the reverse of the extracted order (h_3dData, p_stAnim, p_stEvent, i, bBTLevel, ucMemoryType, h_CurrentState) reproduced the retail's callee-saved assignment s0=h_3dData, s1=p_stAnim, s2=p_stEvent, s3=bBTLevel, s4=ucMemoryType, s5=parameter, s6=h_CurrentState exactly (MATCH with the retail line map). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 register allocation for an indexed store through a pointer field depends on the field's declared type: with the field typed 'int' the base pointer lands in v1 and the scaled index in v0 ('addu v0,v1,v0'), while retyping the same field as the real pointer type ('short* d_xListOfElementsTypes') and writing 'p->field[i] = v' gives the retail's base in v0 and index in v1 ('addu v0,v0,v1'). Confirmed on COL_vCreateElementAlignedBoxes/COL_vCreateElementSpheres (COL/CollElts.c): the extractor emitted 'int' for the short* element-type list, and the retail 'lw v0,0xc(s3); sll v1,s1,1; addu v0,v0,v1' only reproduced with the pointer type. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Loop unrolling depends on where a pointer increment sits: MWCC -O3,p unrolls 'for (p = base, i = 0; i < n; i++) { p->f = x; p++; }' by eight, but keeps it rolled when the increment moves into the for header 'i++, p++' (Mmg_fn_v_InitMmg, cpammg.c: 232 -> 156/156 MATCH, replacing '#pragma opt_unroll_loops off'). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Scope: functions the retail debug attributes to a header (.h/.hpp/.hxx) are library support code and are excluded from the decompilation effort. match.py list/worklist/batch skip them by default (list --include-headers shows them), coverage counts them out of the totals and reports them as 'excluded (library header functions, out of scope)', and translation_units ignores them so a translation unit is never held open by a header function. 177 such functions exist in the retail (C4_R3/harmonx Pustl portable STL, public MTH/Mat headers, PS2GL support headers). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC treats a .h passed on the command line as an include, not a translation unit: it compiles it but writes no object, so match.py used to fail with a missing temporary file. compile_source now drives a header through a generated one-line .cpp that includes it. Separately, check --source PATH compiles that translation unit instead of the owning source, which is how a function whose retail debug points at a header is verified in the unit that instantiates it. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] MWCC debug type records can under-report an aggregate's stack size: in SVG/vm_backup.c the local file-info object of VMS_lGetFileDate sits at sp+0x20 with the next local (the RTC time struct) at sp+0x98, i.e. the retail frame gives it 0x78 bytes, while its debug type record (@anon2 / sceMcTblGetDir) reports 0x40. Declaring the local with the recovered 0x40-byte layout plus 0x38 bytes of trailing storage reproduces the retail 0xd0 frame byte-for-byte (496/496, lines=ok). Compare a local's debug offset with the next local's debug offset instead of trusting the type record's size when a frame does not add up. (2026-10-20)

- [abi] Frame-size-only residual in SVG level-0 files (mc_menu.c): when retail reserves one or two extra 16-byte slots the code never addresses, the function has unused leading parameters the debug record omits. Adding 'char* p_szUnused' (one slot) or 'char*, int' (two slots) reproduced MC_NotEnoughtSpace (0x30 frame) and MC_CheckNeededSize (0x50 frame); unused/array/block-scoped locals do not. Check other frame-slack attempts in level-0 files for the same cause. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0.1/3.0.3) reproduces a retail bit-packed screen-coordinate clamp (GLI_xSerialProjection, Tex.c) with a 'volatile int lNewX = 0x45FA0000;' local: the volatile keeps the constant in a stack slot (sp+0xc) and forces a reload after each store through the unknown output pointer ('lw a1,0xc(sp)'), while a plain int local would be kept in a register. The register/schedule of the function head only matches when the pointer local 'p_stDestLast' is declared WITH its initializer before 'lNewX' (MWCC then emits lui/sw for the volatile first and the sll for p_stDestLast second). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 cannot inline fabsf, so an epsilon comparison chain needs a statement-level 'asm { abs.s d,d }' on a reused float temporary. The retail form is a NESTED-if chain (one temporary reused for every term), not a '&&' chain: '&&' makes MWCC emit the extra sltu/beqz test pattern around the first condition (MAT_fn_bAreEquivalentWithEps insight), while nested ifs with the in-place abs reproduce the retail exactly (sub.s/abs.s/c.lt.s/bc1f per term, all false branches to the same target). Reproduced in COLGOCo.c COL_fn_vAddInStaticCollisionTable. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail branch polarity inside a loop can come from a 'continue' rather than an inverted if: 'slt v1,a0,s2; bnez v1,<loop increment>' (branch when the test is TRUE, increment as the fall-through) is what a source 'if (x < limit) { continue; } x++;' compiles to, while 'if (x >= limit) { x++; }' gives the opposite 'slt; beqz' shape. Reproduced in COLGOCo.c COL_fn_vAddInStaticCollisionTable (the collided-edge Ind fix-up). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 and the retail build disagree on the branch polarity of a forward 'goto L' inside an if: for 'if ((id & 4) == 4) goto L; return;' our build emits 'bne id,4,<the return>' with the label as the fall-through, while the retail emits 'beq id,4,L' with the return as the fall-through. Neither the goto, an explicit if/else, an empty then-block, nor a 'while (1) { break; }' loop reproduces the retail shape in our build, so this residual is a compiler-build difference and not a source-shape problem (COLGOCo.c COL_fn_vAddInStaticCollisionTable, +0x6c). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The pointer-to-field spelling that reproduces the retail's unfolded member offset ('(base+fieldoff) + index*stride', load at +0) also REVERSES the operand evaluation order: the retail evaluates the index expression before the object chain, while '((char*)&table[0].field + index*sizeof(elem))' evaluates the chain first because the address operand comes first in the C expression. Hoisting the index into its own statement restores the retail order but leaves the chain loads interleaved into the index arithmetic. Seen in PhysColl.c MOR_fn_xPrepareMorph (+0x21e). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (game baseline) allocates a pointer-derived index chain before the pointer chain in the unfolded element-offset idiom '*(int*)((char*)&arr[0].field + i*stride)', while the retail allocates the index chain first and the pointer chain second (INT_fn_bIntersectSemiAxeWithSphere: retail 'addiu v1,v0,0x20 / sll v0,s2,2 / addu a0,v0,s2' vs ours 'addiu a0,v0,0x20 / sll v0,s2,2 / addu v1,v0,s2'). MWCC canonicalises the commutative '+' so no source operand order changes this; every spelling tested (char*/int*/long base, index-first, local temporaries, sizeof stride, ((T*)&arr[0].f)[i*n], elem+0x20) gives the same allocation. Treat it as a compiler-build residual of the unfolded-offset family. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 keep a multi-instruction statement-level asm block (mula.s/madda.s/madd.s/msub.s) contiguous, but the retail compiler schedules the individual accumulator instructions around the surrounding call setup: in INT_fn_bIntersectSemiAxeWithSphere the retail emits 'mula.s / madda.s / addiu a0 / madda.s / addiu a1 / jal / msub.s(delay slot)' while every available build (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3, 3.0b38) emits the asm block contiguously followed by the call setup. Splitting the macro into separate asm statements, into two inline functions, or using a macro statement does not change it. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (game baseline) picks the callee-saved FPRs from the LOCAL DECLARATION ORDER of the float locals: declaring the three components of a vector in Z,Y,X order (and assigning them in that order) makes the compiler hand out the temporary FPRs in the retail's order even though the emitted loads keep X,Y,Z order (INT_fn_bIntersectSemiAxeWithSphere: retail 'lwc1 f7,0(s4) / lwc1 f5,4(s4) / lwc1 f6,8(s4)' only with 'float xVz; float xVy; float xVx;' declared and assigned Z,Y,X; the X,Y,Z spelling gives f6/f5/f7). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers a ternary condition two different ways: '(X) ? 1 : 0' used as an if-condition becomes 'li v0,1; <test>; movz v0,zero,v1; beqz/bnez v0' (the 1 is materialised early and movz clears it), while '(X) ? 0 : 1' folds the negation into the movz ('movz v1,v0,v1'), giving a different encoding for the same semantics. The retail form (value computed, then tested for zero) is reproduced by writing '!((X) ? 1 : 0)'. Confirmed on MEC_fn_vInertiaCoeff's two flag tests: the 0x800 test matched byte-exactly with '(ulEndFlags & 0x800) ? 1 : 0', the 0x10 test only with '!((ulEndFlags & 0x10) ? 1 : 0)'. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 allocates standalone 12-byte MTH3D_tdstVector_ locals 16 bytes apart (16-byte aligned), although the same type is packed at 12 bytes when it is a struct member (DNM_stDynamicsBaseBlock has its vectors at 0x24,0x30,0x3c,...). Reproduced in MEC_fn_vInertiaCoeff, where four vector locals occupy sp+0xb0,0xc0,0xd0,0xe0 with a plain 'MTH3D_tdstVector_' declaration and no alignment attribute; the first-declared local gets the lowest slot, so the declaration order fixes the offsets. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] usnd::slist::iterator is 4 bytes in the retail (a single _Node* _Ptr) and does NOT derive from streebase::iterator_base. A local copy that gives it a second pointer (a _Myhead member) makes the iterator 8 bytes, and every function that copies one gets an 8-byte-bigger frame: mfn_bSynchroCallbackInstance__23CLinkCallbackResObjContFv then has a 0x50 frame where the retail has 0x40. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 keeps a multi-instruction statement-level asm block (mula/madda/madd/msub) contiguous, while the retail schedules the individual accumulator instructions around the surrounding call setup (madda.s after the argument setup, msub.s in the jal delay slot). Splitting the block into separate asm statements, two inline functions or a macro statement does not change it, and none of the four MWCC builds reproduces the retail's interleaving. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 picks the callee-saved FPRs from the local DECLARATION order of the float locals: declaring the three vector components Z,Y,X puts the temporaries in the retail's FPRs (f7/f5/f6) even though the emitted loads stay in X,Y,Z order. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] SND_R3 usnd STL: the retail ELF has out-of-line copies '__ct__<stree>8iteratorFPQ<stree>9sTreeNode' but never '__ct__<stree>8iteratorFPQ34usnd9streebase9sTreeLink', so the retail's tree iterator has NO sTreeLink* constructor - only (void), (sTreeNode*) and (const iterator&). A local template that declares 'iterator(sTreeLink*)' makes MWCC emit an out-of-line '__ct__<stree>8iteratorFPQ...sTreeLink' weak copy and call it, which the retail lacks -> unresolved relocation in the verifier. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] SND_R3 usnd STL: the retail's stree iterator copy constructor is NOT trivial - it forwards the node pointer to the base class ('iterator(const iterator& ro_It) : iterator_base(ro_It._Ptr) {}'). MWCC inlines it, so the copy becomes a call to the out-of-line 'usnd::streebase::iterator_base::iterator_base(sTreeLink*)' (retail mfn_poFindSoundSemaphore +0x2c/+0x30, mfn_bRemoveSoundSemaphore +0x30/+0x34). A local copy with a trivial copy ctor ('_Ptr = ro_It._Ptr') instead emits 'lw v0,src; sw v0,dst' and misses the retail by 12 bytes. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1-020123 and 3.0.3-020716 copy a BY-VALUE class parameter through the class's copy constructor, while the retail's compiler copies it as raw bytes. Evidence: retail SND_R3 CSoundSemaphoreCont::mfn_vDelete materialises the by-value 'erase(iterator)' parameter with 'lw v0,0x38(sp); sw v0,0x44(sp)', but every MWCC build calls 'usnd::streebase::iterator_base::iterator_base(sTreeLink*)' instead. Combined with the non-trivial iterator copy constructor the retail needs for stree::find(), the two requirements are mutually exclusive in our builds: this is a retail-compiler-build residual, not a source-shape problem. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The retail's SDL/SDC helpers take more arguments than their debug records list: GLI_vGetVertexPointersFromSDCStructureSpriteGens is called by u_SDL.s with seven arguments (a0,a1,a2,a3,t0,t1,t2 - five stack arrays at sp+0xE0..0x120) while the MWCC definition record lists only five. The two unreferenced middle parameters were dropped by the extractor, so the recovered 'float ** pVOrg[4]' parameters are plain 'float **' pointers and the callee's three stores land in the 3rd, 4th and 7th argument slots (a2, a3 and t2). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] A 'found' boolean in a search loop is kept in a caller-saved register by MWCC when it is assigned on every exit path of the loop (so it is dead across the call inside the body), but is copied into a callee-saved register when it is initialised before the loop. The retail CineDef.c search loops (fn_vStopEngineCinematics / fn_vLaunchEngineCinematics) use the former shape: 'bFound = 1; break;' plus 'bFound = 0;' as the last statement of the loop body, which puts the flag in $v1 and makes it dead across the CINE_CheckEndCineActor call. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 narrow a 64-bit local ('long', 'long long', 'unsigned long') that is only tested against zero to 32 bits, so the constant store becomes 'li/addiu' where the retail emits the 64-bit 'daddiu v1,zero,1' / 'daddu v1,zero,zero' (CineDef.c fn_vStopEngineCinematics +0x34/+0x50, fn_vLaunchEngineCinematics +0x40). Declaring the flag with a 64-bit type does not restore the retail form. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 assigns FPRs to the operands of a float expression in DESCENDING order of first use (first live range gets the highest register, e.g. f5,f4,f3,f2,f1,f0), while the retail ELF uses ASCENDING order. Writing the three component subtractions in reverse source order (z, then y, then x) makes MWCC allocate ascending (x lowest, z highest) and reproduces the retail register assignment byte-for-byte (Inters.c INT_fn_bIntersectSphereWithSphere, 84/84 MATCH). The reversal is invisible in the emitted instruction order: MWCC keeps the loads/subs in the source order, only the register numbers change. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 rewrite a signed (int)float conversion as trunc.w.s (an unsigned conversion) when the value range proves the result is non-negative: PS2glFogfv's '(int)(255.0f * clamp01(x))' becomes 'mul.s / trunc.w.s / mfc1', while the retail uses the signed 'cvt.w.s'. Any source that proves the operand is >= 0 (here the [0,1] clamp) triggers it; the retail keeps cvt.w.s, so the conversion must be spelled so the range analysis cannot fire. Related: the retail's clamp block also ends with an out-of-line join ('b' + assignment in the delay slot) where ours falls through into the join. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3) always narrows a bitfield read-modify-write to the SMALLEST unit that contains the field: an 11-bit field at bit 21 of a 32-bit container is updated through a 16-bit halfword (lh/sh, mask 0xFFFF001F, shift 5) even though the container is 4 bytes, and the clear-mask is computed in 32 bits (0xFFFF001F). The retail PS2GL code (PS2glSetInteger's tagGLtexblock.nLength) instead uses a 32-bit lw/sw of the whole word with the 64-bit mask 0xFFFFFFFF001FFFFF (= ~(long)0xFFE00000). Verified with a probe on every field of the struct: the bit layout (0/10/21) and sizeof match, only the RMW unit and the mask width differ. Explicit C bit manipulation does not help either: MWCC folds 'x & 0x1FFFFF' into a shift pair (dsll32/dsrl32) and drops the 64-bit mask. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 always rotate a register-tested scan loop into [guard][body][increment][b back-to-guard]: the bound test is a 'sltu/beqz' guard that precedes the body and the back edge is an unconditional 'b' to the guard. The retail AGO2_MEM_FreeFromHandle rotates the same loop the other way: [b guard][increment][guard][body][bne back-to-increment], i.e. the increment block sits BEFORE the guard and the body's back edge targets it. No natural spelling (plain for, for(;;) with an explicit 'if (i >= n) break;', while, do-while, array vs (char*)base+offset indexing) reproduces the retail rotation in 3.0, 3.0.1 or 3.0.3, so this is a retail-compiler-build difference in the loop rotation. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 reproduces the retail 'movz rd,zero,rs; beqz rd' boolean-test idiom (a normalize-then-test of an int expression) when the source spells the condition as a ternary: 'if ((flags & 0x4000) ? 1 : 0)'. The plain 'if (flags & 0x4000)' folds to a direct 'andi/beqz' and 'if ((flags & 0x4000) == 0x4000)' folds to 'andi/bne', neither of which matches. Verified by DNM_fn_vDynamicsInitDefault (MEC/DNMMTH.c): the ternary form reproduces all four retail movz+beqz flag tests byte-for-byte. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 allocates callee-saved registers for locals in the source DECLARATION order (first declared -> $s0, next -> $s1, ...), and the function PARAMETER takes the last free slot: in DNM_fn_vDynamicsInitDefault the retail uses s0 = p_stReport (declared second), s1 = ulEndFlags (declared first) and s2 = the parameter, i.e. the reverse of the declaration order. Declaring 'DNM_stReport* p_stReport;' before 'unsigned int ulEndFlags;' reproduces the retail register assignment exactly. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail DNM_stDynamics layout: the base block is 0x170 bytes, the advanced block 0x88 bytes but the complex block starts at 0x200 (8 bytes of gap at 0x1f8) and stMACDPID is 0x7c bytes (8 bytes of gap at 0x6c). The retail's DNM_fn_vDynamicsInitDefault memsets 0x170 / 0x200 / 0x370 bytes, i.e. sizeof(base), sizeof(DNM_stDynamics)-sizeof(complex) and sizeof(DNM_stDynamics). include/r3/DNM.h's DNM_stDynamicsAdvancedBlock has no tail padding and its DNM_stReport is 0x16c bytes while the retail's g_stReport is 0x274, so the memset uses the literal 0x274. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 folds 'if (flags & MASK)' into a direct 'andi/beqz' and never uses the retail's normalize-then-test idiom, but a source-level ternary 'if ((flags & MASK) ? 1 : 0)' does. The same ternary also restores the retail's register allocation, because the extra live value keeps the comparison result in a callee-saved register instead of letting MWCC fold the test away. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail pads an EMPTY infinite loop ('for (;;) ;') to 7 instructions (6 nops + the back-branch), but no available MWCC PS2 build reproduces that: 3.0-011126 and 3.0.1-020123 emit a bare 'b .' (1 instruction), 3.0.3-020716 pads to 6 instructions, and 3.0b38-030307 pads to 9. In ERM/CpaError.c Erm_fn_v_UpdateLastError the two such loops are worth 24 bytes each in the retail, so that file needs a compiler build between 3.0.3 and 3.0b38 (3.0.3 already gives the exact 492-byte size, 3.0.1 gives 444). No source spelling changes the padding: while(1), do/while(1), for(;;){} and for(;;) break; all give the same one-instruction loop. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 evaluates a call's arguments right-to-left by default (CD_Read(fh, &local, 4) emits the second argument's address first), while the retail sometimes emits the first argument first (loadarea.c MNC_fn_pcLoadAreaWithAllocator at +0xd4: 'daddu a0,s1,zero' then 'addiu a1,sp,0x1ac'). The retail's own order is also visible for a constant second argument (CD_Seek(fh,4,0)) and reversed for a loaded second argument, so this is a per-call scheduling choice, not a fixed rule. No source spelling (argument casts, prototype types, comma expressions, temp pointer local) or scoped pragma (schedule/peephole/opt_*/optimization_level) changed it. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] The branch that ends an if/else arm is attributed to the closing brace's line: in loadarea.c MNC_fn_pcLoadAreaWithAllocator the retail's line map has entries at +0x410/+0x480/+0x4e0 (the join branches of the three 'u32Area = pstLinksTable[i].u32AreaId + 2 - fn_lGetTransitionBloc()' if/else arms), and adding braces around those arms (so the '}' sits on its own line) reproduced them exactly - the candidate went from lines=different to lines=ok with byte-identical code. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 always narrows a bitfield read-modify-write to the smallest containing unit (a 32-bit lw/sw of the whole struct becomes a 16-bit lh/sh) and computes the clear-mask in 32 bits. The retail instead updates the 11-bit field at bit 21 of a PS2GL tagGLtexblock with a 32-bit lw/sw and a 64-bit clear-mask 0xFFFFFFFF001FFFFF. Layout and sizeof agree in every build; only the lowering differs, and explicit C bit manipulation does not help because MWCC folds the mask into a shift pair. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 rewrites a signed (int)float conversion as trunc.w.s when the value range proves the result is non-negative; the retail keeps the signed cvt.w.s. A retail clamp that ends in an out-of-line join (a branch with the assignment in its delay slot) is also a different shape from MWCC's fall-through. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3 narrows a bitfield access to the smallest addressable unit that contains the field regardless of the declared container type: a probe with 'unsigned long f:1' and 'unsigned char f:1' members produces the identical lbu/sb (mask 0xfe) sequence under all three builds. So retyping the PS2GL tagGScontext registers PRMODE/TEST_1/TEST_2/ModFlgs from unsigned long containers to unsigned char/unsigned short containers (as the game's u_draw.c does) is a byte-identical code change, not a fix for a 32-bit read-modify-write: the whole 15-unit PS2GL pass produced 0 differing text bytes. The bit positions are also unchanged, because the corrected declarations keep the same field order and the same per-container packing (54 one-bit flags still occupy bits 0..53 = bytes 0..6). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 delay-slot fill difference (library build 3.0): for a loop whose exit branch targets a 'return NULL' block, our build copies the block's 'move v0,zero' into the beqz delay slot and retargets the branch to the block+4 (jr ra), while the retail leaves the slot as nop and branches to the block itself (PS2glSearchList, PS2GL_Lists.c, +0x38). The scoped pragmas schedule off / peephole off / opt_dead_assignments off / opt_dead_code off and the 3.0.1, 3.0.3 and 3.0b38 builds all keep our shape; 'return 0;' and 'return (tagListData**)0;' make no difference. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1-020123 and 3.0.3-020716 do not elide the copy of an INLINED function's class result into a local: 'iterator oIt = find(key);' materialises the returned iterator in a temporary and then copies it into oIt (one extra copy-constructor call, 12 bytes for a 4-byte class). The retail builds the caller's iterator directly in the return slot (SND_R3 CSoundSemaphoreCont::mfn_poFindSoundSemaphore +0x9c/+0xa0, CLinkTypeActorCont::mfn_poGetActorTypeFromId +0x98/+0x9c). Treating this as a source-shape problem wastes time: no spelling of the assignment avoids the temporary in our builds. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] MWCC debug type records can under-report an aggregate's stack size: VMS_lGetFileDate's local sits at sp+0x20 and the next local at sp+0x98, so it occupies 0x78 bytes while its type record says 0x40. Compare a local's debug offset with the NEXT local's offset instead of trusting the type size, and pad the declaration with the difference. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The retail pads an empty infinite loop (for (;;) ;) to 7 instructions. No available MWCC PS2 build reproduces it: 3.0 and 3.0.1 emit 1, 3.0.3 emits 6, 3.0b38 emits 9, and no loop spelling changes it. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 block layout: 'if (C) {A} else {B}' where A is a no-op assignment (xResult = xValue) keeps the else-block out of line and emits 'bc1t' to it, while 'if (C) {B}' (A folded away) emits 'bc1f' over B and drops 8 bytes. MEC_fn_xConvertScale matches 184/184 only with the inverted 'if (xValue >= 1.0f) { xResult = xValue; } else { clamp }' form; the plain 'if (xValue < 1.0f) { clamp }' form compiles 8 bytes shorter with the opposite branch polarity. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0.1-020123 at optimization_level 0 materialises an absolute 0x1000xxxx hardware address as lui+ori+lw (lui v1,0x1000; ori v1,v1,0x8000; lw v1,0(v1)), while the retail code uses the lui hi/lo pair (lui at,0x1001; lw v1,-0x8000(at)). MWCC 3.0.3-020716 emits the retail form at level 0, so a level-0 file that touches the PS2 hardware registers (vu0prg.c: 0x10008000/0x10008020/0x1000E010) needs the per-file 3.0.3 profile; at level 1 all three builds use the retail form. Evidence: VU0_fn_vWaitEndLoadVU0 is 148/144 bytes under 3.0.1 and 144/144 (EQUIVALENT) under 3.0.3, and vu0prg.c's two already-recorded matches (VU0_fn_vLoadPrgCollision/PlayAnim) also only match under 3.0.3. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The retail's STANDALONE and INLINED copies of the same function can disagree on the last-element addressing: fn_p_stGetPersoInList's standalone lowers p_hTabElt[ucNbElt-1] as (base-1)+ucNbElt*4 (addiu v0,v0,-4; addu v0,v0,v1; lw v0,0(v0)) while the copy inlined into fn_vSortListByModel folds the -4 into the load (addu v0,v0,v1; lw a1,-0x4(v0)). MWCC renders one source consistently, so a source that matches the standalone necessarily breaks the inlined caller and vice versa: the plain p_hTabElt[ucNbElt-1] keeps fn_vSortListByModel matching and leaves the standalone 4 bytes short, the int-address spelling matches the standalone byte-for-byte and breaks the caller. Treat 'standalone vs inlined disagree' as a compiler-build difference, not a source-shape error. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] To reproduce the retail's unfolded 'one before the base' addressing (addiu base,-4; addu base,base,index*4; lw 0(base)) the source needs the offset on the BASE, not in the index: *(T**)(((int)base - 4) + (iIndex = n * 4)) with a dead scaled-index assignment reproduces it byte-for-byte (List.c fn_p_stGetPersoInList, 120/120). The plain p[i-1], the (p-1)[i] form and the char* base form all fold the -4 into the load displacement instead. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Files that read the retail's sin-effect tables need the MWCC 3.0.3-020716 profile for two reasons: 3.0.3 folds the punned stack address (swc1 f3,0xc(sp) + lw v1,0xc(sp) instead of addiu a2,sp,0xc + lw v1,0(a2)) and it inlines the (unsigned int) float conversion (trunc.w.s + or 0x80000000 instead of a jal fptoui). GLI_vSinusEffetOn3DVertex is 272/268 under 3.0.1 and 268/268 under 3.0.3; fn_uiGetSomeBytes is 396/428 (unresolved fptoui relocation) under 3.0.1 and 424/428 under 3.0.3. Both files' other recorded matches still verify under 3.0.3, so they satisfy the per-file 3.0.3 criterion. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: an explicit redundant '& 0xFF' on an int expression is folded away, but a '(unsigned char)' cast on the same expression keeps the 'andi 0xff'. Retail 'andi a0,a0,0xff' after 'andi a0,reg,0xfe' is therefore a (unsigned char) cast of the masked value, not a literal mask (GLI_vUnloadTexturePartial, HdwTx_R3.c). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] SND_R3 C++ mangling: a member taking a usnd class by reference must match the retail's const-ness exactly - 'const usnd::sstring&' mangles to FRCQ24usnd7sstring but the retail's mfn_bRead__12CTextFileSrcFRQ24usnd7sstring uses a NON-const 'usnd::sstring&' (FR), while mfn_poAddCResData__9CBankDescFRCQ24usnd5CUUID really does take 'const usnd::CUUID&'. Declaring the usnd types in a 'namespace usnd { struct X; }' block with 'struct usnd::X { ... };' definitions makes the verifier see the symbol instead of reporting OBJECT_ERROR. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 (AI/AIGame/procMorf.c): the retail line table attributes a whole error block to one source line - fn_p_stPrepareMorph/fn_p_stMorphing/fn_p_stMorphingMT/fn_p_stPrepareMorphMT all call Erm_fn_v_UpdateLastError(...) followed by Erm_fn_ClearLastError(0) with a single DWARF line entry covering both calls, so both statements must sit on the same source line to reproduce the target line map. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: the union member spelling of an argument changes the emitted argument-evaluation order. fn_p_stPrepareMorph passes the morph type as stValue[3].uDummy.uParam.cValue (signed char) to the unsigned char parameter of MOR_fn_xPrepareMorph; with .ucValue the compiler loads that argument after the 5th argument, with .cValue it loads it first, which is what the retail does (lbu t3 then lw t0). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: indexing an array through a cast pointer, ((float *)axStart)[i] = 0.0f, makes the compiler materialize the array base in a register and add the scaled index (addiu v0,sp,base; addu v0,v0,index; store 0(v0)); plain axStart[i] uses the shorter sp+index plus a displacement (store disp(index+sp)). fn_p_stPrepareMorphMT matches the retail loop only with the cast form. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] The extractor's per-local stack_offset is shifted by one: MWCC's DWARF writes the 04 <offset> record after the variable's type descriptor and before its 07 38 name record, so tools/extract_debug.py attaches the previous variable's slot to each name. Reading the raw .debug stream (04 offset then 07 38 name) gives the true slot, e.g. fn_p_stMorphingMT hNewSuperObjPerso@0x164, stParam@0x150, ucArrayId2@0xc0, stValue@0x130, xChannelIndex@0xb0, axRatio@0x100, axEndIndexInObjectTable@0xe0, xStartIndexInObjectTable@0xa0. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 keeps a comparison as a branch (bne + 'li v0,1' delay slot) when the 'return 1' sits inside the enclosing else block; moving that 'return 1' after the if/else makes MWCC if-convert the comparison into xori/sltiu. Writing the early-exit tests in the inverted 'if (C) { return 1; } else { body }' form reproduces the retail's branch-to-body layout (bne to the out-of-line body, 'li v0,1' in the delay slot). Matched PLA_fn_bTestNewState 144/144. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3: a pointer that is dead after a post-increment is kept in a callee-saved register only if the incremented value is bound to its own local ('char* p = body + 1;' -> 'addiu s0,s0,1' then 'move a0,s0' at the use, as in retail strFileOpen), while 'body += 1' on the original variable folds the +1 into every use (strlen(body+1), body[i]+1) and needs an extra strength-reduced induction register (retail 804 bytes vs 808). The retail debug local list (mode, tail, len, i, dlen, devname, fn, body, ret) has no such extra pointer, so the original source incremented 'body' itself and the retail's compiler build kept the increment materialised - a compiler-build difference, not a source-shape one. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 lowers 'x = (c) ? 1 : 0' (and the same value tested in an if) with a conditional move (movz reg,zero,val), while retail code materialises the 0/1 value with the branchy expansion 'move v0,zero / li v0,1' in a virtual register and only then masks it ('andi v0,v0,0xff'). Reproduced on fn_vTreatCharactersInSector's misc-flag test: source forms 'x = c ? 1 : 0', 'x = c ? (unsigned char)1 : (unsigned char)0', 'unsigned char m; m = 0; if (c) m = 1; if (m & 0xff)' and the macro/inline-function spellings all emit movz or a different register; only the 'm = 0; if (c) m = 1' form keeps the branchy shape, and then in a different register than retail. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 places a memory store in a conditional branch's delay slot only when that store is unconditional in the source: in FMN_SCE_ConvertPathDos2Unix the retail's 'sb v1,0(s0)' sits in the beqz delay slot (so it always executes), which forced the source to write '*p_cConverted = '\\';' BEFORE the 'if (p_cConverted > s_szFileName)' guard; with the store inside the guard the constant materialization moved to a different block and the bytes differed at +0x90. (2026-10-20)

- [abi] MWCC PS2 o32: a 3-parameter prototype for a function the retail calls with one argument makes the compiler set up a0/a1 twice (once before the call and again in the delay slot). Declaring 'extern int sceCdMmode(int media);' (one parameter) reproduced CD_Init's retail bytes exactly: the retail's a1/a2 zeroing is the loop's counter initialisation, not call arguments. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 register allocation follows the LOCAL DECLARATION ORDER for callee-saved registers: in fn_vTransferSpeedInformationPLA_DNM the retail keeps the dynamics pointer in s0 and the speed vector in s1, which needs the locals declared in the reverse of the debug list order (h_Dynam, p_stDynamics, p_MthVector, p_MthRotationMatrix); the extracted list order produced s1/s0 swapped and a 1-instruction register mismatch. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: on a 'char' local, 'if (flag == 0)' materialises the 8-bit sign extension (dsll32/dsra32 by 24) before the test, while 'if (!flag)' branches on the raw register. WPARCLST_fn_vAdd (AI/AIBase/WParc.c) only matches with the '== 0' spelling (428/428 bytes, retail line map). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Rayman 3 LST2 list removal (AI/AIBase/WParc.c WPARCLST_fn_vAdd/vDestroy, GAM/Actions/Dynam.c): when the removed element is the anchor's hLastElementDyn the retail stores the element's PREDECESSOR (hPrevBrotherDyn) into the anchor, not the successor (hNextBrotherDyn) - the first-element case takes the successor. Reproduced byte-for-byte in WPARCLST_fn_vAdd. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] PS2GLAUX.c (PS2GL20 library) needs MWCC 3.0.1-020123 or 3.0.3-020716, not the 3.0 library baseline: PS2auxInitPosition's if/else-if chain only keeps the retail's 'next condition computed in the branch delay slot' shape (and the nop delay slots of the second level) under the later builds - the 3.0 build emits 480/496 bytes. All 8 pre-existing recorded matches in the file verify under 3.0.1 and 3.0.3, so the file qualifies for config/library-3.0.1-sources.txt (or library-3.0.3-sources.txt). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 emits the retail 'condition register' form of a short-circuit && chain when the value is materialised through an if/else assignment: 'if (a && b && c) bFlag = 1; else bFlag = 0; if (bFlag) ...' compiles to branches on the raw operands (beqz of the pointer/loaded word) plus a phi block (li v1,1 / b merge / move v1,zero at the false merge) and a final 'beqz v1'. A plain 'if (a && b && c) ...' instead branches straight to the body, and 'bFlag = a && b && c;' materialises each operand with sltu. Reproduced byte-for-byte in GLI_fnvPrecalculateStuffForAllMaterials (load.c), J_RemoveRedundantSprites (Camera.c) and GLI_xSetViewportCamera's sibling. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 allocates the inline-asm operands of an accumulator dot product in the order the operands first appear in the asm block, not in the parameter declaration order: with 'asm { mula.s a,b } asm { madda.s c,d } asm { madd.s e,f,g }' the six parameters land in f0,f1,f2,f3,f4,f5 as a,b,c,d,e,f. Writing the helper's parameters in the interleaved order (a,b,c,d,e,f) and calling it as dot(row.x, src.x, row.y, src.y, row.z, src.z) reproduces the retail register allocation (GLI_xSerialLinearOp, load.c). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] MWCC PS2 copies a 16-byte MTH4D vector with integer lw/sw only when the local has a word view: a union { MTH4D_tdstVector_ v; int i[4]; } local keeps the 16-byte stride, the destination stores stay sp-relative and the source loads stay matrix-relative (GLI_xSerialLinearOp, load.c); plain float member copies use lwc1/swc1 and punned '*(int*)&v.xX' copies materialise every member address in a callee-saved register. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Library build flags: PS2GL_Lists.c (src/Rayman3/dev/R3/Version13/ps2/PS2GL20/Src/PS2GL/PS2GL_Lists.c) has two functions that match ONLY with '-O3,p' added to the library profile - PS2glMergeBB (148/148, the retail's conditional-branch-target alignment nops at +0x24/+0x44) and PS2glSearchList (132/132) - while impPS2glPartialDeleteLists in the SAME file then regresses (the retail's loop exit at +0x44 is NOT padded, while -O3,p pads it to +0x48, 88 vs 84 bytes). The same holds for MWCC 3.0.1 and 3.0.3, so the file cannot simply be added to config/library-O3p-sources.txt; it needs a per-function decision (or an intermediate compiler build). Measured with the baseline flags plus '-O3,p' on the whole unit: PS2GL_Lists.c gains 2 and loses 1, PS2GL_DataInput.c loses 76 (as already documented), and PS2GL_Array.c, PS2GL_BufferControl.c, PS2GL_EnableDisable.c, PS2GL_Fragment.c, PS2GL_InitContext.c, PS2GL_PixelOperation.c, PS2GL_Texture.c and PS2GL_VU1_Pipeline.c neither gain nor lose. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 lays a jump-table switch's case blocks out in the order the case LABELS appear in the source, not in the enum order: with the labels written Zoom(3), RotationZoom(6), Scale(4) the blocks are emitted in that order, while the enum order (3,4,6) emits 3,4,6. The case bodies themselves must still match the retail (MAT_fn_vZoom, mat/matutils.c, needs the 1.0f W stores to use a GPR constant). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail MWCC does not widen a 'short' argument to an 'int' parameter at a call site (the value is already sign-extended in the 32-bit o32 register), but MWCC PS2 3.0.1 emits dsll32/dsra32 for the promotion. In GLI_xSetViewportCamera (Camera.c) the retail passes its two short parameters to GLD_bGetViewportAttributes without a conversion, so the source must either declare the callee's parameters as short or declare the function's own parameters as int (the DWARF records both functions with short parameters, and a single short prototype then breaks the sibling GLI_xGetViewportCamera, whose int parameters would be narrowed). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 narrows an 'int' comparison to 'slt' when it can prove the left operand is non-negative (a loop counter starting at 0), so 'i < (unsigned)count' loses the retail's 'sltu'. Declaring the loop counter 'unsigned int' keeps the unsigned compare and reproduces the retail bytes (GLI_fnvPrecalculateStuffForAllMaterials, load.c). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The retail's standalone fn_p_stGetPersoInList keeps the last-element access UNFOLDED (addiu base,-4 + index*4) while the copies inlined into fn_vSortListByModel fold it to scaled addressing. MWCC renders one source consistently, so the two cannot both match: the plain p_hTabElt[ucNbElt-1] spelling matches the 464-byte caller (the standalone is then 116/120), the int-address spelling matches the 120-byte standalone (the caller is then one register allocation plus an explicit sll off). Keep the larger function when forced to choose. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The retail's usnd::stree::iterator copy constructor is NOT trivial (it forwards _Ptr to iterator_base(sTreeLink*)), and MWCC inlines that into a call to __ct__iterator_base(sTreeLink*); but the retail also copies a by-value iterator parameter as RAW BYTES in some functions, which MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3 never do -- they always call the copy constructor. The two requirements are mutually exclusive in our builds, and the retail itself mixes them inside one translation unit, so there is no single canonical template text for all SND_R3 units. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Compiler-build evidence on AI/AIGame/Procedu1.cxx: the retail needs BOTH MWCC 3.0.1 and 3.0.3 behaviour in the same unit. fn_p_stReleaseAnalogJoystick (0x0022f360) matches byte-for-byte (176/176, 3/3 relocs, lines ok) ONLY under 3.0.3-020716, because the retail inlines the (unsigned char)float conversion (lui 0x4f00 / mtc1 / c.le.s 2^31 / bc1t / trunc.w.s) while 3.0.1-020123 emits a jal fptoui. But AI_vProcessSetPersoWithPerso (0x00236dc0, already recorded MATCH) regresses under 3.0.3: the retail keeps the 4-byte alignment nop before the bnez at +0xe0 that 3.0.1 emits and 3.0.3 omits. Moving the whole file to config/compiler-3.0.3-sources.txt therefore gains one function and loses one, so the retail unit was built with a compiler between 3.0.1-020123 and 3.0.3-020716 (the same 'retail sits between the two builds' pattern already seen in AGO2_Mem.c and padloop behaviour). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3, 3.0b38) evaluates float call arguments RIGHT-TO-LEFT only when two arguments share the same literal constant (it then CSEs the constant into one arg register and copies it to the other); with two different literal constants it evaluates the EARLIER argument's operand group first. Verified with a controlled probe on GLI_vDrawWorldSpriteWithUVsOrientedOpt's 11-argument shape: '0.25f + s->a, -0.25f + s->b' materialises 0.25f first into the U2 register and V1 first into the V1 register, while '1.0f + s->a, 1.0f + s->b' materialises 1.0f into the U2 register first. The retail AGO3_GrowingUVAnimatedFireBall::Display evaluates the V2 argument group (-0.25f constant, V1 load) before the U2 group (0.25f constant, U1 load), which no spelling we tried (operand order, macro form, local-variable form, 1.0f/4.0f form, negative-literal forms, all four compiler builds) reproduces. AGO3_Smoke::Display shows the same residual: retail puts the 0.0f constant in $f14 (U1) and the 1.0f in $f16 (U2) while our build puts 1.0f in $f15 (V1) and 0.0f in $f14. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] Procedur.c holds mixed Latin-1/UTF-8 text: editing it with a UTF-8 decoding replaces the Latin-1 bytes (0xE9 'e-acute', 0xFA 'u-acute', 0xDF 'ss') with U+FFFD (EF BF BD). The corrupted strings shift the .rodata layout, so every anonymous @N literal relocation in the translation unit stops resolving (fn_p_stGetNbAvailableResolution and PS2_fn_vSetControllerMessage both regressed to 'unresolved relocations' until the original bytes were restored). Restore the original bytes after any edit of that file. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 assigns the FPRs of a float expression in DESCENDING order of first use (the first live range gets the highest register); the retail ELF uses ASCENDING order. Writing the three component subtractions in reverse source order (z, then y, then x) makes MWCC allocate ascending (x lowest, z highest) and reproduces the retail register assignment byte-for-byte without changing the emitted instruction order (Inters.c INT_fn_bIntersectSphereWithSphere, 84/84 MATCH with the MTH_SQR3_TO mula.s/madda.s/madd.s accumulator macro). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail 'addiu base,base,0' / 'li reg,imm' constant materialisation and the R5900 accumulator ops are not the only divergence between the 3.0.1 and 3.0.3 game builds: mc_menu.c (SVG, level-0 file) needs 3.0.3-020716 for MC_HasEnoughtSpace and MC_AskForInsertion (both keep a dead store that 3.0.1 folds away; under 3.0.1 it needs '#pragma peephole off', which then duplicates the level-0 constant argument moves), but the six MC_WaitFor*ButtonRelease matches need 3.0.1's loop-head padding (3.0.3 leaves the back-branch four bytes early). The file therefore cannot switch to the 3.0.3 list without losing six recorded matches. (2026-10-20)

- [tooling] Editing a Latin-1 source with a UTF-8 text editor silently replaces every non-ASCII byte with U+FFFD (0xef 0xbf 0xbd): in Functio1.cxx the four 'e-acute' bytes of the 'non implémente' / 'pas implémente' printf strings were destroyed this way and the two recorded matches fn_p_stGetCurrentResolution / fn_p_stGetNbAvailableResolution regressed (relocs 1/3 instead of 3/3). Repair byte-wise ('\xef\xbf\xbd' -> '\xe9') and compare the non-ASCII byte count with 'git show HEAD:<file>' after every edit of that unit. (2026-10-20)

- [abi] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 narrows a returned boolean differently depending on the declared return type: a 'unsigned char' function that returns a comparison emits a trailing 'andi reg,reg,0xff' (4 extra bytes), while the same source declared 'int' returns the raw 0/1. The retail INT_fn_bGetInclusionPointInBox ends with 'xori v0,v0,1' and no mask, so its return type must be int even though the debug record suggests unsigned char (COL/Inters.c, 188/188 MATCH after changing the return type). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1: a comparison's operand registers depend on the source spelling. 'A >= B' loads A into a1 and B into v0 and emits 'sltu v0,a1,v0; bnez v0'; the equivalent 'B <= A' loads B into a1 and A into v0 and emits the same compare with the operands swapped - which is what the retail code has (GLI_vDeleteAllWaitingPointers, u_SDL.cpp). Flip the comparison spelling to match the retail operand order. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The PS2GL globals PS2glLiberator and PS2glAllocator64 are function pointers, not ints: the extractor types them as int, so the retail's 'lw v0,PS2glLiberator(gp); jalr v0' indirect call needs 'void (*PS2glLiberator)(void*)' and 'void* (*PS2glAllocator64)(unsigned int)' (see PS2GL_EnableDisable.c). With the int type the call becomes a direct jal to the global's address instead. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] A local 'union { MTH3D_tdstVector_ v; int i[3]; }' reproduces the retail MTH-vector copy shape: the compiler loads the source components as words (lw), stores them into the union's int view (sw) and reads them back as floats (lwc1) before the float stores (swc1). Confirmed byte-exact in Micros.c fn_s32ObjectTypeAnimationX/Y/Z and fn_bObjectTypeAnimationPosition/Velocity (the plain float-field copy would emit lwc1/swc1 instead). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] A pointer local to a local aggregate ('_stVector* pst_Answer = &st_Answer;' plus 'if (pst_Answer != NULL)') reproduces the retail guard 'addiu v0,sp,off; beqz v0,exit' and stops MWCC from deleting the dead stores to the aggregate's other members. Confirmed in Micros.c fn_s32ObjectTypeAnimationX/Y/Z (retail keeps the unused y/z stores). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC 3.0.1 register allocation follows the LOCAL DECLARATION ORDER for callee-saved registers: declaring 'hVisualSet, xNbLodDefinitions, i, iLodOffset' instead of 'i, xNbLodDefinitions, hVisualSet, iLodOffset' moved the locals from s0/s2 to s0..s3 and turned GLI_PS2GL_CleanSpecifForPO into a byte-exact 236/236 match. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Early 'return;' statements and nested ifs are not equivalent: with nested ifs the compiler branches to a join nop before the epilogue, while early returns branch straight to the epilogue. GLI_PS2GL_CleanSpecifForPO (u_drawHier.c) only matched after replacing the nested-if chain with early returns for the PO/hVisualSet/xType/LodDefinitions checks. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] The verifier compares only the CODE OFFSETS of the debug line changes, so a retail function whose body sits on one source line is reproduced by putting the whole body on one line in the candidate (Micros.c fn_bObjectTypeAnimationRollOff: the body, the b_Answer=0 initialisation and the return all on one line matched the retail's three line entries 448/449/450). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 C++ delete/destructor idiom (verified byte-exact on sndeevid.cpp SND_VID_fn_vDesinitVideoSound, 124/124): 'delete p' on a polymorphic class emits 'lw a0,p; lw t9,0(a0); lw t9,0xC(t9); jalr t9' with a1=1 in the delay slot (the deleting-destructor flag) and a null check on p (beqz p) - the destructor slot is the vtable index of the virtual destructor (vptr[2] is the first declared virtual, the 2-word null prefix means the first virtual is at vptr+8). An explicit 'p->~T()' emits the same call but with the flag -1 (complete-object destructor, no free), which is how retail code destroys an object allocated with a custom 'operator new' and then frees it with an explicit usnd_free call. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail Rayman 3 vtable layouts can be read straight from the ELF's __vt__ symbols: the vptr points at the start of the vtable, vptr[0]/vptr[1] are a null prefix, and vptr[2] is the first declared virtual (so the Nth declared virtual is at vptr+8+4*(N-1)). Reading __vt__Q24usnd12CPS2Renderer (0x369910) and __vt__Q24usnd20CPS2StreamVBRenderer (0x3699B0) showed that the base class has 18 virtuals (2nd = the virtual destructor, 7th = fv_vStop, 8th = fv_vPause, 9th = fv_vResume, 11th = the volume setter) and that CPS2IOPPullDMA's destructor sits at vtable offset 0x10 - which is what let the delete/destructor calls in sndeevid.cpp be reconstructed with the right slots. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 lower a counted for/while loop into 'guard + bottom test' (beqz before the body, test at the bottom), while the retail Rayman 3 code uses the jump-to-test rotation (b to the test, the loop init in the branch delay slot, the back-branch at the end). Spelling the rotation out in C with goto reproduces the retail shape exactly: 'i = 0; goto test; body: ...; i++; test: if (i < N) goto body;' - confirmed byte-for-byte on the g_a4_ucSectorDisplayPriority loop in GAM/Engine.c fn_vOneTrame, including the addiu of the array base in the back-branch's delay slot. It also fixes the loop size (the candidate grew from 532 to the retail's 548 bytes). The same goto spelling is needed for the second (viewport) loop in that function. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1/3.0.3 always sink the store of a call's result to immediately after the call: 'p = f(); g = 0; h = 0;' compiles to 'call; sw v0,p; sw zero,g; sw zero,h' and the retail code keeps the result store in the middle of the independent zero stores ('call; sw zero,g; sw zero,h; sw v0,p'). The candidate source order does not change this (direct assignment, a local plus a later assignment, the call as the last statement, and separate vs combined source lines were all tried) and no available compiler build (3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.3, 3.0b38) reproduces the retail order - a compiler-build scheduling difference, seen in sndeevid.cpp SND_VID_fn_vInitVideoSound (+0x28) and (the same pattern) elsewhere. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail game/library code sometimes materialises one string literal address for two calls: a single %hi in a branch delay slot plus a single %lo in the later call's delay slot, so the first call receives a truncated pointer (fn_vFreeInBinaryPreservingTheCurrentMode, fn_pvMallocInBinaryPreservingTheCurrentMode in HdwTx_R3.c, retail string 'Bad Allocation Mode\n'). MWCC 3.0.1 and 3.0.3 emit two full lui/addiu pairs instead (156 vs 152 bytes) and the verifier cannot pair two HI16 relocations for one anonymous literal, so the anonymous-literal address check fails as well. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 keeps a 'short' loop counter sign-extended in a 64-bit register: the increment is 'addiu rd,rs,1' + 'dsll32 rd,rd,0x10' + 'dsra32 rd,rd,0x10' (a 16-bit sign extension) and the test is 'dsll32/dsra32' + 'slti'. Declaring the counter 'short' reproduces it (MOR_fn_xGetMorphTaskIndex, fn_vLastDeinitMorphTaskList); an 'int' or 'long' counter drops the extension and changes the loop shape (the int form also loses the retail's 'jump to test' loop entry). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 lowers a conditional expression 'c ? X : Y' with the TRUE arm in the movz source register: 'li rd,Y; li rs,X; movz rd,rs,c' (MIPS movz copies rs when the test register is 0). So the source '(x != NULL) ? 8 : 1' yields 'li v1,8; li v0,1; movz v1,v0,x' - the arm order in the source is the opposite of what the instruction order suggests. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail CAM/MOR code keeps the body of an 'if (ptr == NULL) { return; } else { <body> }' OUT OF LINE: 'bnez cond,<body>' plus a 'b <epilogue>' in the fallthrough, while MWCC 3.0.1 always inlines the body and emits 'beqz cond,<end>'. Reproduced/observed in CAM_fn_vConstraintMove and MOR_fn_bRemoveMorphedObjectFromHierarchy; the block layout difference survives every spelling of the condition (== NULL, != NULL, empty then-block). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 canonicalises 'pointer + unsigned offset' into 'addu rd, offset_reg, ptr_reg' (integer operand first) and keeps the pointer as the accumulator, while retail emits 'addu rd, ptr_reg, offset_reg'. The source spelling does not change it: subscript form ((uchar*)&x)[off], pointer+offset (*((uchar*)&x + off)), reversed operands, char*/int base casts and an unsigned/int offset local all produce the same integer-first addu. Blocked one-word residual: fn_vTakeControlOfChannel (retail 'addu v1,v1,a0' vs candidate 'addu v1,a0,v1'). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Retail line maps often attribute dozens of instructions to ONE source line: PLA_bCheckDistForMorph's target rows jump from +0x38 straight to +0xd0 (about 35 instructions on one line), and fn_vTakeControlOfLipChannel interleaves rows 212/213/212/213 for two adjacent statements. To match such a line map the candidate must put the corresponding statements on a single physical line, not one statement per line. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] The engine's float-to-int helper 'inline long MTH_M_xRealToLong(float x) { long r; asm { cvt.w.s f30, x / mfc1 r, f30 } return r; }' fixes TWO things at once in a candidate: the conversion (cvt.w.s $f30 vs MWCC's own trunc.w.s $f0) and the STACK FRAME SIZE (f30 is callee-saved, so the frame grows by 0x10 and the prologue gains swc1/lwc1 $f30). A candidate that is exactly 0x10 bytes short in frame size and converts through $f0 is missing this macro (verified in playevts.c fn_vDesInitAllEvents: 0x80 -> 0x90 frame and the exact conversion sequence). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail usnd::stree::_Erase (SND_R3, stree<i, spair<const i, CSoundSemaphoreInfo*>, smap<i, CSoundSemaphoreInfo*>, less<i>, sallocator<CSoundSemaphoreInfo*> >) shows MWCC's call-delay-slot fill differs from retail in a way no source spelling fixes: retail keeps 'daddu a0,reg' as its own instruction before 'jal usnd_free' and hoists the NEXT statement's load 'lw reg,0(reg)' into the jal delay slot (the load runs before the free, which is also what the source means), while MWCC 3.0, 3.0.1-020123 and 3.0.3-020716 all fold the argument move into the delay slot and leave the load after the call, because a load through the freed pointer cannot be hoisted across an opaque call. Spelling the advance as a temp (poNext = poRight->m_poLeft) reaches the retail's 160-byte size but needs a fourth saved register. The candidate's line map can still be made exactly right: the retail puts the whole inner do/while on ONE source line and the 'usnd_free(node); node = node->m_poLeft;' pair on the next. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail usnd::slist::const_iterator copies (SND_R3 sndclinkcallbackresobj.cpp mfn_bSynchroCallbackInstance) use an lwc1/swc1 pair to move the 4-byte _Node* into the copy-constructor temporary - a 4-byte copy through the FPU - whereas MWCC 3.0/3.0.1/3.0.3 lower a 4-byte class or struct copy with lw/sw (probed: struct{int}, class with a pointer member, and a derived-to-base slice copy all use lw/sw; only float-typed data uses lwc1/swc1). The consequence is that the retail needs ONE copy-constructor call where ours needs two, so the candidate's frame is 0x40 vs the retail's 0x50 and every stack slot is shifted. This is a compiler-build difference in how a base subobject copy is lowered, not a source shape: making erase() take a const_iterator (212/244) or adding an explicit const_iterator temp (220/244) gets the structure closer but loses relocations. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] SND_R3 template members are attributed to the WRONG translation unit by the extractor: the retail ELF groups each file's weak template instantiations together with that file's own functions, so the LAST symbol of one object is attributed to the next file's debug record. Confirmed: _Init__usnd::stree<i, spair<const i, CSoundSemaphore*>, smap<i, CSoundSemaphore*>, ...> (0x001caab0) belongs to the sndcsoundsemaphorecont.cpp object (CSoundSemaphoreCont), _Insert__usnd::stree<i, spair<const i, CLinkMicro*>, smap<i, CLinkMicro*>, ...> (0x001d0210) to the sndclinkmicrocont.cpp object (CLinkMicroCont), and __dt__13CBankDescContFv (0x0018d120) to the sndeecbankdesccont.cpp object. Check the retail address order (the template symbols are interleaved with the owning file's game functions) before trying to match such a symbol in the unit the index names. (2026-10-20)

- [debug-format] A UTF-8 decode/encode round trip over a Latin-1 source silently corrupts it, and the damage has SEVERAL forms: a byte above 0x7F becomes the 3-byte U+FFFD (0xEF 0xBF 0xBD); sometimes only 0xEF 0xBF survives with the original byte as the third (0xEF 0xBF 0xF3); and sometimes characters are dropped as well. The damage shifts .rodata, so every anonymous @N literal relocation in the translation unit goes unresolved and the recorded match regresses. Repair by locating the surviving 0xEF 0xBF / 0xEF 0xBF 0xBD sequences and restoring the bytes from 'git show HEAD:<file>' using the surrounding ASCII as the anchor; a whole garbled string literal may need HEAD's version spliced back in. Affects Procedur.c (German/French/Spanish menu-pause strings) and Functio1.cxx. A file whose only non-ASCII bytes are the en-dash in the extractor's 'lines 76-93' comment is harmless: the cleanup pass removes that comment. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] PS2GL EndDraw (VUSprites.cpp): a switch whose retail code uses the jump table 'addi v1,v1,-0x200' must be written with the ABSOLUTE case labels (case 0x200 ... case 0x207) - writing 'switch (eDepthFunc - 0x200)' with case 0..7 makes MWCC emit 'addiu' for the dispatch index instead. Eight case labels are needed for MWCC to build the table at all. The retail's table has four distinct blocks (0->zTst 0, 1->zTst 3, 3->zTst 2, 7->empty) and MWCC lays them out in the same order but merges the last two (8 bytes short). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] PS2GL tagGScontext (VUSprites.cpp): the byte at +0x1b4 is a bitfield container - the retail updates bit 5 with 'lbu / and ~0x20 / or 0x20 (register) / sb', and MWCC only emits that register-materialised mask/value form when the field is a real bitfield in an 'unsigned char' container (an explicit '& 0xFFFFFFDF | 0x20' on a plain byte gives 'andi/ori' with immediates instead). Declaring the extractor's +0x1b4 padding as 'struct { unsigned char bUnused0 : 5; unsigned char bModified : 1; unsigned char bUnused1[3]; }' (same size/alignment) reproduces it. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail MWCC lowers 'A && !B' (and '!A && B') where A and B are equality comparisons of a masked value: the comparison is materialised as a value ('and / xor / sltiu rd,rd,1') and then BRANCHED on, with the 0/1 result materialised in the branch blocks ('bnez v0,L' + 'daddiu t2,zero,1' / 'daddu t2,zero,zero'). MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3 instead materialises the negation ('xori v0,v0,1' for '!(x==y)', 'sltu zero,v0' for 'x!=y') and reuses the value, and an if/else spelling of the same condition uses the direct beq/bne form for the first comparison. This blocks the whole IPT_fn_bIsKeyWordValid_Joystick*/Pad* family (retail game code) independently of the source shape. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Retail MWCC keeps a masked 16-bit load in its original width when the mask is applied: 'lh v1,0(a0)' + 'andi v1,v1,0xff' for a table index. MWCC 3.0.1/3.0.3 narrows the load to 'lbu' (the peephole folds lh+andi 0xff into a byte load) for every spelling tried (casts, locals, char*/short* pointers, volatile). It is a compiler-build difference, not a source-shape problem: the retail's own other routine (IPT_fn_bIsEntryActionValid) does fold to 'lbu'. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.3 assigns the callee-saved registers to a function's locals in DECLARATION ORDER, giving the first-declared local the HIGHEST saved register (s4) and the last-declared the lowest (s0) - the opposite of the extractor's debug local list order. AGO2_vDestroyAllParts matched 196/196 (bytes, relocations and line map) only after declaring the locals in the reverse of the extracted order (Source, NumToSkip, psrc, pdst, Limit). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] AGO2.c particle-display helpers (AGO2_DisplayForceField 224/224 MATCH, AGO2_DisplayBalloon): the retail's loop test is attributed to the SAME source line as the last statement of the body, so the while's closing brace has to be written on the statement's line ('i++; }') - a brace on its own line adds a line-table entry the retail does not have. The retail's shape is: 'if (!pParticleArrayOrg) { printf("AGO2MEM DECONNE UN MAX !!!\n"); p->ulType |= 1; } else { i = 0; pParticleArray = pParticleArrayOrg; while (i < p->GroupNumber) { ... } }' with the two increments on separate lines. (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] [source-layout] AGO2.c particle helpers share one retail shape (matched: AGO2_DisplayBalloon 216/216, AGO2_DisplayForceField 224/224, AGO2_FadeFire 244/244, AGO2_ProcessFire 280/280, AGO2_vDestroyAllParts 196/196): 'if (!p->hGroup) { p->GroupNumber = 0; p->ulType |= 1; return; }' then 'p = (T*)AGO2_MEM_GetPointerFromHandle(p->hGroup);' then 'if (!p) { printf("AGO2MEM DECONNE UN MAX !!!\n"); return; }'. The display variants then loop over the particles with 'if (p->fTransparency > 0.0f) { GLI_vSetGlobalAlpha(...); GLI_vDrawWorldQuadWithUVsOpt(g_AGO2_p_stViewport, &p->Points[0].xX, ..., p->VertexUVs[0][0], ..., (GLI_tdstMaterial_*)p->pMaterial1); p->HasBeenDisplayed = 1; }' and the fade variants with 'p->xTransparency -= TimeDiff; if (p->xTransparency < 0.0f) { p->xTransparency = 0.0f; p->Flags |= 1; } else { OneIsAlive = 1; }'. The locals must be declared in the reverse of the extractor's debug list (param0, i, OneIsAlive, TimeDiffTrsp). (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] MWCC PS2 3.0.1 gives a NAMED local the lower argument register and an expression temp the higher one: writing '&m_pHashTable[idx]' inline (no pointer local) puts the hash-slot address in $t0, while a named 'DMM_tdstFreeBloc_** ppHashSlot' local puts it in $a0 and the candidate is then only EQUIVALENT. Matched DMM.CPP remove_from_hashtable (96/96) by dropping the named pointer local. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] A pointer-range test written as 'if (cond) bInRange = 1; else bInRange = 0;' keeps the boolean in a register (the retail 'sltu/bnez' + 'move reg,zero' + 'li reg,1' shape); a plain 'if (cond && cond2) {...}' folds into direct branches instead. Reproduced the two range checks of DMM.CPP PS2GL_free exactly. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] An early 'if (p == NULL) { return; }' as the first statement of a function makes MWCC emit the NULL test before the 'sd ra' prologue (the 'sd ra' then lands in the branch's delay slot, as in DMM.CPP PS2GL_free); with a local initialised before the test the 'sd ra' stays ahead of the branch and the two orders differ by one instruction. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] A two-arm if/else whose condition is a negated && chain ('if (!(A && B && C)) {error} else {success}') makes MWCC branch the failed tests to the error block and lay the error block out before the success block, matching the retail; the positive form ('if (A && B && C) {success} else {error}') produces the same branches but lays the success block first. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Library '-O3,p' also turns on the R5900 divide fix: under it every div.s gains a following nop, which is why PS2GL files with float divides (rec* colour/vertex converters, 66 in ListsRecPly.c) lose matches when moved to -O3,p. Adding '#pragma dividefix off' with -O3,p kept all 505 ListsRecPly.c matches and gained the two alignment-nop functions (plyPS2glBegin, PS2glExecuteListVBuf), completing the file. Other library files rejected for -O3,p may gain the same way. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Follow-up to the -O3,p divide-fix finding: tools/match.py now builds config/library-O3p-sources.txt files with '-pragma dividefix off -O3,p' (no recorded match lost in the 7 listed files). Sweeping the other 15 library files under that profile: 13 neutral (PS2GL_DataInput.c, which lost 76 under plain -O3,p, now loses none), PS2GL_Lists.c +2/-1 (PS2glMergeBB, PS2glSearchList gain), so the earlier 'library loses under -O3,p' result was mostly the divide fix. (2026-10-20)

- [compiler] Follow-up to the (index-1) base-offset entries for fn_p_stGetPersoInList: the retail standalone shape IS reproducible. Int-cast base plus an int temporary assigned inside the scaled term, '*(T**)((int)p->p_hTabElt - 4 + (lOffset = p->ucNbElt * 4))', keeps 'addiu base,-4; addu; lw 0' AND the pointer-form register allocation (count in v1, position in a2); without the assignment the int base swaps them. Found by the permuter. The folded inlined copy in fn_vSortListByModel still needs pointer-form source, so List.c gives that caller a separate inline duplicate (marked Not original source). (2026-10-20)

- [source-layout] Game -O3,p: early 'if (p == NULL) return;' guards branch straight to the epilogue, while 'if (p != NULL) { ... }' bodies branch to a join label that -O3,p aligns, so retail shows a padding nop at 4 mod 8 just before the epilogue (the guards' beqz targets that nop). When the retail has this nop and the candidate is 4 bytes short, wrap the body in nested ifs instead of early returns. Fixed size in ReinitDesignerVariables, fn_vReinitDesignerVariablesWithFlags and AI_fn_vInsertDsgVarInSAI (AI_Proto.c). (2026-10-20)
